# NEXT_GEN.md — 4genthub next generation (new features after the Go port)

Split out of `MIGRATION.md` on 2026-10-02 (Request 10). `MIGRATION.md` tracks only the Python → Go port and can be closed when its `Final` box is checked. Everything new lives here; add new owner requests to this file.

## Direction (owner decision 2026-10-02, option 1)

**OpenRig is the client and runtime. 4genthub is the cloud data for orchestration.**

- 4genthub stores agents, rigs (topology), tasks, contexts and the state the client reports.
- OpenRig (installed `rig` CLI, herdr for terminals) launches and supervises seats. This repo contains no tmux, daemon or restore code.
- Pull model: the client initiates every exchange; the server never reaches into a user's machine. Remote commands (F5) are the only exception and are opt-in.
- OpenRig `agent_ref` accepts only `local:` and `path:` (`openrig/packages/daemon/src/domain/rigspec-schema.ts:545`, `agent-resolver.ts:203`). So the cloud renders AgentSpec/RigSpec files and the client writes them to disk.
- Supersedes the earlier "full OpenRig runtime in Go (`rigd`)" plan: the earlier plan's Go-runtime items (`rigd`, tmux adapter, restore, `cmd/rig`, WSL2 spike) are dropped. The F items below are their client-side replacements.

## Orchestration layer (owner vision, 2026-10-08)

4genthub is positioned as an AI software orchestration layer: a goal becomes tasks that brain and worker seats execute, a gate verifies against evidence, and the next seat can resume. **KPI: verified software per unit of human attention.** The architecture is `ai_docs/core-architecture/agenthub-system-architecture.md`; the decision and its options are `ai_docs/core-architecture/agenthub-system-architecture.md D1`; the work is group O in the checklist.

| Part | State on 2026-10-08 (measured by the architect at `eaa41d18`) |
|---|---|
| Seats, brains and workers as occupants, rendering, overlays | BUILT (`fastmcp/seat_management`, groups F and G) |
| MCP task/subtask/context/project/branch/seat tools | BUILT (`ddd_compliant_mcp_tools.go`); `manage_rule` deliberately not a tool |
| Four-tier context with inheritance | BUILT; context packs PORTED, NOT WIRED (F2) |
| Execution ledger and observable per-task state machine | NEW — O1, O8 |
| Seat identity on MCP calls | NEW — O2 |
| Git and test evidence | NEW — O3 |
| Acceptance criteria and resume/continue | NEW — O4 |
| Jev validation gate (accept / reject / uncertain) | NEW — O5, O6 (was G7, not built) |
| Routing to seats and wake-on-open-work | NEW — O7 (assignees are still legacy role names) |
| Project memory from verified facts | PARTIAL — contexts and `memory` modules exist; O6 writes the verified line |

## How the project and its seats work together (recorded 2026-10-05, owner request)

The concrete mechanism behind the direction above, written from what was operated and read, not from an OpenRig source read. Anything not verified first-hand is flagged.

**Physical layers, in order:**

```
owner / principal session          <- what the owner talks to (this repo's Claude session)
  |  rig send | rig up | rig terminal
  v
OpenRig daemon   pid in ~/.openrig/daemon.json, 127.0.0.1:7433, db ~/.openrig/openrig.sqlite
  |  creates and supervises
  v
tmux sessions named  <rig>-<seat>@<rig>
  |  run
  v
runtime processes  omp (DeepSeek) | claude-code | codex | agy
```

The daemon's database holds rig topology (`rigs`, `pods`, `nodes`), `snapshots`, `sessions`, the work ledger (`queue_items`, `queue_transitions`) and `usage_samples`. That is why a seat can die and resume with its queue intact, and why "down" is a lifecycle state rather than data loss: `rig up <rig> --existing` restores from a snapshot and each seat resumes its own session file.

**What a seat is — two halves.** The *position* (durable) is the key in the rig spec plus its edges and permission policy, e.g. `4genthub-min.go-dev2`. The *occupant* is runtime + model, e.g. `omp` + `deepseek/deepseek-flash`. The seat's behaviour comes from `agents/<seat>/agent.yaml` and `agents/<seat>/guidance/role.md`, which OpenRig delivers as startup text; the role file is the unit of work, which is why an unguided seat flails and a Claude-flavoured role on `omp` misroutes (it looks for MCP tools that runtime does not have).

**How work moves.** owner -> lead seat -> dev seats -> reviewer -> lead -> owner approves the push. The `queue_items` ledger carries owner and state (`pending`, `claimed`, `handed-off`, `blocked`, `done`). Seats commit locally by explicit pathspec, without staging first, and never push; the reviewer is the gate, so "done" means a second seat verified it.

**Where the two halves talk.** Only through client-side scripts holding `AGENTHUB_TOKEN` (`4genteam sync`, `4genteam bridge` and `4genteam team`, implemented in `agenthub_client/src/agenthub_client/` as `seat_sync.py`, `bridge.py` and `team_setup.py`; the `scripts/openrig_*.py` paths this line named until 2026-10-09 no longer exist): they pull seat and room definitions from the cloud, render rig spec plus seat files to disk (the daemon then launches them), and push back what the client observed (seat status, sessions, usage). Verified consequence: the seats read `AGENTHUB_TOKEN` from their inherited environment at call time, so rotating the token requires updating the config, the daemon, the tmux server environment and then reseating - a config-only rotation leaves the seats on the old credential (2026-10-05).

**Not verified by us:** OpenRig's internal reconciliation logic and how `resolved_spec_hash` is computed; we have only its CLI behaviour and its database.

## Gate and rules

Items that need the Go server wait for the matching `MIGRATION.md` slice. Owner-directed exception: F0 was built first on the existing Go server (Request 9).

Rules: group G was built before F1 to F6 (owner-directed); there is no group ordering. Tick a box only when its check is met. Never commit, push, deploy, or touch the production server without the owner's go-ahead. Never print secrets (`.mcp.json` bearer token, `*_SECRET*`, `*PASSWORD*`).

Standing owner permissions (owner-stated 2026-10-03): a push to main deploys production and is allowed after the owner approves (the team still asks before every push); rebuilds on production are allowed; `ssh 4genthub` is allowed for production configuration.

Deploy loop: bump `healthVersion` in `agenthub_go/fastmcp/server/httpapp/http.go` (and its test), push, then poll `/health` until it reports the new version. The GitHub pipeline test job is deliberately not fixed. [**SUPERSEDED 2026-10-06 (owner direction): `agenthub_main` is ARCHIVED and being DE-LINKED from the project — "archived, no use anymore" — and the owner's session is doing the CI half; the measurement that justifies it and the open question about the script tests are in the owner-directives section under DIRECTIVES OF 2026-10-06.**]

**The deploy path is a MAIN PUSH PLUS A FRONTEND MERGE PUSH, with no manual CapRover step** (recorded 2026-10-05, owner request — the owner had to find it in status prose rather than here). Exactly: `git push origin <sha>:main`, then merge that same commit into the `frontend` branch and `git push origin frontend`. Confirm live with `GET /health` (the deployed `healthVersion`) and the frontend at its own origin. Verified first-hand at closeout: `origin/main` = `89feea4a`, `origin/frontend` = `8f6a871b`, `/health` 0.0.18 healthy.

**Review-coverage reconciliation — CLOSED ZERO (recorded 2026-10-05 from the lead's closeout, owner request).** The seven-uncertified list is reconciled to **zero**: 22 code commits certified by the reviewer's note, 4 recovered from the reviewer's transcript, and 3 settled by a per-commit sweep — so every commit above the deployed base `c08063d5` now carries a reviewer verdict, and the batch deployed as `origin/main 89feea4a` / `origin/frontend 8f6a871b` (health 0.0.18). **Method lesson:** the **note table** says only *whose row carried a hash*; the **messages** say *what was concluded*; only the **actor's own transcript** settles *what was actually the verdict*. Certifying coverage from a note table or a message alone is the same class of error as a count without its rule — go to the transcript.

**WHAT "GATED" MEANS, AND WHAT IT DOES NOT COVER (recorded 2026-10-06, after a lead sentence was corrected by measurement — the third such correction that night).** **EVERY BEHAVIOURAL COMMIT IS GATED; THE DOCS COMMITS ARE THE LEAD'S REVIEW, NOT A GATE FILE.** **AND THAT IS A DIVISION OF LABOUR RATHER THAN A GAP: docs passes have no reviewer gate BY DESIGN — the lead reviews them and the reviewer's lane is behaviour — so a sentence that says "every commit is gated" tells a future reader that the DOCUMENTS were independently verified, which is the one thing it must not say** (rule 59's question: *what would a reader conclude about who verified this*). **AND THE CHECK IS A COUNT RATHER THAN AN ADJECTIVE:** on 2026-10-06, **EIGHT of the 26 commits above `origin/main` carried a gate by name** (`12771e93`, `6813ca63`, `1a159ddf`, `2f7b1b8b`, `9b0e55ac`, `a7cf5807`, `5f0fc8b7`, `0383ef8a`), **the rest being docs commits, the marker bump and one comment fix.** **The count moves, so the check is RE-RUN rather than quoted** — and the split it describes, unlike the number, is stable enough to be read.

seat from picking the cheapest option and calling it closed: THE SYSTEM CURRENTLY ACCEPTS FIVE OPERATIONS IT CANNOT PERFORM** — the path is **advertised as valid and dispatched**, and the handler's ZERO-FIELD STRUCT and the service takes only a task facade, so THE FIVE ACTIONS ARE LOCAL DETERMINISTIC PLANNING RATHER THAN A MODEL CALL and wiring creates NO DEPLOYMENT PREREQUISITE — which removes the excuse that the feature is unbuildable.** **THE MENU AS IT STANDS: WIRING IS MORE EXPENSIVE THAN IT READS AND HAS NO CONFIGURATION COST; REFUSING LEGIBLY REMAINS THE SMALLEST SAFE FIX; AND DELETING IS THE ONLY OPTION THAT REMOVES THE PANIC BY REMOVING THE PATH RATHER THAN BY GUARDING IT** — with the seat's **refusal to recommend among them beyond that asymmetry recorded as such.** **AND THIS DECISION NOW CARRIES THREE RUNTIME ERROR STRINGS TO REWORD WITH IT, ONE LINE PER MESSAGE — the current string, what is actually missing, and the sentence that would be true — because THE FALSE PART IS THE TEXT RATHER THAN THE BEHAVIOUR: the central factory IS ported and what is missing is that a HOOK IS UNFILLED, and the mock modules themselves DO exist.** **They are reworded WITH the wiring decision rather than before it, since the message is part of what a caller sees and a pre-emptive edit would create a second edit after the decision.** **AND THE RECOMMENDATION SPLITS THE FAMILY RATHER THAN DECIDING IT WHOLE: KEEP THE FACTORY SEAMS AND CORRECT THEIR TEXT** — the honest replacement for two of the three strings **IS A WIRING STATEMENT THAT ONLY EXISTS IF THE SEAMS ARE KEPT** — **AND DECIDE THE AI SEAM SEPARATELY, SINCE IT PANICS RATHER THAN REFUSING.** **AND THIS ROW NOW CARRIES THE SECOND INSTANCE OF ONE FAMILY RATHER THAN A SEPARATE ITEM (2026-10-06): A SECOND SEAM THAT NOTHING FILLS IN PRODUCTION — built by the server wiring, but THE BRANCH THAT WOULD USE THE UNFILLED HOOK IS SELECTED ONLY BY A CALLER ASKING FOR A MOCK OR CUSTOM REPOSITORY TYPE, and the only occurrences of those types in the tree are IN THE TEST FILE, so NO DISPATCHED REQUEST REACHES IT.** **AND THE CRITICAL HALF: WHEN THE UNFILLED PATH IS TAKEN IT RETURNS A LEGIBLE ERROR THAT NAMES WHAT IS MISSING rather than dereferencing a nil — WHICH IS EXACTLY THE DESIGN THE AI HOOK LACKS, AND THE REASON ONE HAS CRASHED WHILE THIS ONE HAS NEVER BEEN NOTICED.** **THE STANDARD'S COMPARISON FOR ANY SEAM: ASK WHAT THE UNFILLED PATH DOES BEFORE ASKING WHETHER ANYTHING REACHES IT — AN UNREACHABLE PANIC IS STILL A TRAP AND A REACHABLE ERROR IS STILL HONEST, SO REACHABILITY ALONE DOES NOT DETERMINE SEVERITY.** **DISPOSITION: LEFT UNTOUCHED, NOT WIRED, and folded here because the remaining question is THE SAME PRODUCT QUESTION — whether a ported capability nobody asks for should be WIRED, KEPT AS A LEGIBLE FAILURE, or REMOVED WITH ITS BRANCH AND ITS TEST.** **AND WHAT MAKES IT LOWER SEVERITY, in a form a reader can check: THE TEST PINS THE SEAM'S CONTRACT RATHER THAN HIDING AN UNUSED FIELD — it asserts that a MISSING SEAM MUST FAIL and that a FILLED ONE RETURNS, so IT WOULD FAIL IF THE SEAM WERE DROPPED, which is the strongest thing a test of a seam can do.**
- **(4) THE SEVEN ROUTE COMMENTS THAT SAY A CONTROLLER IS NOT PORTED YET — MEASURED BY IDENTITY RATHER THAN BY NAME, ALL SEVEN FALSE, and THE SAME RULE IS NOW BEING APPLIED TO THE FORTY-EIGHT**, because **A GO TYPE OF THE SAME NAME MAY EXIST AND BE A DIFFERENT THING.** **WHY THESE SEVEN ARE FIRST, which is the record's own history: A CLAIM OF EXACTLY THAT SHAPE ALREADY PROVED FALSE ONCE, on a branch controller — this is the SECOND TIME the shape has been looked at.** **RESULT, 2026-10-06: ALL SEVEN WERE FALSE** — each checked **by identity rather than by name** — and **the commit DELETES THE FALSE HALF IN EACH WHILE PRESERVING ANY TRUE DESCRIPTION** (the named findings and the clause-versus-line shape are in the `CHANGELOG.md` entry of the same date, which also carries the marker rule and the namesake counterexample). **AND THE FORTY-EIGHT ARE BEING CHECKED BY THE SAME RULE, BY IDENTITY RATHER THAN BY NAME, WITH THE COUNTEREXAMPLE THAT DIRECTION NEEDS: a file speaks about the PYTHON server while the Go side has its own `Server`, so A NAME MATCH ALONE WOULD PRODUCE A FALSE POSITIVE IN THE OPPOSITE DIRECTION — A NAMESAKE IS NOT THE CLAIM, in either direction.** **THE STANDING BOUNDARY, APPLIED FOR THE THIRD TIME: NO FRESH STATUS CLAIM ANYWHERE — delete the false half and never install a replacement that will itself go stale.** **The rest of the judgement pass is ruled and recorded in the same `CHANGELOG.md` entry ("The port-prose judgement pass"): true gap notes stay untouched, one group stays with its nuance recorded, and two groups remain unmeasured rather than decided on a guess.**
- **(5) A FACTORY SEAM WHOSE ONLY ASSIGNMENT IN THE WHOLE TREE IS IN A TEST — REACHABILITY ANSWERED (NO DISPATCHED PATH, AND THE UNFILLED BRANCH FAILS LEGIBLY), NOW AWAITING THE OWNER'S DECISION ON THE FAMILY.** **WHAT WAS MEASURED: NO DISPATCHED PATH REACHES THE SEAM — the object IS BUILT BY THE SERVER WIRING, so "unused" would be the wrong word; the branch that would call the unfilled hook is selected only by a caller asking for A MOCK OR A CUSTOM REPOSITORY TYPE, and THE ONLY OCCURRENCES OF THOSE TYPES IN THE TREE ARE IN THE TEST FILE, so NO REQUEST THE SERVER ACCEPTS ARRIVES THERE.** **AND THE CONSEQUENCE IN THAT BRANCH IS A LEGIBLE ERROR THAT NAMES WHAT IS MISSING — not a nil dereference — WHICH IS THE DESIGN THE AI HOOK LACKS AND THE REASON ONE HAS CRASHED WHILE THIS ONE HAS NEVER BEEN NOTICED.** **SO WHAT IT AWAITS IS THE OWNER'S DECISION ON THE FAMILY, the same product question as the AI menu: WIRE IT, KEEP IT AS A LEGIBLE FAILURE, OR REMOVE THE SEAM WITH ITS BRANCH AND ITS TEST.** **ITS SEVERITY IS LOWER THAN THE AI HOOK FOR THE MEASURED REASON RATHER THAN FOR A JUDGEMENT, and THE REASON IS THE FAILURE MODE, NOT THE REACHABILITY: AN UNREACHABLE PANIC IS STILL A TRAP AND A REACHABLE ERROR IS STILL HONEST.** **AND THE OTHER HALF TO KEEP: THE TEST PINS THE SEAM'S CONTRACT RATHER THAN HIDING AN UNUSED FIELD — it asserts that a MISSING SEAM MUST FAIL and that a FILLED ONE RETURNS, so THE SEAM CANNOT BE DROPPED WITHOUT THE TEST FAILING, which is the strongest thing a test of a seam can do.** **The pair is the standard's comparison now (ask what the unfilled path does before asking whether anything reaches it), and the description lives in the `CHANGELOG.md` entry of the same date; this row carries what is owed.**
- **(6) THE PIPELINE TESTS NOTHING THAT SHIPS — PROPOSAL AWAITING THE OWNER'S WORD, not open work.** **NOTHING IN THE PIPELINE RUNS ANY TEST OF THE SHIPPED CODE: no workflow mentions the frontend test runner, the frontend is built as an image WITHOUT ITS TESTS, and NEITHER workflow installs or runs Go — so after the de-link what remains is A SECURITY SCAN PLUS A WORKFLOW STILL BUILDING THE ARCHIVED PYTHON TREE.** **THE CONSEQUENCE, WHICH IS THE POINT RATHER THAN THE LIST: EVERY GATE THAT GATED TONIGHT'S WORK RAN BECAUSE A SEAT RAN IT, so A GREEN PUSH SAYS NOTHING ABOUT WHETHER THE TESTS PASS — and A PIPELINE THAT TESTS NOTHING THAT SHIPS IS WORSE THAN A RED ONE, BECAUSE A RED BUILD INVITES A FIX AND A GREEN ONE INVITES TRUST.** **THE PROPOSAL, awaiting the owner rather than agreed: TWO JOBS THAT ARE THE EXACT COMMANDS THE SEATS RUN BY HAND, with THE DATABASE-GATED TESTS EXPLICITLY NOT PROPOSED FOR CI WITHOUT THE OWNER'S SAY.** **The description lives in the `CHANGELOG.md` entry of the same date; this row carries what is owed.**

**(A) DOCUMENTATION = THE REAL API AND MCP.** Rewrite the project documentation to document the Go server's actually mounted routes and the MCP tool surface as they exist today, replacing stale and legacy descriptions. The constraint is the acceptance rule, quoted so it survives this session: *every documented endpoint must grep to a real mount, and no document may describe a removed route, table or the retired agent library as live.* The reference for that rewrite is the authoritative surface inventory (owner directive B, step 1); the rewrite itself is step 2 and is GATED on that inventory - rewriting from memory would reproduce exactly the stale-doc problem the directive is about.

**(B) DOCS AND FRONTEND ALIGN WITH THIS FILE.** Bring the documentation and the frontend in line with `NEXT_GEN.md`: the OpenRig client and cloud-data direction, the seat model, the retired agent library, the removed routes and tables. Constraint, quoted: *no document may describe a removed route, table or the retired agent library as live.* The frontend half belongs to its own seat; the documentation half is the same rewrite as (A).

**(C) SLIM `AGENTS.md` (owner directive, 2026-10-05).** The root agent-instruction file must stop carrying the full context now that context is split per seat — each seat receives its own role file (`agents/<seat>/agent.yaml`, `guidance/role.md`) at launch. `AGENTS.md` carries only: the hazard note, identity (`rig whoami --json`), where context lives (seat role files, the queue, this file), the universal hard rules, and pointers. The detail moves into `ai_docs/` with a mapping from every section that left. Constraints, quoted: *"AGENTS.md contains only the five items above - a reviewer must be able to point at every paragraph and name which of the five it is"* and *"no rule was silently weakened in the move - quote the before and after for the hard rules"*.

**(D) SEAT CUSTOMIZATION IN THE FRONTEND (owner directive, 2026-10-05).** A user customizes a seat by adding and removing blocks, modelled on how OpenRig presents composition: `SeatAuthoringPage` is rebuilt around one block at a time, showing inheritance and what a removal does. The frontend seat owns the build.

**The model behind (D) — owner-stated 2026-10-05, quoted, then interpreted.** *"context no more store on local, when agent call seat, it call full context + skill + tools documentation needed about his seat, we can use multiple mcp but seat use tools maybe different, it defind on each seat"* and *"user can custom seat, drag and drop skill, mcp tools for configurer + custom seat if they need, by default we have preconfigured seat for default team creation"*.

That is the existing contract, not a new one, in three of its four parts — verified by reading, not assumed:

| Part of the owner's statement | Status |
|---|---|
| A seat call delivers its own context + skills + tool documentation | **Already built.** `seatrenderer.RenderSeat` emits guidance (instruction + document + memory modules), one `skills/<slug>/SKILL.md` per skill module, and for claude-code seats an MCP fragment plus a settings fragment merged from every tool module. |
| The block vocabulary | **Already built.** `resolver.go:17-21`: `instruction`, `document`, `skill`, `tool`, `memory`. `comm-guard.json` is a real `tool` block. |
| Put/remove blocks with inheritance | **Already built.** Overlays fold company -> room -> seat, four ops (`add`, `remove`, `override`, `pin`). HTTP API already mounts all three levels plus module publish and seat-type versions. |
| **A seat choosing which MCP servers mount** | **The gap.** `seat_resolution_service.go:85` calls `RenderSeat(resolved, s.MCPURL)` and `renderMCPFragment` (renderer.go:423) hardcodes ONE server for every seat. What differs per seat today is the permission set, not the server set. |

Four owner decisions resolve the open questions. They are the acceptance criteria for this work:

- **D1 — the seat selects servers.** A seat's `mcp` blocks decide which servers mount into that seat; permission fragments (the `tool` kind) keep narrowing within a mounted server. Owner's own shape: `seat lead / mcp: [agenthub_http, sequential-thinking]` against `seat go-dev / mcp: [agenthub_http]`.
- **D2 — one MCP block is one whole server**, not one tool. The palette stays short; per-tool narrowing happens through the permission layer.
- **D3 — "custom seat" means both**: composing blocks onto the nine seeded seat types, AND creating a new seat type with its own role text and blocks (`POST /api/v2/openrig/seat-types/{slug}/versions` already exists).
- **D4 — the palette is the catalog PLUS an import path**: published module versions and the seeded blocks, and project-local material (`.claude/skills/*`, MCP servers from `.mcp.json`) publishable as blocks. Without the import path the palette starts nearly empty.

**Model consequence, stated before any code is written.** A server block is a **new module kind** (proposed `mcp`), not a second meaning for `tool`: `mergeToolModules` merges tool content as JSON settings fragments, and one kind carrying two meanings would break the renderer's grouping and the frontend's single source of truth (`seatTypes.ts:25-28`). A new kind costs a `ValidKind` entry, a `kindRank` position, a renderer branch, seed entries for the nine seat types, and the frontend union — and it makes "by default we have preconfigured seat for default team creation" real, because the seeded types would then ship with sensible server sets.

**Payload note for the implementer:** an `mcp` block's content must express what `.mcp.json` already expresses — name, type (http or stdio), url or command plus args, and headers. The built-in server's bearer header uses `${OpenRigTokenEnvVar}`; a block that carries its own server needs its own header story, and secrets stay out of the block (the "never print secrets" rule below applies to any imported server definition).

**GENERIC ACROSS PROJECTS (owner constraint, 2026-10-05, quoted: *"attention make it generic for all project"*).** Seat composition is a capability of the platform that serves any workspace a user connects — not a surface that knows about this repository. Four consequences, and the fourth is the one that changes the design rather than the wording:

1. **No hardcoded names.** No block, seat type or server may be pinned to 4genthub. `agenthub_http` is one published block among many, never a required one; the nine seeded seat types stay role-generic so a fresh workspace gets a sensible team on day one.
2. **The catalog is per workspace.** Company, room and seat overlays already resolve per user and per room; a block resolves inside the workspace that owns it, and one workspace's catalog must not leak into another's palette.
3. **A second project must onboard without a code change.** Its own `.mcp.json` and its own `.claude/skills/*` become blocks through the same publish path the UI uses. Acceptance rests on that case, not on revisiting 4genthub's own blocks.
4. **Import cannot be a server-side file read.** The Direction section above is explicit: *"the client initiates every exchange; the server never reaches into a user's machine."* The browser cannot read the user's disk either. So reading `.mcp.json` and `.claude/skills/*` is **client-side work** — a CLI step that pushes what it finds to `PUT /api/v2/openrig/modules/{slug}/versions/{version}` — with a UI paste or upload path as the manual fallback for one block at a time. A server-side read of a path on the user's machine would contradict the direction this file is built on, so it is not an option to weigh, only to avoid. The natural home for that client step is the same client-side scripts that already hold the token and push observations back (`scripts/openrig_seat_sync.py`, `openrig_bridge.py`) — the implementer verifies which one owns project-scoped reads before adding a third.

**(E) A DOCUMENTATION PAGE IN THE FRONTEND (owner directive, 2026-10-05).** Owner's words: *"i want page Documentation represent like this page"*, with the pdf2w API reference pasted as the reference. That reference is a local repo, so its mechanism is readable rather than guessed: `~/__projects__/markdown-extract-service/app/web/src/pages/ApiDocs.jsx`, `src/components/Markdown.jsx`, `src/lib/markdownHtml.js`, `src/docs/api-reference.en.md`.

What that page is, mechanically:

- The markdown is imported **raw from the app's own source tree** (`src/docs/`), so what a reader sees is the file that gets reviewed — its own words: *"generated from the same file the repository ships, so what you read here is what the code reviews."*
- `{{API_ORIGIN}}` and `{{MCP_URL}}` are substituted **at render time** from runtime config, so a self-hosted install documents itself instead of someone else's domain. This is the same genericity rule as (D), expressed in content.
- Heading ids are assigned **at render** and the "On this page" list is built from the rendered `h2`/`h3`, so the TOC cannot drift from the file.
- Rendering is `marked` (gfm) into `DOMPurify.sanitize` before `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` — two dependencies, one sanitizer policy in one module.
- Language lives in the URL (`/docs/en|fr|vi`), and the footer carries `version (commit) • date`.

**Build constraint that decides where the file lives — verified, not assumed.** `docker-system/docker/Dockerfile.frontend.production` builds with `COPY agenthub-frontend/ .`, so anything outside `agenthub-frontend/` does not exist at build time. The markdown therefore lives **inside the frontend tree**, exactly as the reference does; a source file under `ai_docs/` would render fine locally and then be absent from the image. This is a build input, not agent prose, so the "documents live in `ai_docs/`" convention is not bent by it — worth one line in the file's header so a reviewer does not file it as a violation.

**4genthub's page needs what the reference has, plus our own truth source:** a `/docs` route, the same render pipeline, the same placeholder rule, and content covering the real route surface and the MCP tool surface — which is directive (A)'s deliverable, so the two converge: (A) writes the file, (E) gives it a page. Acceptance:

- the page renders the reviewed file; there is no second copy to drift;
- every endpoint named in the file greps to a real mount (directive A's rule, unchanged);
- a second deployment with a different origin renders its own URLs and version with no code change.

Split: a frontend seat builds the shell and the pipeline; the `writer` seat fills the markdown from the surface inventory. The shell must not wait on the inventory — it renders whatever the file contains, so an empty or partial document is a valid intermediate state, not a blocked one.

**(F) MOVING OPENRIG INTO THE CLOUD (owner decisions 2026-10-05).** The owner asked which parts of OpenRig can move to 4genthub, then answered four questions. All five candidates are IN. Ownership: **the cloud computes** — the logic is ported to Go and the client reports raw facts. Sequencing: the content slice runs now, the code-bearing items queue behind (D) and (E). The skills are **mirrored with a digest guard**.

OpenRig is the owner's own repository (`phamhung075/openrig`, Apache-2.0, `private: true`), so this is a design decision and not a licensing one; the Apache notice travels with any content taken from it. A full module inventory of OpenRig was taken on 2026-10-05 (daemon: 408 `.ts` files under `domain/`, 65 routers, 92 migrations; cli: 85 commands and 18 MCP tools; ui: declared **maintenance mode**; tui; skills) and the paths below come from it, so a seat does not re-survey.

**F1 — the skill library (content, no code) — DELIVERED, verified 2026-10-05.** The library is **52 distinct skills**, and the count needs care because it is the union of two **committed** edges, not one directory:

| Edge (committed) | Skills | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `skills/_canonical/` (mirrored to `packages/daemon/specs/agents/shared/skills/`) | 35 | `core/` 17, `pm/` 7, `pods/` 4, `process/` 7 |
| `packages/daemon/assets/plugins/openrig-core/skills/` | 19 | flat; **this is the edge that carries `delegating-work` and `queue-handoff`** |

They overlap by two names (`messaging-the-human`, `openrig-skills`), so the union is **52**. That union is why seeding the obvious 35 is the wrong move: our own `lead.yaml` names `delegating-work` and `queue-handoff`, and both live only on the 19-skill plugin edge. Seeding the canonical 35 alone would leave the seat types referencing blocks that still do not exist — the exact defect this row exists to clear.

**Do not read the daemon's assembled tree as the source.** `packages/daemon/context-packs/` is **gitignored** (`.gitignore:44`) and is a build artifact assembled from the edges at package time; it is also where the 52 first caught the eye, since it is what the daemon actually ships. Digests must pin the **committed** files on the two edges, or the guard measures a rebuild against itself. OpenRig's own `.agents/skills/` is a local working set (1 committed skill), not part of the library.

The 52 become catalog blocks of kind `skill`, published through the module publish path, and the nine seat types gain refs to the ones they name, so a default team arrives with its skills already attached. Each published block carries its **source path and sha256**, and a check compares stored against source and **reports drift** rather than diverging silently — OpenRig already guards its own three mirrors with pinned digests (`scripts/skill-edge-digests.generated.json`), and we produced this exact bug by hand today when two copies of the rules file disagreed.

**F1 delivered — what closed it (verified 2026-10-05).** `913d57bd` (writer) produced the 52-skill inventory (`ai_docs/agent-system/skill-library.json` + `.md`) with `source_path` and sha256 provenance plus the per-seat curation; `413c74de` (go-dev) delivered the publish path, the drift check, the nine seat types wired and the renderer unwrap (`seat_management/domain/skillblock`, seedmap, `scripts/openrig_team_setup.py`). Verified read-only by this seat: the inventory's own count is **52** = canonical **35** + plugin **19** with **2** overlap, and the two overlaps are **identical by digest** (`messaging-the-human` `581372434f27…` and `openrig-skills` `e5f47e24a4c3…`, the same value on both edges in `skill-library.json`); OpenRig's pinned digest file (`~/__projects__/openrig/scripts/skill-edge-digests.generated.json`) carries canonical **51** and plugin **41** with the same `messaging-the-human` value `581372434f27…`, so the overlap count arrives from BOTH directions. The reviewer's verification that closes the row: the full 52-row digest cross-check against OpenRig's own pinned file, **zero misses out of 54 digest assertions**, and the drift check **reproduced hermetically** — clean exits 0 with NO output, one byte flipped exits 1 naming the slug, the path, the root and BOTH hashes, restored exits 0. **One named follow-up, NOT a defect:** the pinned digest file's KEYS do not resolve as paths under the current OpenRig root — `core/agent-starters/SKILL.md` is absent on disk (verified: `ls` fails) while the inventory's `generated_from` and its paths name the current layout (`skills/_canonical/<group>/<name>` and `packages/daemon/assets/plugins/openrig-core/skills/<name>`, both verified present) — so the guard is sound BY VALUE while its path keys describe an older tree; whoever re-derives that file must confirm which layout it keys against. This replaces the open box that read "runs now".

**F2 — the context-pack algebra (PORTED, NOT WIRED — status corrected 2026-10-08 by the architect).** `9924b1fc` (2026-10-06) ported it as the pure package `fastmcp/seat_management/domain/contextpacks`; `go test` on it passes (run 2026-10-08), and a grep finds no importer outside its own tests. O4 below is its first consumer. Original text, kept for traceability: `domain/context-packs/`: `profile-composer.ts` (three modes — `FRESH` base walk, `HANDOVER` with handover atoms, `POST-COMPACTION` tagged subset plus handover), `token-estimate.ts` (token budgeting), `seat-recap-store.ts` (the outgoing occupant authors the recap), `bundle-assembler.ts`, `ref-safety.ts`. Pure logic, no OS dependency. This is the composition brain the cloud's module/overlay model lacks.

**F3 — the queue ledger (queued).** `domain/queue-repository.ts`, `queue-owner.ts`, `queue-pickup.ts`, `queue-transition-log.ts`, `queue-wake-{repository,ladder,backoff}.ts`, `queue-retention.ts`, `queue-stuck-sweep.ts`, `claim-service.ts`, `hot-potato-enforcer.ts`, `human-questions.ts`, `human-route-enforcer.ts`, `seat-delivery-guard.ts`. The property worth keeping intact: **a pickup receipt is derived from facts and never written by the claimant**, so the ledger cannot be talked into a claim its evidence does not support. States: `pending`, `claimed`, `handed-off`, `blocked`, `done`.

**The sharp edge, and the owner's ruling on it (2026-10-05).** "The cloud computes" makes the cloud's ledger the **system of record**, but OpenRig's daemon also owns a `queue_items` ledger locally — it is what actually wakes a seat. Two ledgers of the same work diverge, and the failure is silent: a seat works on something the cloud believes is unclaimed.

The owner chose **the cloud always wins**: on any disagreement a seat abandons its local view and takes the cloud's state as truth. The implementable form of that, stated so the code does not have to rediscover it:

- **Claims route through the cloud; they are not reported to it.** A seat requests a claim, the cloud grants it, the client then records it locally. With the grant coming from the cloud there is no claim divergence to reconcile at all — which is what "cloud always wins" means when taken seriously. A claim the cloud has not granted is not a claim.
- **Staleness is the trap, and routing is what defuses it.** For an observation (status, usage, model) the cloud wins by definition, because it is the record. But a cloud reading that is merely *older* than the client's is not authority, it is lag — a seat forced to honour a stale "unclaimed" is how two seats end up on one task. Routing every claim decision through the cloud removes the case rather than ranking it.
- **Observational divergence is reported, not silently overwritten**, so the lag is visible in the same way `seatsync` refuses to call two different hashes equal.

**Three more owner decisions, 2026-10-05, same round.** They answer the last questions before implementation:

1. **A default team arrives with a curated skill set.** Roughly six per seat type, chosen from the 52 and mapped to the role; the rest stay in the catalog to drag. Every skill a role text already names must be in its set (`lead` -> `delegating-work`, `queue-handoff`; `developer` and `tester` -> `test-driven-development`; `debugger` -> `systematic-debugging`), and the curation is a judgement the lead records in the slice, not a mechanical attach-all.
2. **Permission levels are vocabulary, not enforcement.** `locked`, `standard`, `open`, `yolo` become the seat's `permission_policy` vocabulary and land as **advisory prose** in the seat's guidance. Enforcement stays where it already is — in the rendered `tool` modules that produce real deny rules (`comm-guard` is the working example). This keeps OpenRig's advisory, fail-open stance and does not create a second enforcement path.
3. **Order after (D) and (E): F2, then F4, then F3.** Context packs first (pure logic, no divergence risk, and it makes (D)'s composition smarter), then usage and policies (small, data-only), then the queue ledger (largest, and it now depends on claim routing rather than on a reconciliation policy).

**F4 — usage samples and permission policies (HALF BUILT — status noted 2026-10-08 by the architect: the `policy` module kind exists (`resolver.go:29`, `ck_modules_kind`, `97fb2979`); no usage-samples code exists in `agenthub_go` (grep)).** Original text: `domain/usage-samples-store.ts` (**advance-only**: a byte-identical sample in the same lane is refused, so an idle seat adds zero rows), `domain/usage-series.ts` (over-time query and burn projection; it serves facts and leaves thresholds at the edge), `domain/model-divergence/*` (runtime-versus-declared model). Policies are data: `packages/daemon/policies/builtin/{locked,standard,open,yolo}.policy.md`, parsed by `domain/permission-policy/policy-spec.ts` — a Markdown plus YAML-frontmatter contract that is **advisory and fail-open, never enforcement**. That stance is worth copying deliberately, not accidentally: it decides what a seat does with a policy it cannot parse.

**(G) THE HOME PAGE MATCHES THE PRODUCT (owner directive, 2026-10-05).** Owner's words: *"https://www.4genthub.com/ home page, need rewite content, match all NEXT-GEN for promotion"*. The page is `agenthub-frontend/src/pages/LandingPage.tsx`, rendered at `/` for anonymous visitors (`App.tsx:221`), plus the static head in `index.html`.

Read at 22:22 on 2026-10-05, the copy is a mixture, and the stale parts are specific:

| Claim on the page today | What is true |
|---|---|
| `index.html:14` title **"Agenthub Task Manager"** | The deployed title. The React page sets its own title at runtime, so the static head is what a crawler, a link preview and the pre-hydration view show. |
| Feature **"4-Tier Context System"** — *Global -> Project -> Branch -> Task* | The retired Python-era hierarchy. The live model is company -> room -> seat overlays, plus context packs (F2). |
| Feature **"Claude Code Integration ... via MCP protocol"** | Narrows the product to one runtime. The direction is OpenRig as client *and* runtime, with Claude Code, `omp` and codex as runtimes, and 4genthub as the cloud data for orchestration. |
| Feature **"Lightning Fast Development ... 10x faster than manual work"** | No measurement exists behind the number. |
| Feature **"Enterprise Security ... every message is audited against your team policy"** | Overstates enforcement. The policy layer is **advisory and fail-open** by design (F4). What is actually enforced is seat links and the permission fragments rendered into a seat. |
| "Rooms, Seats and Modules", step 3 "Compose Your Rooms" | Correct and current — keep them. |

Two structural gaps beyond the copy: `index.html` carries **no `og:` tags at all**, so a shared link previews as nothing, and there is no description meta.

**The discipline is directive (A)'s rule, applied to marketing: the page may claim only what ships and greps.** The queue ledger (F3), the context-pack algebra (F2) and teams/sharing (D5) are **not built** and must not appear as live features; roadmap material stays out of promotion. Acceptance:

- every claim maps to a capability a reviewer can point at in the code or the API;
- the static head describes the same product as the page body;
- no number appears without a measurement behind it.

One seat owns both `LandingPage.tsx` and `index.html`; the `writer` seat reviews the copy before it lands rather than editing the same file.

**(H) SEATS CAN REPORT FRICTION (owner directive, 2026-10-05).** Owner's words: *"when these llm use 4genthub and openrig, if have contraint or need improve, they can feedback for we can make better"*. The seats are the only users of this platform who hit its edges every day, and today their friction dies in a transcript. This is the channel that captures it.

Four owner decisions, all recorded as chosen:

1. **One endpoint, two front doors.** `POST /api/v2/openrig/feedback` — **DECIDED, NOT YET MOUNTED** (the route is not in the HEAD mount set and the `seat_feedback` table and page are unbuilt; this is a decision to implement, not a live surface). A claude-code seat calls an MCP tool; an `omp` or codex seat — which has no MCP at all — runs a shell command. Both write the same record, so every runtime can submit.
2. **One line at submit time, with optional detail.** The seat writes a sentence; the client attaches rig, seat, runtime, model, timestamp, cwd and the row in flight. Optional flags add `--layer openrig|cloud|project|role` and `--severity blocked|annoying|idea`. Capture must stay cheap while a seat is mid-task, or it will not happen.
3. **Stored in the cloud, read in the frontend.** A `seat_feedback` table per user and workspace, and a page that groups by layer and shows recurrence, so a theme that four seats hit is visible as four, not as four separate notes.
4. **Built in 4genthub, not in OpenRig.** The seats already carry a token and already POST upward, so no OpenRig change is needed and the channel stays generic for every project.

**The seam is already there, and it carries one constraint that must be handled deliberately.** `POST /api/v2/openrig/seat-status` is wrapped in `machineAuthed(sessions, func(w, r, token *repositories.MachineToken))` (`seat_status_mount.go:91`), and the machine-token doc comment states the rule plainly: *"A machine token is valid on one route, POST /api/v2/openrig/seat-status, and only for the [token's] machine"* (`machine_token_mount.go:8`). Adding a second route means **widening that contract on purpose** — a documented second valid route — rather than discovering later that a token silently stopped working. The read side is the easy half: `GET` takes the ordinary user token via `authed` and is tenant-scoped by the caller's user id, exactly as `GET /api/v2/openrig/machines` does.

Two more things the existing endpoint already does that this one should copy rather than reinvent: **every string field is scanned for credentials before storage** — so a seat pasting a token into its own feedback does not store a secret, and the `secretscan` package already exists for it — and **reports store under the token's user and machine**, which is the tenancy this table needs too.

Acceptance: a seat on a runtime without MCP can submit from the shell; a claude-code seat can submit by tool call; both appear in the same list; and a submission carrying something credential-shaped is stored with that field redacted rather than rejected or leaked.

**Bugs are the same channel with one more axis (owner decision, 2026-10-05, same day).** Owner's words: *"they feedback and report bug too"*. So `--kind friction|bug` joins `--layer` and `--severity` as a third, orthogonal axis — a bug is not a severity, it is a kind of report, and a bug can be `annoying` or `idea`.

- **A bug needs the same one cheap line**, with evidence optional: `--expected`, `--actual`, `--repro`. The API must **not** require them, because the moment a bug report costs more than the friction report did, seats stop filing at exactly the moment they are blocked. The list instead shows a **"no repro" badge**, which is what makes triage possible without making capture expensive.
- **The cloud is the record; the CLIENT files the GitHub issue.** The server stores every report; a client-side sync turns the **platform-layer** ones into issues — `layer=openrig` into `phamhung075/openrig`, `layer=cloud` into `phamhung075/4genthub` — using the `gh` auth already on the machine. `layer=project` and `layer=role` stay in the cloud; they are about a workspace and its guidance, not about the platform. This keeps a repo-scoped credential out of the cloud entirely, which is the same reasoning the pull model rests on and the same class of risk this project already lived through once in the 2026-10-05 token exposure.
- **One bug is one issue, however many seats hit it.** Four seats hitting the same defect means four reports and **one** issue carrying the count and the reporter links — the recurrence view already groups them, and the sync must respect that grouping rather than opening four. Without that rule the sync is a spam cannon aimed at the owner's own repositories.
- **`severity=blocked` notifies; everything else waits in the list.** One honest caveat recorded rather than assumed: the push path is D3 (the human-notification route), which is still blocked on D1 and does not exist yet. Until it does, the interim signal is already real and needs no new work — a seat that reports `blocked` is also a seat that reads as `blocked` in `rig ps` and in the cloud's seat status, because the bridge maps attention to that state. The notification is an improvement on a signal that already exists, not the only way this gets seen.

**Pilot entries for the channel (observed 2026-10-05, while growing this rig).** Two real OpenRig defects found by using it, recorded here because the channel that would hold them is still being built and neither should be lost:

1. **`rig grow` cannot set a permission policy, and the per-seat verb that would repair it is refused for `omp`.** `rig grow` takes `--runtime` and `--cwd` but no permission-policy flag, so a grown seat lands with `nodes.permission_policy` **NULL** while a seat launched from a spec gets `builtin:yolo`. The consequence is not cosmetic: the grown seat can read and grep, but every `write`, `edit` and `bash` raises an approval request that a managed RPC pane **cannot answer**, so the action is cancelled and fails. The obvious repair, `rig seat set-permissions --mode full_bypass`, is refused — *"per-seat permission mode is unsupported for `omp`"* — leaving a grown `omp` seat with **no supported repair path**, only a spec-based re-add. Verified on three seats: `skills-dev`, `context-dev`, `feedback-dev`.
2. **`rig terminal open` is not idempotent.** Running it twice for the same rig opens a **second** herdr workspace instead of reusing or focusing the first, so a watcher ends up with two workspaces under one label — one with 9 panes, one with 12.

Both are the kind of report directive (H) exists to capture: `layer=openrig`, and both are `severity=annoying` rather than `blocked` for the platform, even though entry 1 blocked three seats in this rig.

**Never moved, whatever else changes.** All of `adapters/` (tmux plus the five runtime adapters), `terminal/TerminalSessionBroker.ts`, the `daemon-lifecycle*` scripts, `restore-packet/*` transcript parsers, and the vendor-local readers (`claude-usage-reader`, `codex-auth-reader`). These are this machine's processes, and the Direction above already assigns them to the client. Do not port the 92 SQLite migrations either: the cloud schema already exists as Postgres DDL and is re-derived, not translated.

**D1-D4 slice plan (2026-10-05).** The decisions above are the owner's wording; this is the pointer table to the work, so a reader can find it without the queue. Row ids are the durable addresses.

| Slice | Seat | Row |
|---|---|---|
| D1/D2 backend — the `mcp` module kind and rendering the seat's server set (replaces the hardcoded single server) | go-dev | `qitem-20261005194434-5980ba3f75f64033` |
| D1/D2/D4 frontend — the `mcp` block kind in the palette (one server per block) + the publish-one-block fallback; **gated on the backend kind** | web-dev | `qitem-20261005194435-5bbfdb5aee77876a` |
| D3 — verify what "custom seat = both" means today (compose blocks + create a new seat type with its own role text) from the mounts and handlers | go-dev | `qitem-20261005194446-0c6a16bfb45491bd` |
| D4 client side — the import path that fills the palette from a project's own `.mcp.json` and `.claude/skills/*` (the client pushes; the server never reads local disk) | go-dev | `qitem-20261005194447-a511710c95f912d7` |
| Record this plan in this file | writer | `qitem-20261005194448-636ee1ff2644f2cb` |

**D1/D2 DELIVERED — the `mcp` module kind is on main (`7a5d792a`).** `resolver.KindMCP` ships with its own `ValidKind` entry and `kindRank` position, deliberately **not** a second meaning for `tool`; `renderMCPFragment` merges the seat's mounted blocks in resolved order into one `mcpServers` fragment; the built-in server is now the payload of the seeded block `agenthub-http` through the same merge rather than a special case; two guard tests at `renderer_test.go:600` and `:625` pin it, the second failing if the hardcoded single server returns; all nine seeded types carry their server sets; and an imported server's secrets are env-var references only, refused by `secretscan` at load AND at render.

**D1/D2 DEPLOY-PACKET GATE — the owner-gated DDL that makes it usable in production (pending approval).** Production holds the **five**-value `ck_modules_kind` list while the tree's schema file and boot DDL carry the **six** values (`instruction`, `document`, `skill`, `tool`, `mcp`, `memory`), so an `mcp` block **cannot be published today**: the feature is **inert, not broken**. The deploy-packet item is `ALTER TABLE modules DROP CONSTRAINT ck_modules_kind;` then `ADD CONSTRAINT ck_modules_kind CHECK (kind IN (...the six...));` — owner-gated, and no other deploy step is needed for it. **Observed in the wild (2026-10-06):** a database created BEFORE the constraint was widened fails the seed with `violates check constraint "ck_modules_kind"`, because `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` never updates a constraint — it reads like bad seed data and is not. The general line worth keeping beside it: **A SCHEMA CREATED BY `IF NOT EXISTS` IS A SNAPSHOT OF WHEN IT FIRST RAN**, and no later DDL change reaches it without a migration.

**Test-record correction — `57de3715` (`resolver_test.go`, +24, one test): the property was ALREADY PINNED, and the new test is KEPT.** go-dev added determinism coverage and then found its own premise was wrong: `TestResolveHashDeterministic` already existed at `resolver_test.go:344`, asserting equal hashes on the base seat type with nil overlays, and the check that reported nothing had used a **case-sensitive** pattern for a **capitalised** test name, so the grep answered a narrower question than the sentence written around it. Outcome: the property was already pinned; the new test is **kept** because its input is strictly stronger — five modules plus a company overlay with two ops, so it exercises the map-iteration ordering in `resolveModules`, which is where nondeterminism would actually break — and it adds a non-empty assertion the older one lacks. Its commit message says plainly that it is smaller than the request assumed.

**Records addendum (2026-10-05).** (a) **The fold order is a two-door invariant, independently confirmed.** The reviewer verified from the source that resolution folds company → room → seat, AND that the SAME function folds the candidate during a write validation — so the read path and the write guard cannot disagree about order (one stack, one fold). That is the pin-change design requirement ("one invariant, two doors, one fold") confirmed from outside its own author. (b) **The registration count is DATED, not pinned.** Three counts of one thing: 120 in the inventory snapshot, the reviewer 122 (a pattern that likely includes `_test.go` files), the lead 121 — carry the date and the pattern beside any figure rather than a bare number, per rule 3.

**Validation-blocking hook fix (`d27ea24`, `.claude` hooks repo — not this tree).** The `UserPromptSubmit` hook validator required `CLAUDE.md` by name; it now accepts `AGENTS.md` or `CLAUDE.md`. That is why a project carrying either name validates after the rename.

**Repo change preceding (C) and (D) — `CLAUDE.md` renamed to `AGENTS.md` (`f7a809dc`, local, 2026-10-05).** Claude Code reads `AGENTS.md` natively and it is the cross-tool convention (Codex, Gemini, omp seats read it too); the hooks, the allowed-root-files config and the legacy launcher were updated with the rename. **The old rule "`CLAUDE.md`, `.claude` and `agenthub_go/seatcheck` stay out of every commit" is superseded for its first item:** `CLAUDE.md` no longer exists and `AGENTS.md` is tracked, so the keep-out now applies to `.claude/` and `agenthub_go/seatcheck` only.

**Operational fact for readers - `AGENTHUB_TOKEN` rotation (2026-10-05).** The rotation for this rig is COMPLETE and verified across the daemon and all four seats, and the OLD TOKEN IS NOW REVOKED (`tok_5bf9b2d3db9fd747`, revoked 19:46 local; the API answered "Token revoked successfully" and the row reads `is_active false`, owner-verified — relayed by the lead, not observed from this seat). The owner migrated and verified the whole stack per process BEFORE the revoke. A 401 on a production call during the switch is the rotation, not a code defect - check the token before debugging code. Still open and recorded on the token-exposure item below: two git-ignored November 2025 logs hold a JWT header fragment with no signature (unusable), and a duplicate token sits in the tracked `.claude/settings.local.json` pending the owner's cleanup decision.

## Owner demands (2026-10-06) — research first, then BUILD ON THE ANSWER (the owner's confirmed sequence)

**▲ TOP PRIORITY — OWNER PRIORITY BUILD, 2026-10-06 (PACKET 5 — AND IT MUST NOT TOUCH THE PACKET-4 GATE).** **THE DECISION, IN THE OWNER'S TERMS: omp seats must get their MCP servers (`agenthub_http` first) FROM THE SEAT RENDER, so that a seat can sync context through `api.4genthub.com/mcp` — ONE WAY ONLY, NO COMPATIBILITY LAYER.** **IT IS PACKET 5, NOT PACKET 4 (the owner stated it; the lead confirms), so NOTHING HERE CHANGES THE PACKET-4 GATE AND NOTHING HERE TOUCHES THE TIP LINE.** **THE FULL DISPATCH — the six facts below, the plan A–E, the serialization between go-dev2 and go-dev, the acceptance, and the flags — is `MCP-OMP-DISPATCH.md` beside this rig's seat area. THIS ROW IS THE DURABLE RECORD AND POINTS THERE RATHER THAN DUPLICATING IT.** **The two rows: go-dev2 `qitem-20261006170508-75af2e033b8609c3` (the vertical A/B/C/E), go-dev `qitem-20261006170509-5480cce55d43132f` (D, the client merge).**

**THE OWNER'S SIX MEASURED FACTS (supplied, not re-derived; the first and the sixth were re-read in the tree by the writer seat):** **(1)** `fastmcp/seat_management/domain/seatrenderer/renderer.go:113-117` renders an MCP fragment for **claude-code seats only** — `receivesClaudeFragments` returns true for exactly that runtime (`:203-207`, "codex, agy and omp get guidance and skills only") and the comment states OpenRig has **no omp/codex/agy MCP fragment type**. **(2) The rig `.mcp.json` is OPERATOR-OWNED, preserved by `9c4a5981`, and holds only the deepseek server.** **(3) EVERY member in `rig-omp.yaml` has `cwd: .`**, so all ten seats share **ONE** `.mcp.json` and per-seat server selection cannot ride that file. **(4) The endpoint works:** `initialize` and `tools/list` answer **200 with the token and 403 without**. **(5) `AGENTHUB_TOKEN` is in the seat harness environment; `AGENTHUB_URL` is NOT; the seat `AGENTS.md` never mentions MCP.** **(6) The codex rules file is the precedent** (`renderer.go:141-147`, with `codexRulesPath = "runtime/codex.rules"` at `:25`): a runtime with no fragment type gets its file **inside the render**, and the client installs it.

**AND STEP A IS ALREADY HALF-MEASURED — the lead's addition, made under the house rule that says check whether the fact is written before repeating it.** **`MCP-RIG-FILE.md` (seat area, fe-dev, 2026-10-06) holds a REAL BEFORE/AFTER PROBE on the runtime: a fresh `omp -p "…list any MCP tools…"` in the rig directory answered `NONE` WITHOUT a `.mcp.json` and the five deepseek tools WITH one — so OMP DOES READ A PROJECT-LEVEL `.mcp.json` AT STARTUP**, and that file records the precedence the runtime documents (`.omp/mcp.json` highest project priority; root `mcp.json`/`.mcp.json` as the lowest portable fallbacks). **THE OPEN HALF OF A IS THEREFORE NARROW AND SPECIFIC: (a) whether omp EXPANDS A VARIABLE IN A HEADER (the owner's `Bearer ${AGENTHUB_TOKEN}`), and (b) what omp supports PER AGENT — because fact 3 rules out per-seat delivery through one shared rig-root file. MEASURE BOTH; ASSUME NEITHER.**

**THE PLAN, in the owner's order:** **A. MEASURE FIRST (blocking everything else)** — extend, do not repeat, the probe above. **B. THE RENDERER** emits an omp MCP file into the render for an omp seat **that has `mcp` blocks**, and none for a seat with none — following the codex precedent. **C. PER-SEAT DELIVERY** — a per-seat `cwd`, or whatever omp supports per agent, **CHOSEN BY A rather than by preference**. **D. THE CLIENT** (`scripts/openrig_seat_sync.py rig`) installs it and **MERGES with the operator's deepseek entry — NEVER OVERWRITES IT**. **E. SEAT GUIDANCE** tells the seat to call **`manage_context`** and **`call_seat`**.

**ASSIGNMENT AND SERIALIZATION: go-dev2 owns A, B, C, E** — it owns the seat renderer, the catalog and the bridge surfaces, and it delivered the demand-2 research whose central finding is the same boundary ("only runtime `claude-code` receives the fragment"). **go-dev owns D**, the client merge, the half a renderer change cannot make safe alone. **The lead serializes**: `app.go`, the renderer and `scripts/openrig_seat_sync.py` are single-owner at a time; **the interface between the two seats — the rendered file's NAME AND SHAPE — is set by step A and stated to the lead by go-dev2 before D starts.** For go-dev2 the sequencing is: finish the in-flight D5 test fix (one test), then this; **the DDL divergence row yields to this.**

**ACCEPTANCE (the owner's, unchanged): in the live rig, a seat lists the `agenthub_http` tools and one call lands; a seat with no `mcp` block gets none; tests pin both.**

**THE ROOT CAUSE — FOUND AND VERIFIED BY THE OWNER (2026-10-06 17:53Z), recorded here as THE FINDING because everything above this line describes the SYMPTOM.** **`omp`'s `mcp.startupTimeoutMs` DEFAULTS TO 250 ms** — the setting's own words: *"wait this many ms for initial MCP tool discovery, 0 waits until connections settle."* **The local stdio `deepseek` server connects inside 250 ms; the HTTPS `agenthub_http` server does not — so a seat's first turn starts without it** and its device list shows only deepseek (`No such tool xd://mcp__agenthub_http_manage_project`). **AND THE SETTING CANNOT COME FROM THE ENVIRONMENT: the runner's env allowlist is DENY-BY-DEFAULT, so `MCP_STARTUP_TIMEOUT_MS` never reaches `omp` — it must live in the SEAT'S AGENT DIR.** **THE FIX THAT WORKS:** `omp config set mcp.startupTimeoutMs 0` with `HOME` and `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR` pointing at the seat state dir, which writes `agent/config.yml` — **the owner has written that setting into ALL TEN seat agent dirs under `~/.openrig/state/omp/<seat>/agent`.** **VERIFIED BY THE OWNER: the reviewer failed twice, then after the setting PLUS A RELAUNCH it made the call and returned the real project list; fe-dev, writer, web-dev and reviewer all return it.** **SO THE ACCEPTED BOUND SURVIVES AND IS NOW EXPLAINED — AND THE EXPLANATION HAS TWO HALVES ABOUT DIFFERENT THINGS (the reviewer's gate, 17:58Z): THE 250 ms DEFAULT *DELAYS* THE MOUNT RATHER THAN ALWAYS PREVENTING IT.** **WITHOUT THE SETTING the device is DELAYED — absent on the first turn and arriving once the connection settles — so it is a TIMING problem rather than a permanent one (which is why a retry sometimes worked and why an early report was still true at the time), and THE REVIEWER MEASURED EXACTLY THAT ON ITS OWN SEAT: THE DEVICE APPEARED BETWEEN TWO ATTEMPTS WITH NO RELAUNCH. WITH THE SETTING the runtime waits until connections settle, so the device is THERE FROM THE FIRST TURN — and THE RELAUNCH IS WHAT A RUNNING SEAT NEEDS IN ORDER TO PICK UP *THE SETTING*, not what makes the device appear.** **So "asynchronous" was the RIGHT SHAPE with the WRONG CLAIM: the device does arrive late on a session nobody relaunched, and what was wrong was "no relaunch was EVER needed".** **AND THE GATE'S PINNING IS STRICTER THAN "a test exists": the setting is pinned BY NAME (exactly two omp runtime files) and by VALUE BYTE-FOR-BYTE AS AN EQUALITY rather than a substring, so a reordered or reformatted file fails — and its author's bound is carried, that the INSTALL half and the end-to-end chain were NOT exercised there.** **THE CONSEQUENCE FOR THIS PACKET, which is the durable half: the omp render must deliver BOTH the MCP server entry AND the `config.yml` setting `mcp.startupTimeoutMs 0`, with A TEST THAT PINS THE SETTING — otherwise every new seat fails the same way.** **AND IT IS NOW DELIVERED ON BOTH SIDES, WITH THE SPLIT NAMED: the RENDER is go-dev2's `9b0e55ac` (`runtime/omp-config.yml`, appended at `renderer.go:207-208` only when the seat has a server to wait for, content at `:498`) and the CLIENT INSTALL is go-dev's `a7cf5807`, which installs it as the agent dir's `config.yml` BY MERGING THE ONE KEY rather than copying (`scripts/openrig_seat_sync.py:102-106`, `merge_config_key` at `:741` called at `:684`, `_merged_config` at `:730-731`; the author notes at `:748` that the runtime's own `omp config set` was measured to merge at exactly this level) — the merge mattering because that file is the RUNTIME's own (26 bytes, one key, against the render's 27), so a copy would drop whatever else it carries and differ from the runtime's write by one byte on every install.** **AND THE FALSIFICATION IS PINNED: `test_rig_sets_the_omp_startup_setting_without_clobbering_other_keys` writes a pre-existing config with an unrelated key, so a verbatim copy fails `KeyError: 'renderMarkdownResults'` while the other three tests pass.** **AND ONE THING NOT TO COLLAPSE WHEN CITING THE LIVE CALL: it proves the SETTING and the DISPATCH, not the DELIVERY — the seat that called had the setting because the OWNER wrote it into the agent dirs by hand, and the delivery claim rests on those tests.** **AND THE DELIVERY IS NOW GATED END TO END (`GATE-MCP-END-TO-END-CHAIN-2026-10-06.md`, APPROVE): the render from `resolved_seats.files`; the client's own functions installing both files with the merge NON-DESTRUCTIVE ON THE REAL PATH; the runtime reading it — `omp config get mcp.startupTimeoutMs` = 0 where installed against *250 where no `config.yml` exists*, the control, and that default is exactly the 250 ms this root cause names; and a scratch `omp` whose agent dir received ONLY the two installed files, NEVER `omp config set`, landing a real read-only call.** **Its boundary is carried as a boundary — not a runner-launched rig seat, `require_checker` the only stub, and the reviewer reproduced none of the links (the scratch trees are gone, so that evidence is the report's transcript).** **AND THE RELAUNCH PROCEDURE HAS A GAP WORTH RECORDING BESIDE IT: `rig launch` NEEDS THE RIG ID rather than the rig NAME — the name form answers `Rig not found` AFTER the kill, leaving the seat down for the whole retry window (about three minutes), which one seat named as A SILENT LOSS REPRODUCED BY OUR OWN HAND; the corrected form is `rig launch <RIG ID> <nodeRef>`.** **AND THE FIX IS CONFIRMED BY EXECUTION ON FIVE SEATS (the owner's four plus the lead's) rather than by inference: the lead's session, relaunched by the reviewer, called `manage_project {"action":"list"}` and got the three real projects.** **AND THE REASON THE MERGE MATTERS, MEASURED ON TWO SEATS: the live agent-dir `config.yml` is the RUNTIME's own file — 26 bytes, one key, present on this seat and on fe-dev's — against the render's 27.** *(Owner's security note, recorded with it: the entry sends the owner's FULL USER TOKEN to every yolo seat; a scoped per-seat token is the better end state.)*

**AND THE THREE FLAGS THAT MUST SURVIVE INTO ANY REPORT:** **(1) THE `mcp` MODULE KIND MAY BE INERT IN PRODUCTION until the owner's own DDL and enforcement window (decision 9) — so state whether production carries the six-value `ck_modules_kind` constraint BEFORE claiming anything works there, and never claim production behaviour from a local run.** **(2) No compatibility layer, one way only.** **(3) DO NOT CHANGE THE PACKET-4 GATE.** **Anything that would ship production behaviour waits on the owner's window and must be NAMED AS WAITING rather than assumed.**

Two demands from the owner, recorded with the facts supplied and their date. **THE SEQUENCE IS THE OWNER'S CONFIRMED DECISION OF 2026-10-06: RESOLVE THE PREMISES FIRST, THEN BUILD IMMEDIATELY ON THE ANSWER** — it is **NOT** a research-only instruction, and it is **NOT** something the owner asked for at the outset. The distinction is the principal session's own: **research was OUR framing, and the owner has since CONFIRMED it as the sequence** — the same correction as any other attribution that overstated its source. Routing: **fe-dev (demand 1)** and **go-dev2 (demand 2)**.

**DEMAND 1 — AUTOMATIC COMPACTION (standing behaviour).** A seat holding **more than 100k session tokens**, having **finished a task and reading idle**, should **send the compact command TO ITSELF with no human** — as standing behaviour, not an operator action. **THE MEASURED FACTS the owner supplied (quoted, 2026-10-06):** `rig ps --nodes --full` carries a **CTX column reading `??` for all ten omp seats**, and `context_usage` rows read **availability unknown, reason `parse_error`** — so **THE TRIGGER HAS NO DATA SOURCE TODAY**; and the runner advertises only **`/followup` and `/abort`**, so **it is unsettled whether omp honours any compact at all**. Consequence for the research: the demand's PRECONDITION is not observable from here and the MECHANISM it would trigger is unverified — two open questions, not two gaps to close by guessing. **OPERATIVE CONSEQUENCE (2026-10-06): THE DEMAND IS NOT RESEARCH-ONLY — once the two unknowns are resolved it is BUILT ON THE ANSWER, and the delivered research already suggests BOTH RESOLVE YES:** a real per-seat signal exists in the session journal, and omp honours compaction with the idle path implemented behind a setting that **DEFAULTS OFF**. So the build follows the confirmation rather than a further study.

**DEMAND 2 — the `claude-deepseek` route must carry deepseek-offload AND SURVIVE A RESTART.** Because a restart must not lose it, it has to live in **RENDERED SEAT CONFIG** rather than in a hand-started process. **THE MEASURED FACTS the owner supplied:** the deepseek MCP is attached to the **principal session** today, so the **bridge works**; the open question is the **SEAT half** — whether a seat's rendered configuration can carry it — and **where it persists** across a restart. The principal-session attachment does NOT answer the seat question, and nothing here assumes it does. **OPERATIVE CONSEQUENCE (2026-10-06): MAY START ITS BUILD — the research SETTLED the seat half, so the only remaining blocker is ONE DESIGN DECISION: how the declaration's TWO HOST-SPECIFIC PATHS are supplied, given that a block can carry exactly ONE platform-substitutable value. **AND THE DEMAND WAS WIDENED TO ALL SEATS (owner direction, 2026-10-06): every team session should be able to delegate to the DeepSeek Harness, NOT only the principal session and NOT only the `claude-deepseek` route** — folded here rather than opened as separate work, as the owner allowed. **AND THE BOUNDARY IT HITS, WITH ITS EVIDENCE — recorded here because the row is where a future reader looks, not only the note box (2026-10-06; go-dev2's derivation, re-read here at the source): THE RENDERER GATE IS ONE CONDITION — ONLY `claude-code` RECEIVES THE MCP FRAGMENT — AND ITS OWN COMMENT SAYS WHY:** `renderer.go:116` — *"Only claude-code seats get an MCP fragment: OpenRig has no codex/agy/omp MCP fragment resource"* — **so the fragment's PATH and its RESOURCE TYPE are BOTH CLAUDE-SPECIFIC.** **AND THE DECISIVE FACT IS ON THE CLIENT: the string `omp` appears NOWHERE in the installed daemon, exactly ONE adapter (`claude-code`) handles an MCP fragment, and the installed adapter set is `agy, codex, pi, stub, claude-code, terminal`** — so **widening the server EMITS BYTES NOTHING CONSUMES.** **WHY THE CHANNELS AN OMP SEAT HAS CANNOT CARRY IT:** an omp seat renders **`agent.yaml` plus guidance, and its `runtime_resources` slot is EMPTY in a real seat spec**; **skills and plugins ARE genuine rendered channels, but A SKILL CARRYING THE ATTACH INSTRUCTION IS RULED OUT because it MAKES ATTACHING A RUNTIME AN ACT OF THE AGENT — hand-attaching, which the owner rejected for not surviving a restart.** **THE ROUTE THAT EXISTS, VERIFIED ON A RUNNING SEAT: `omp` LOADS A PROJECT-ROOT `.mcp.json`** (its own setting reports this enabled), and **a seat's cwd IS the rig directory — which TODAY HAS NO SUCH FILE** (verified here: the rig directory has no `.mcp.json`, while the repo root has one), **which is why NO SEAT IN THIS RIG HAS PROJECT MCP SERVERS while the operator session, running from the repo, has several.** **THE THREE COSTED OPTIONS: (A) WIDEN THE RENDERER — HIGH and DUPLICATIVE**, needing a new adapter to consume a fragment nothing reads today; **(B) DECLARE IT IN THE RIG'S PROJECT-ROOT FILE — no server or client change, ONE FILE, covering EVERY omp seat whose cwd is that directory, and it DISSOLVES the host-path tension that blocked the shared-catalog route; (C) ACCEPT `claude-code` ONLY.** **AND THE PRECISION THE OWNER NEEDS TO SEE: (B) SURVIVES A RESTART — the property they cared about — BUT IT IS NOT RENDERED SEAT CONFIG, the mechanism they presumed:** the file sits on disk beside the rig, **is NOT reproduced by re-rendering**, and **an offline bundle would have to carry it the way it carries the policy file.** **TWO THINGS LEFT HONESTLY UNVERIFIED: whether that omp setting is the SHIPPED DEFAULT or was configured for this rig, and WHAT MATERIALIZES AN OMP SEAT beyond its YAML and guidance.** **THE ROW STAYS PARKED PENDING THE OWNER'S A, B OR C.****

## Owner directive (2026-10-06) — ONE CLIENT for the local-to-cloud bridge and sync, on Linux, WSL and Windows

**The owner's words:** keep ONE source code for the client or bridge, adapted to Linux, Windows and WSL, to run everywhere on every type of machine. **Decision: one Go binary `agenthub-client` in `agenthub_go/cmd/agenthubclient`**, replacing `scripts/openrig_bridge.py`, `openrig_scrub.py`, `openrig_seat_sync.py`, `openrig_seat_client.py` and `seat_feedback.sh`, and absorbing `cmd/seatcheck` as a subcommand. Clean break: no compatibility layer, no second copy once parity is shown.

**Platform fact that shapes it (OpenRig's own README):** OpenRig supports macOS and Linux only; native Windows is unsupported and WSL2 is untested upstream (it runs on WSL2 here). So: Linux and WSL2 get everything; native Windows reaches OpenRig through `wsl.exe -e rig ...` or runs cloud-only commands and says so. The client never shells out to `tmux`; it uses `rig` commands only. Per-OS rules (links, atomic writes, token in the OS keychain, services via systemd, launchd or Task Scheduler) and the build order are in the lead's `ONE-CLIENT.md`.

**Acceptance:** `agenthub-client sync status 4genthub-dev` gives the same answer on Linux, WSL2 and native Windows (three outputs attached); `bridge once --print` equals the Python bridge's output for the same `rig ps` input; a Windows machine without OpenRig exits non-zero with one clear message for `bridge`; no token appears in a log or process listing. Compiling for Windows without running it there is not proof.

## Owner directive (2026-10-06) — a DOCS PAGE in the frontend as the single source of truth between AI and humans

**The owner's words:** for ai_docs and the other docs, add a documentation page on the frontend that explains how the application works (API, MCP, everything), and make it the SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH for every future update between AI and humans. **Design: two tiers on one page.** (1) A REFERENCE tier generated from the code (every mounted route, every MCP tool) with a drift test, so it cannot go stale; (2) a GUIDE tier of versioned cloud `document` modules that a human edits in the page and an AI edits through a `manage_document` MCP tool, both writing the same row. **Existing:** `/docs` renders one hand-written `src/docs/api-reference.en.md`; `ai_docs/` holds 71 files. **Two owner decisions are flagged, not assumed:** where the guides live (recommended: the cloud) and whether `ai_docs/` and its hooks are retired once imported (a change to the owner's own rules; only on the owner's word). Build order, rules and acceptance are in the lead's `DOCS-PAGE.md`; step 1 (the generated reference and its drift test) needs no decision.

## Rig capacity (2026-10-05)

The rig grew from four seats to **seven**. Original four: `lead`, `go-dev` (backend), `fe-dev` (frontend), `reviewer`. Added: `web-dev` (second frontend seat), `writer` (documentation), `go-dev2` (second backend). Both rig spec files (`rig-omp.yaml`, `rig-claude.yaml`) declare all seven, so a restore or the Claude switch-back keeps them.

- **Backend:** the two backend seats must not touch `fastmcp/server/httpapp/routes_mount.go`, `fastmcp/server/httpapp/ws_mount.go` or `fastmcp/task_management/infrastructure/database/models.go` without the lead serializing it with `go-dev`; `go-dev2`'s single `fastmcp/server/httpapp/app.go` line (`mountTeamRoutes`) is claimed with `go-dev`'s agreement.

Current assignments: `web-dev` C3 (dashboard sessions page) then F6 (topology graph); `writer` the residual documentation truth-audit (directive B continuation, docs-only); `go-dev2` D5 teams slice 1 (self-contained teams/members/roles, with the surface-inventory update riding the same commit); `go-dev` the board ticks and G3; `fe-dev` directive C closed and available.

(1) ROUTING (the owner's probe, relayed): an unauthenticated upgrade to `/ws/realtime` through CapRover reaches the Go app and returns 403 `Authentication required` with `Server: nginx`, so the app (not the proxy) refuses it. (2) AUTHENTICATED UPGRADE (go-dev, 2026-10-05): `wss://api.4genthub.com/ws/realtime` with `Authorization: Bearer <AGENTHUB_TOKEN, read from the rig daemon's /proc environment>` returned status `101` with headers `Server: nginx`, `Upgrade: websocket`, `Connection: upgrade`, and the FIRST FRAME was the v2 welcome — `type: sync`, `payload.entity: connection`, `action: welcome`, `data.primary.authenticated: true`, `scope: branch`. (3) LONG TIMEOUT (go-dev): the same authenticated socket held IDLE for 90 seconds stayed OPEN with no close and no extra frames, so `proxy_read_timeout` does not cut a quiet realtime connection. Probe scripts kept at `/tmp/ws_prod_probe.py` (upgrade + first frame) and `/tmp/ws_prod_hold.py` (90s idle hold); both read the token from the daemon's environment and print presence/length only. After the reviewer's hardening note, `ws_prod_probe.py` also REFUSES to print any header or frame whose text contains the token, so "never prints a secret" is structural rather than dependent on what the server sends; re-run after the guard: the same 101 and the same v2 welcome frame.

The D5 statements are all idempotent and purely additive, no CASCADE: `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS teams` (`infrastructure/schema/seat_management_postgresql.sql:258`), `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS team_members` (:272), `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS ix_teams_user_id` (:268), `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS uq_team_members_one_owner … WHERE role = 'owner'` (:287), so the first boot after the push creates them and the flag can stay on.
