Update 2026-10-04 (tester, scratch only; the writer did not run these; evidence file `~/.openrig/agenthub-seats/4genthub-dev/handover/f4-evidence-tester.md`, sections 1 to 7): a scratch Postgres 16.4, the Go server built from main (health 0.0.12) and a scratch OpenRig daemon; the live rig, daemon and seat store were not touched. Verified: (1) a 3-seat rig from the cloud (seats `zulu`, `mike`, `alpha`; links `zulu delegates_to mike`, `mike delegates_to alpha`) passed `rig spec validate` and `rig spec preflight` and came up in edge order, zulu then mike then alpha (daemon `node.launched` events, tmux session creation times and startup_ready agree; alphabetical order would have been alpha, mike, zulu). (2) The bridge `once` with a machine token reported the 3 seats; `GET /api/v2/openrig/machines` showed each `hash` equal to its `expected_hash` and to the pin, `sync: in_sync`. Right after launch the seats read `unknown`, not `idle`, because OpenRig gave no activity state until activity arrived. (3) A seat stopped with `rig seat stop` showed `stopped` in the API. (4) Relaunch of a stopped seat passed: after `rig seat stop`, `rig seat launch <seat> --fresh` returned ready with a new session id and generation, and the bridge no longer reported `stopped` (`unknown`, then `idle` on a later run). (5) Recovery of a seat whose claude process was killed: `rig seat clean` is refused (the tmux session is still alive, and clean works only on dead seats); `rig seat launch <seat> --fresh --stop --reason ...` returned ready with a new claude process, and the bridge reported `unknown` instead of `blocked`. (6) `seatcheck` allow and deny, run with the exact environment of the live scratch seat's claude process (read from `/proc`, not printed), not typed into the claude prompt: a seat with no link was denied for every recipient (exit 3, audit lines with `Allowed:false`, nothing delivered; an unknown recipient also printed the hint); a seat with a `delegates_to` link delivered to its peer (exit 0, audit decision line plus `Outcome:delivered`, text visible in the peer's pane) and was denied for an unlinked seat (exit 3, audited). Isolation caveat: `seatcheck` reads `$HOME/.openrig/agenthub-seats` by design and scratch seats inherit the real `HOME`, so a scratch run must point `HOME` at the scratch store (the tester used `HOME=/tmp/f4/fakehome` with `.openrig/agenthub-seats` linked to the scratch seat store); otherwise it reads the live store. Open, needs an owner decision: any death outside `rig seat stop` reads `blocked` in the cloud, not `stopped`. Both a `tmux kill-session` and a `kill -9` of only the claude process (the tmux session stayed alive) left OpenRig at lifecycle `attention_required` for 2 minutes of polling (8 polls), and the bridge reported `blocked` (mapping `attention_required` to `blocked` in `scripts/openrig_bridge.py`). The check wording "a killed seat shows as stopped" therefore holds only for the sanctioned stop. Either accept `blocked` for non-sanctioned deaths and reword the F4 check, or change the bridge. Not run: the Claude-level permission deny of `rig send` typed into a seat prompt (the seat's Claude permission rules were not exercised), herdr agents, codex or agy rigs, `rig up` from a bundle, production. Update 2026-10-04 (go-dev, F4 death reproduction; scratch rig `f4death`, rig `01M44F89ZY64NTYFYJAH09SMCC`, torn down after, no other rig touched): the blocked-versus-stopped defect is FIXED in `scripts/openrig_bridge.py:seat_state`. Reproduced: bring a one-seat scratch rig up, kill only the agent process (`kill -9` of the pane's grandchild `agy`; the tmux session stayed alive) — `rig ps --json --nodes -A` then reports `sessionStatus: running`, `lifecycleState: attention_required`, `agentActivity.state: unknown`, reason `no_runtime_hook`, and it does NOT converge (unchanged after 25s more). The bridge then reported `state: blocked`, from the old `life == "attention_required"` clause — so the state was decided by OUR mapping (line 127 of `seat_state`), not by OpenRig, which only supplies the raw fields. Fix: `attention_required` alone is no longer a blocked signal; blocked comes from `agentActivity.state == needs_input` or `startupStatus attention_required|failed`, so the dead agent now reads `unknown` (verified live: `{"room":"f4death","seat":"alpha","state":"unknown","detail":"no_runtime_hook"}`) while the healthy control seats were unchanged (`idle`/`running`). `rig seat stop f4death.alpha --reason …` still reads `stopped` (`sessionStatus: exited`, `lifecycleState: recoverable`), so the tester's item 3 is unaffected. Tests: three cases in `src/tests/scripts/test_openrig_bridge.py` (the captured death node -> `unknown`, `attention_required` alone -> `unknown`, `attention_required` + `needs_input` -> `blocked`); scripts suite 172 passed. The third clause is therefore RESTATED: a death outside `rig seat stop` reads **unknown** in the cloud — not `blocked` and not `stopped` — because OpenRig exposes no distinct "agent gone" state (the tmux session is alive and `rig ps` still calls the node running), so `unknown` is the honest reading and the detail `no_runtime_hook` names why; reporting `stopped` would assert more than OpenRig tells us. The clause was a two-sided bug: the same `attention_required` test also mislabelled healthy busy seats — a live seat with `agentActivity.state: running` and `lifecycleState: attention_required` read `blocked` at the reproduction's baseline — so the fix removes both the death mislabel and the busy-seat mislabel. Note that `unknown` is also the cold-start reading (the tester's evidence above records that right after launch seats read `unknown`, not `idle`, because OpenRig gives no activity state until activity arrives), so `unknown` does NOT by itself distinguish a cold start from an agent that is gone; the distinguishing detail we do have is `no_runtime_hook` in the seat detail. Lead ruling 2026-10-04: keep `unknown` (the tmux session is alive and `rig ps` still calls the node running, so `stopped` would assert more than OpenRig tells us). F4's residual cases, each now stated for what it is rather than left as a blanket "not run": (i) the Claude-level `rig send` deny typed into a seat prompt — still unrun and PARKED on the account's Claude weekly cap (resets Oct 6 14:00 Europe/Paris); what is proven at rest is recorded above. (ii) herdr agents — ENVIRONMENT-ABSENT here, not unproven by omission: `herdr api snapshot` returns `{"id":"cli:api:snapshot","error":{"code":"server_not_running","message":"no herdr server is running at …herdr.sock"}}` (the binary exists at `/home/daihu/.local/bin/herdr`), and the bridge already treats an unavailable herdr as an absent source and says so once per change, so this cannot be exercised on this host without an herdr server. (iii) codex or agy rigs — COVERED BY OWNER DECISION, not unrun: codex and agy receive no Claude settings fragment (`receivesClaudeFragments` is claude-code only), so they have no deny path, and G3(b) then refuses allowing links for them (commit `c0987c29`); the owner chose L2 and that restriction, so there is nothing left to run here beyond what G3 records. (iv) `rig up` from a bundle — PROVEN 2026-10-05 on a scratch stack: the `f4off` bundle (alpha=developer, beta=reviewer, built from the fixed rig root) was materialized with `rig up <bundle> --target <dir>` and both seats came up, with the outcome stated per seat rather than as `ready`/`failed`: alpha ran `startupStatus ready` with `agentActivity idle`, and beta ran `startupStatus failed` with `agentActivity {unknown, no_runtime_hook}` — its agent never started, which is the credential/cap limit below and not a bundle-materialization failure — the bundle's `rig.yaml` gives both seats `runtime: claude-code`, so the Claude credential/account cap, not the bundle, is what stopped beta. The two limits a bundle still carries are stated with it: `tar tzf … | grep -ci seatcheck` -> 0, so an offline host needs the checker installed separately, and the launched agent had no model credential (the OF3 finding; the claude seats here also hit the account cap). (v) production — owner. Update 2026-10-05 (go-dev, typed-deny attempt; local scratch only): a real claude-code seat WAS built from the real render (local Go server on the throwaway Postgres, seats `alpha`/`beta` in room `f4typed`, pulled with the sync client's own writer into a scratch store) and the guard IS applied to it: the rendered `runtime/claude-settings.fragment.json` (`allow = Bash(seatcheck send:*), Bash(rig whoami:*)`; `deny = Bash(rig send:*), Bash(rig queue:*), Bash(rig broadcast:*), Bash(tmux send-keys:*), Bash(tmux paste-buffer:*)`) appears verbatim in the seat's `/tmp/f4typed/rig/.claude/settings.local.json`, alongside `disabledMcpjsonServers: [agenthub_http]` (that key is the seat's own Claude runtime state, not render output — see the G4 correction of 2026-10-05). OBSERVED (not proof): the deny rules did NOT stop a command the human typed with `!` — `!rig send f4typed-nonexistent@f4typed "guard test"` (a deny-listed command) EXECUTED and returned exactly the control's output (`Session 'f4typed-nonexistent@f4typed' not found`), as did the allow-listed `!rig whoami`. This does not separate "the `!` path consults no permission layer" from "the deny rule did not match that command form on the `!` path": the allow half is non-discriminating (an allow-listed command executes under either hypothesis) and the deny half shows only that the rule did not stop it. Discriminators that would settle it: a model Bash tool call with the same command (blocked by the weekly cap until Oct 6) or a `!`-typed command that would otherwise be prompted for. Either way, a refusal on the `!` path would not be evidence the deny rules work, and this non-refusal is not evidence they fail; the deny must be exercised through a real Bash tool call, which needs the model. ALLOW PATH VERIFIED without a model call: as the seat (`OPENRIG_SESSION_NAME=f4typed-alpha@f4typed`, `HOME` at the scratch store), `seatcheck send --to beta --intent task -- "allow-path test from alpha"` printed `Sent to f4typed-beta@f4typed` (exit 0), the text arrived in beta's pane (`From: f4typed-alpha@f4typed`), and `audit.jsonl` recorded `Allowed:true, Reason:"allowed"` then `Outcome:"delivered"`; the same call with `--intent notice` was `Allowed:false, Reason:"no link"` (the alpha→beta link is `delegates_to`, which permits `task`, not `notice`). BLOCKER (verbatim, still open): the seat's Claude reports `You've hit your weekly limit · resets Oct 6, 2pm (Europe/Paris)`, so it will not answer a prompt and no Bash tool call can be attempted; the runtime refusal of a direct `rig send` therefore remains UNPROVEN — what is proven today is the rendered list, its application to the seat, the `!`-path finding and the allow path, not the tool-call refusal. OWNER DECISION 2026-10-05 (supersedes the earlier `unknown` ruling, which was the lead's reading): a seat whose agent dies outside `rig seat stop` reads **stopped** and is **respapped**. Implemented: `scripts/openrig_bridge.py:seat_state` maps a live session whose `agentActivity.state` is `unknown` with reason `no_runtime_hook` to `stopped` (it was `unknown`); `attention_required` alone still never means blocked, and a live busy seat still reads `running`. LIVE PROOF (scratch rig `f4kill`, one agy seat, torn down after; live rigs untouched): `kill -9` of the pane's `agy` leaves `rig ps --json` at `sessionStatus running`, `lifecycleState attention_required`, `agentActivity {unknown, no_runtime_hook}`, and the bridge reports `state: stopped` for it — the same fields read `unknown` before this change. COLD-START OVERLAP, measured and stated with its runtime dependence (the doc and the code comment now agree; the reviewer flagged the earlier mismatch): the reason field is the only discriminator, and what a just-launched seat reports depends on its runtime. Measured 2026-10-05 on the scratch rig: a just-launched AGY seat reports the SAME reading as a dead one — `unknown` + `no_runtime_hook` — until its runtime hook attaches (observed ~15s), so it reads `stopped` in that window. The live inventory shows an OMP seat instead reporting `null` while it starts (e.g. `4genthub-deepseek.supervisor`: session running, lifecycle running, activity `unknown`, reason `null`), which stays `unknown`. So `stopped` does cover a just-started seat for that window on runtimes that behave like agy, and nothing may act on a single sample — the `respawn` hold (default 30s) is sized against that window and must not be shortened without re-measuring per runtime. RESPAWN PATH: OpenRig has no automatic trigger (no respawn/auto-restart logic in its installed CLI: `grep -rn "respawn|autoRestart|restartPolicy" @openrig/cli/dist` is empty); it owns only the deliberate primitive `rig seat launch <seat> [--fresh] [--stop] --reason <text>` (`@openrig/cli/dist/commands/seat.js:419`), which creates a blank native occupant with a new session id and generation. We call it from `scripts/openrig_seat_sync.py respawn <room> <seat> [--after-seconds N] [--reason …]` (`cmd_respawn`): it re-reads the node every 5s and refuses unless the dead reading holds for the whole hold (default 30s) — that is what keeps it off a merely-starting seat — then runs the launch and surfaces OpenRig's own words when it exits non-zero (a launch can start the occupant and still exit 1 with `Fresh occupant started but runtime identity requires attention: tmux pane … foreground command 'sh' contradicts runtime 'agy'`). LIVE RESPAWN PROOF: after killing agy on the scratch seat, `respawn f4kill alpha --after-seconds 10 --reason …` printed that notice and the seat came back (`agentActivity {running, window_activity_motion}`). Tests: bridge 41 passed, seat-sync 86 passed (four new respawn cases).

(a) RENDERED AND SPEC-VALID, with the validator named because it is the whole of (a): the artefact is validated by `rig agent validate` — that is the validator available for a spec that carries this file — and it runs in the test suite behind `OPENRIG_TEST_AGENT_VALIDATE=1`: `OPENRIG_TEST_AGENT_VALIDATE=1 go test -count=1 -run TestRenderSeatRigValidate ./fastmcp/seat_management/domain/seatrenderer/` -> PASS for claude-code, codex and omp with `Agent spec valid` (the test now renders the seat with the seed library's real modules, so the new file is covered); the codex-side validator `codex execpolicy check` cannot run here because codex is not installed, and that is (c), not an omission. A codex seat whose modules carry a `tool` module renders `runtime/codex.rules` through the normal `RenderSeat` path; `gofmt -l` clean, `go vet ./fastmcp/seat_management/domain/seatrenderer/` clean, `go test -count=1 ./fastmcp/seat_management/domain/seatrenderer/` ok. The file carries one `prefix_rule(..., decision = "forbidden")` per `Bash(...)` deny entry the Claude fragment carries (`rig send`, `rig queue`, `rig broadcast`, `tmux send-keys`, `tmux paste-buffer`), deny-only (no `allow` rule, which would widen execution outside the sandbox), each justification naming `seatcheck send` as the audited alternative; an entry that is not a `Bash(...)` prefix is listed in a comment rather than dropped. (b) MECHANISM CITED, RE-CHECKED not inherited: the earlier codex facts were worker-reported, so they were verified against the vendor documentation — `developers.openai.com/codex/exec-policy`, which today redirects to <https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/agent-configuration/rules> (the page actually read; "Rules": a `.rules` file in a `rules/` folder of an active config layer, `prefix_rule(pattern, decision, justification, match, not_match)`, most restrictive decision wins `forbidden` > `prompt` > `allow`, format Starlark, feature experimental, validator `codex execpolicy check --rules <file> -- <command>`). The config reference confirms a deny prefix rule cannot be expressed in `config.toml` (its controls are `approval_policy` and `sandbox_mode`), and its `auto_review.ignore_rules` entry states that the model slugs it names ignore `allow` prefix rules in command execution policy while `deny` and network rules still apply (from the same config reference, read 2026-10-05). (c) RUNTIME REFUSAL UNVERIFIED: codex is not installed on this host, so no refusal was observed and this box must not imply one. Operator procedure to close it: install codex, place the file in a `rules/` folder of an active config layer, run `codex execpolicy check --rules <file> -- rig send <args>` and require the strictest decision to be `forbidden`, then try the command in a seat. THE GAP, stated rather than implied: OpenRig has no codex rules resource type — its only codex runtime-artefact type is `codex_config_fragment`, which merges TOML into `~/.codex/config.toml` (`packages/daemon/src/adapters/codex-runtime-adapter.ts:640`, `applyRuntimeResource`) — so the renderer emits the file as a plain spec file and the client must install it (a trusted project's `<rig root>/.codex/rules/<seat>.rules`, or `~/.codex/rules/`); until that install step exists the artefact is the seat's pinned policy, not enforcement. G3(b)'s API restriction on allowing links for codex therefore STAYS until the refusal is verified per (a)-(c). ONE ASYMMETRY between the runtimes, recorded rather than left implicit: this artefact is DENY-ONLY, so on codex the audited path is NOT pre-approved — `seatcheck send` may prompt, or be refused by the seat's `approval_policy`/`sandbox_mode` — where the claude-code fragment explicitly allows it; that is the conservative side of the trade (mirroring the allow rules would widen execution outside the sandbox without prompting), not a defect, and a reader comparing the two runtimes should expect it.
- [x] G4 (explicit offline mode = OpenRig bundles, owner decision 2026-10-03): after every successful sync the client builds a bundle with `rig bundle create <rig.yaml> --rig-root <dir> -o <bundles dir>/<rig>-<resolved sha8>.rigbundle --notes "resolved_sha256=<cloud hash>"`. Offline launch is `rig up <bundle> --target <dir>`, chosen explicitly by the operator; `rig bundle inspect` verifies the digest and integrity; `rig bundle history` (which reads `~/.openrig/bundle-audit.jsonl`) is the audit for `bundle install` — it does not record `rig up <bundle>`, see the 2026-10-04 update below. No custom cache code and nothing is picked automatically: when the cloud is unreachable the sync fails loudly and may print the newest bundle path as a hint (a hint, not a fallback). Verified 2026-10-03 on the synced specs: `rig bundle create` works with `path:` refs and vendors the agents; `rig bundle inspect` reports `Integrity: PASS`; `rig up <bundle> --plan --target <dir>` reports planned with preflight ok and leaves the target untouched (a real launch from a bundle was NOT run). Finding: the archive hash is NOT a content identity (only `created_at` in `bundle.yaml` differs between two builds of identical inputs, contradicting the doc's determinism claim), so the pin is the cloud's resolved-seat hash, carried in `--notes`; `bundle.yaml` also records the client's absolute `original_ref` paths. Both open checks this box used to carry are ANSWERED (2026-10-05): (1) how the local enforcement point (G3) reads its pinned policy from inside a bundle — the bundle carried the Claude deny/allow fragment but NOT `policy.json`, so `seatcheck` could not decide or audit offline; that is now FIXED and proven offline in the update below (`materialize_agent` puts the policy in the agent directory the bundle packages, and `offline-install` writes it to the store path `seatcheck` resolves); (2) seats cannot reach the `agenthub_http` tools while offline — RENDER-VERIFIED: the materialized `.mcp.json` (the render's `runtime/claude-mcp.fragment.json`) declares that server at the pull-time `AGENTHUB_URL` with an `${AGENTHUB_TOKEN}` placeholder, so the URL is baked at pull time and could not work offline. CORRECTED 2026-10-05 (the reviewer found the original clause had no source; re-verified by go-dev): this sentence used to add "while the seat's own settings carry `disabledMcpjsonServers: ["agenthub_http"]`", which stated as a RENDER property something the render does not produce. Nothing in this repo writes `.claude/settings.local.json` (`grep -rn "settings\.local\.json" --include=*.py --include=*.go .` -> 0 hits); the rendered fragments carry only `permissions` and an `mcpServers` declaration, with no enable/disable key; and the installed `@openrig/cli` never mentions `McpjsonServers` at all (`grep -rn McpjsonServers <npm root -g>/@openrig/cli/dist` -> 0), so it cannot write it either — its `commands/setup.js` writes `settings.local.json` with `statusLine` plus the acceptEdits floor fragment and states outright that "OpenRig bakes NO allow/ask/deny permission policy — the harness-native permissions are the control surface". The key belongs to Claude Code's own settings (the workspace's own `.claude/settings.local.json` carries its sibling `enabledMcpjsonServers` with `agenthub_http` listed enabled), and it is per-session state rather than a property of any input: across six materializations four carry `disabledMcpjsonServers: ["agenthub_http"]` and two do not, and the decisive case is the reviewer's controlled pair — the SAME bundle materialized with the SAME command into two clean targets a minute apart produced OPPOSITE outcomes (run A carried the key, run B did not and still did not when its settings file was rewritten). `rig up` returns after it has launched the seats, so that pair cannot by itself exclude a settings write inside that window; it does not need to, because identical inputs cannot produce opposite outcomes if the key were a render, bundle or command property — which is precisely why the clause cannot stand as one. So the offline conclusion here rests on the pull-time URL, which IS render-verified; the observation that a running seat's Claude had that server disabled is a RUNTIME observation and must not be cited as a render property (recorded in the same update). Check: with the cloud unreachable and no flag the sync fails loudly; `rig up <bundle>` brings the rig up from the pinned versions. Update 2026-10-04 (OF3, go-dev): the real launch WAS run. A bundle was built from the synced `4genthub-deepseek` spec (one `omp` member, vendored `agents/supervisor`) with the rig renamed `of3-bundle-test`; `rig bundle inspect` reports `Digest valid: true` / `Integrity: PASS` (digest `d6cced37…`); `rig up of3-bundle-test.rigbundle --target /tmp/of3/target` completed and materialized `rig.yaml`, `agents/supervisor/`, `bundle.yaml` (carrying the provenance note) and the managed `AGENTS.md`; `rig ps` showed `of3-bundle-test` 1 node, session `running`, startup `ready`, runtime `omp`, model `deepseek/deepseek-flash`; the note `resolved_sha256=64bc3d67…` (go-dev's pinned hash from the 4genthub-dev store) is in `bundle.yaml` provenance. Tore down with `rig down of3-bundle-test` (1 session killed, snapshot `01M448E658DTWX4ZKRR4BR24A7`); `4genthub-min` and `4genthub-deepseek` were untouched. Two findings from the real run: (1) the launched seat's agent failed with `[omp-runner] ERROR rpc: No API key found for deepseek` — a bundle carries no model credential and the seat's state HOME has none, so an offline bundle launch brings the node up but the agent cannot authenticate (`agentActivity` = `needs_input` / `runtime_error`); (2) `rig up <bundle>` does not append to `~/.openrig/bundle-audit.jsonl` (mtime unchanged, last row 2026-10-02), only `bundle install` writes it, so `rig bundle history` is the audit for `bundle install`, not for the `up` path. Also: `rig down` leaves the rig stopped with a snapshot rather than removing it. Update 2026-10-05 (go-dev, bundle-policy checks; local scratch only): the first open check now has a definite answer. Built a real bundle from the pinned scratch snapshot (`openrig_seat_sync.py bundle f4typed alpha --rig-yaml <rig.yaml> --rig-root <dir> --out-dir <dir>`, `HOME` pointed at the scratch store) -> `f4typed-alpha-4f25a783.rigbundle` (gzip, 3229 bytes) containing `rig.yaml`, `bundle.yaml` (provenance `notes: resolved_sha256=4f25a783…`) and `agents/developer/{agent.yaml, guidance/role.md, runtime/claude-mcp.fragment.json, runtime/claude-settings.fragment.json, skills/comm-guard-skill/SKILL.md}`. It does NOT contain the seat's `policy.json`/`pinned.json` (`tar tzf … | grep -c policy.json` -> 0), and after `rig up <bundle> --target <dir>` neither exists anywhere in the materialized rig (`find … -name policy.json -o -name pinned.json` -> empty). Consequence, measured: in that materialized seat `seatcheck send` fails with `seatcheck send: read policy: open <HOME>/.openrig/agenthub-seats/…/policy.json: no such file or directory`. So the Claude-side deny/allow fragment DOES travel with the bundle (the direct-send denies are enforceable offline), but the ALLOWED path cannot decide or audit offline — `seatcheck` needs the seat's pinned policy, which nothing in the bundle supplies. Two related observations: the bundle maps BOTH seats to the same `agents/developer` dir (per-seat identity/policy is not represented at all), and the materialized `.mcp.json` declares `agenthub_http` at the pull-time `AGENTHUB_URL` (`http://127.0.0.1:8000/mcp`) with an `${AGENTHUB_TOKEN}` placeholder while the seat's settings carry `disabledMcpjsonServers: ["agenthub_http"]` — so the second open check resolves to: the seat does not reach those tools at all, offline or online, in the current render, and the URL is baked at pull time so it could not work offline anyway. Open if a bundle is meant to be self-sufficient offline: it must carry the seat's pinned policy (and per-seat dirs), otherwise the enforcement point degrades to the fragment's deny list only. FIXED 2026-10-05 (go-dev, G4 row qitem-20261005045513): the loss is in `scripts/openrig_seat_sync.py` — `cmd_bundle` never placed the seat's policy into the rig root it bundles (the store's `policy.json` is written by `pull_seat`), and that rig root's `agents/<seat>` was a symlink to the pinned snapshot, which holds the rendered files only. A probe confirmed an extra file inside the agent directory DOES travel into a bundle (`agents/developer/policy.json` and `pinned.json` appear in `tar tzf`), so the fix is `materialize_agent()`: the agent directory is materialized as a copy of the snapshot plus the seat's `policy.json`/`pinned.json`, and `cmd_rig` uses it (its only caller, next to `cmd_bundle`). Because `seatcheck` resolves `<HOME>/.openrig/agenthub-seats/<rig>/<member>/policy.json` by design (not a flag), the client gained `openrig_seat_sync.py offline-install <target> [--home <home>]`, which copies the bundled policy into that store path after the operator materializes a bundle. OFFLINE END-TO-END PROOF (scratch only; the local server was stopped, port 8000 closed, no network): room `f4off` (alpha=developer, beta=reviewer, alpha `delegates_to` beta); the bundle built from the fixed rig root carries `agents/developer/policy.json` (Seat alpha) and `agents/reviewer/policy.json` (Seat beta); `rig up <bundle> --target` materialized and launched both seats; `offline-install` wrote both store entries; then as alpha, `seatcheck send --to beta --intent task` -> `Sent to f4off-beta@f4off` exit 0, the text arrived in beta's pane, and the offline `audit.jsonl` recorded `Allowed:true, Reason:"allowed"` then `Outcome:"delivered"`; `--intent notice` -> `denied: no link` exit 3 with `Allowed:false, Reason:"no link"` in the same file. Tests: `test_openrig_seat_sync.py` 82 passed (four rig tests moved from the symlink contract to the materialized one, plus two new offline-install tests: it writes the store, and it SKIPS a policy that names another seat); scripts suite 174 passed. Named, not hidden: members that SHARE a seat type share one agent directory in a bundle, so only one of their policies can ride there — `offline-install` refuses a policy naming another seat rather than silently installing the wrong links, and per-seat policy in one bundle would need distinct seat types or an upstream bundle change; and the bundle carries no `seatcheck` binary, so an offline machine still needs the checker installed separately — with no `seatcheck` on `PATH` the seat cannot send at all (the design has the client build it outside the repo and link it, `install-checker`), so an offline host needs that binary before the allowed path works. **CLOSED ON EVIDENCE 2026-10-06 (go-dev2's own verification after a read-only subagent's first pass; NEITHER BOX NEEDED A CHANGE).** **(i) THE ENFORCEMENT POINT READS THE PINNED POLICY FROM INSIDE A BUNDLE — VERIFIED, each link of the chain checked rather than one end:** the sync writes `policy.json` beside the pinned snapshot, `materialize` copies it to the rig root, and the offline install puts it at the seat's home path; **THE BUNDLE BINDS IT BY CONTENT** — the extracted `policy.json`'s sha256 equals the hash `bundle.yaml` declares — and `seatcheck` reads `<pins>/<rig>/<member>/policy.json` from a deliberately **non-flag default directory**, parses it, checks that the policy's seat IS the caller, decides, **exits 3 on denial** and writes an audit line carrying the **PolicyHash**. **AND IT WAS EXERCISED OFFLINE WITH A FAILING `curl` CONTROL:** an escalation DENIED, a cross-seat message denied **with the seat-key hint**, and — **the control the investigator omitted and go-dev2 added — A TASK ALLOWED with `Allowed:true`, which is what proves the policy is APPLIED rather than everything denied.** **(ii) SEATS CANNOT REACH THE `agenthub_http` TOOLS WHILE OFFLINE — VERIFIED, and shown as REFUSALS rather than as the absence of a success:** after a `SIGKILL` the second and third tool calls fail **ECONNREFUSED**, `listTools` fails, there is **no cached tool list and no retry into success**, and **no allow-list entry outlives the network** — the rendered settings allow only the send guard and the seat-identity command, and **NO `mcp` grant appears in any fragment**. **THE ONE INCH, NAMED FOR THE BOX RATHER THAN LEFT IMPLIED:** **a seat that is BUNDLED AND RUNNING, so that seat identity answers natively from the local daemon while the seat's outward path is cut** — that is what would close (i) completely, and it is **OWNER-SIDE**. The other stated limits are (ii)'s: **the `claude-code` MCP client could not be driven here because its native binary is missing**, so the demonstration used the **official SDK client** (same protocol family, different implementation), and **any proxy in front of the API is untested**. **AND THE METHOD POINT THAT MAKES (i) TRUSTWORTHY, which is the reusable part: A SUITE OF DENIALS PROVES NOTHING ABOUT WHETHER A POLICY IS READ** — everything-denied and policy-applied produce **the same exits** — **the ALLOWED case is the control that distinguishes them, and it was absent from the first pass.**

Confirmed by the owner 2026-10-03; the offline bundle (G4) is the pinned artifact. Implemented in the seat API (a new seat pins the current latest version unless `follow_latest` is true) and in the client (`scripts/openrig_seat_sync.py` keeps a lock and never moves it without `--update`). The company-level opt-in is the `seat_settings.follow_latest` flag (`GET|PUT /api/v2/openrig/settings`). Run scripts/openrig_bridge.py on your PC."). `/seats/of4room/alpha` rendered the seat header (`Room of4room · developer · pinned 1.1.1 · omp / deepseek/deepseek-flash`), the Modules tab with the effective modules (`developer-role`, the four developer rules, `developer-output-format`, `comm-guard`, `comm-guard-skill`, all `@1.1.1`) and the overlay editor; the Links, Preview (hash `0330a8e3…`, runtime, `scripts/openrig_seat_sync.py pull of4room alpha`, Files (3) with the agent.yaml body and the Policy JSON), Permissions (locked/standard/open/yolo/none) and LLM (claude-code/codex/agy/omp + model) tabs all rendered. Two blockers found while setting it up, both real: (1) CORS in a TOKEN-LESS stack — the middleware echoes the origin only when the request carries a Cookie (`config/cors_factory.go:177-185`, Starlette's `allow_all_origins` + `has_cookie`): with no Cookie it sends `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` together with `Access-Control-Allow-Credentials: true`, which a browser rejects for a `credentials: 'include'` fetch (`apiV2.ts`). This run had no cookie (AUTH_ENABLED=false, the token injected as a request header), so every seats panel showed `Failed to fetch` until `CORS_ORIGINS=http://localhost:3800` was set. A real logged-in dashboard session sends the `access_token` cookie and the middleware then echoes the request origin (reviewer probe 2026-10-04: no Cookie -> `*`, with `Cookie: access_token=x` -> the origin), so the default works for it; only a cross-site/no-cookie setup needs `CORS_ORIGINS` set explicitly. Both branches were pinned by `TestWithCORSSimpleRequestDefaultWildcardWithoutCookie` and `...WithCookie`, and those two are now SUPERSEDED: `ff5f10d6` DELETED the first — it pinned behaviour that was never true of the design (a wildcard beside `Access-Control-Allow-Credentials`) — and replaced the pair with `TestWithCORSSimpleRequestDefaultWildcardEchoesOrigin` and `TestWithCORSSimpleRequestDisallowedOrigin`; see rule 29 for why a wrong-behaviour test is deleted rather than adjusted. (2) `VITE_DISABLE_AUTH=true` bypasses only the route guard (`ProtectedRoute.tsx`), not the API — the seats pages still send `Cookies.get('access_token')`, so with no token every panel shows `Not authenticated`; a browser run needs a real token injected at the browser level. G6 stays `[ ]` (its own check — launch through the F4 client — was not run here). Update 2026-10-05 (go-dev, G6 launch half + page-vs-API consistency; local stack, done after the earlier capture was resumed): the check is now MET. On a local stack (my Go server on :8001 over the throwaway Postgres with `CORS_ORIGINS` matching the dev server, `vite` on :3900 with `VITE_DISABLE_AUTH=true` and `VITE_API_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8001` — :8000 and :3800 were busy with another seat's processes, which I did not touch), the seat `g6room/alpha` (developer, pinned 1.1.1, `omp / deepseek/deepseek-flash`) was customized IN THE PAGE: the overlay editor (`#overlay-scope=seat`, `#op-kind=add`, `#op-slug=debugger-role`, `#op-version=1.1.1`) added one op, and the page itself reported `seat overlay saved`. Consistency, both directions: the page's effective-modules list showed all nine slugs with the new one tagged `debugger-role … added by seat`, and the API composition matched exactly — the seat type's 8 `module_refs` plus the stored seat overlay op `[{kind:add, slug:debugger-role, version:1.1.1}]` (set equality True). LAUNCH THROUGH THE F4 CLIENT: `openrig_seat_sync.py`'s own `pull_seat` fetched the seat into a scratch store and `pinned.json` recorded exactly `6c14d7f8bdf3956a…` (equal to the page/API hash), the agent directory was materialized with `materialize_agent`, and `rig up` launched it — the launched node came up (`sessionStatus running`, `startupStatus ready`, runtime `omp`) and the launched render on disk carries the page's customization (`grep -c debugger-role guidance/role.md` -> 1). Stated limit: the launched omp seat's agent reports `agentActivity {needs_input, runtime_error}` — the OF3 finding that a seat launched outside the live rig has no model credential — so the launch proves the RENDER and the rig came up, not a model call. G6 was `[ ]` only because the launch half was unrun; it is now `[x]` on this evidence. INDEPENDENTLY REPRODUCED 2026-10-05 by the reviewer, on their OWN stack built from the current tip (Go server on :8010 over a fresh throwaway database, 9 seat types seeded, room `g6claim` with the same seat shape, vite on :3910, managed headless Chromium with the app cookie): the page reported `seat overlay saved` after `PUT …/seats/alpha/overlay` -> 200; the base hash was `0330a8e3fc29fd3386e9963ef88c1d0c4e01760e4af22ed896000cc96c605d2e` and after `add debugger-role@1.1.1` at seat scope became `6c14d7f8bdf3956a9749ea2beb04745d8f9dc1887aa305cbbeac5f11d6a268d4` — both digit for digit as claimed, and a further op moved it again; and the client's own `pull_seat` returned the snapshot directory named by that hash whose `guidance/role.md` contains `debugger-role`. So the "author-reported" limit on the browser hop is lifted for these three. Two operational notes from that run, worth the next person's time: (a) with `AUTH_ENABLED=false` the HTTP admin routes still require a NON-EMPTY `Authorization: Bearer` header (the value is ignored but a missing header is a 403), so a local-run recipe must say so; (b) the `seat overlay saved` toast is transient, so a test or probe asserting it needs a MutationObserver or a fake timer rather than a text scrape.
  - **Local-stack recipe, COMPLETED (2026-10-05, from fe-dev following the OF4 recipe verbatim).** Five omissions were found by following it, four of them in the first hour, and each is now fixed or named — so an operator reaches a page with data needing nothing more than the recipe. **(1) The server will not start without the `DATABASE_*` set.** With no `DATABASE_TYPE` it exits 1 `DATABASE_TYPE environment variable is NOT configured`; with `DATABASE_TYPE=postgresql` it still exits `Database configuration missing for postgresql` unless `DATABASE_HOST`, `DATABASE_USER` and `DATABASE_PASSWORD` are ALSO set — `DATABASE_URL` alone does not satisfy the postgresql path. The full set the code reads: `DATABASE_TYPE`, `DATABASE_HOST`, `DATABASE_PORT`, `DATABASE_NAME`, `DATABASE_USER`, `DATABASE_PASSWORD` (+ `DATABASE_POOL_SIZE`). **(2) Every `/api/v2` route requires a non-empty `Authorization: Bearer <anything>` even with `AUTH_ENABLED=false`.** `http.go` `currentUser` (Depends(get_current_user)) rejects a missing bearer header with `403 {"detail":"Not authenticated"}` BEFORE the provider runs; with auth disabled the provider accepts ANY value (verified: `Bearer dev`, `Bearer dev@example.com`, `Bearer test-token`, `Bearer local`, `Bearer AGENTHUB_TOKEN` all 200; no header 403 — on rooms, seat-types, machines, settings, tasks and projects alike). **(2) is INTENDED, not a defect** — it is the faithful port of FastAPI's HTTPBearer, which refuses a missing header regardless of the provider — so a local-run recipe must send the header. **(3) THE PUBLIC URL IS DERIVED, NOT REQUIRED — settled by `61acda94`.** `GET /api/v2/openrig/seats/{room}/{seat}` returned `500 AGENTHUB_PUBLIC_URL is not set`, so the seat page hung on Loading seat… and the composer was unusable. It now derives the base URL from the request (`seatMCPURL`, `seat_mount.go:41`), honouring `X-Forwarded-Proto` for the scheme and `X-Forwarded-Host` for the host (`:58-69`) — the same two headers in the same order as the Python convention this is a PORT of (`agenthub_main/src/fastmcp/server/http_server.py:169-184`) — with `withRequestPublicOrigin` (`:92-93`) stashing the origin on the context for the one caller that has no request (`call_seat_wiring.go`, fed by `mcp_routes.go:296`). **The variable still WINS when set:** `base := strings.TrimRight(os.Getenv(publicURLEnv), "/")` at `:42` is read FIRST, so a `Host` header can never reach the value when it is set. So the recipe line is: NO VARIABLE REQUIRED — the deployment public URL is derived from the request, and `AGENTHUB_PUBLIC_URL` is an OVERRIDE for a pinned deployment. **(4) THE SOCKET TOKEN SPLIT IS SETTLED — one auth decision for both surfaces, `b6d5db94`.** Under `AUTH_ENABLED=false` the REST routes accepted any bearer while the WebSocket route called `auth.ValidateTokenUniversal` and refused anything invalid with `403 Authentication required` (`ws_mount.go:73`, the error at `:75`), so the recipe's live half could not run in the setup the REST half was happy with. Fixed by ONE reading of the setting (`auth.AuthEnabled()`, `keycloak_dependencies.go`): with auth off the DEV USER resolves on BOTH surfaces (the socket no longer demands a real token), with auth on `ValidateTokenUniversal` stays, and a refused upgrade is COMPLETED AND CLOSED with `1008` plus a readable reason instead of a bare `403` — which a browser surfaces as `1006` with no reason at all. **THE DECISION-VERSUS-REPORT PROPERTY, recorded with this path because it is the same property seen from the other direction (2026-10-06; go-dev's): A PATH THAT SHARES THE DECISION BUT NOT THE REPORT IS STILL A PATH A CLIENT CANNOT ACT ON** — the setting tells it **WHETHER** to authenticate, the reason tells it **WHAT TO DO** about it. **Fixing the auth-setting branch while leaving a bare `403` would move this path from 'ignores the setting' to 'ignores the setting LEGIBLY' — a different DEFECT WEARING THE FIRST ONE'S CLOTHES.** Same family as **the reason text that never reached the chip** and **the `422` that blamed a missing field rather than the encoding.** Reviewer-approved on all four claims. **AND ONE CLOSE RATHER THAN A FIX, on the session-viewer's stream loop (2026-10-06; go-dev's verdict, from the reviewer's observation): THE LOOP TAKES A BACKGROUND CONTEXT, AND THAT IS DELIBERATE.** **AFTER A HIJACK THE REQUEST CONTEXT IS BOUND TO HANDLER RETURN RATHER THAN TO THE CONNECTION**, so it CANNOT fire on a client disconnect for that handler, and swapping to it would add **an INERT CANCEL SOURCE that fires only after the loop has already exited.** **THE BOUND THAT ACTUALLY APPLIES: the loop ends when the HIJACKED SOCKET'S BLOCKING READ FAILS** — the handler spawns a goroutine whose only body is a receive with a `defer` that cancels — which is **MILLISECONDS for a normal browser close** and, for a client that vanishes with no FIN or RST, **the OS TCP keepalive idle timer, at about TWO HOURS**. **No server-imposed deadline shortens it, deliberately: an idle realtime connection is NORMAL here, so a read deadline without ping/pong would disconnect healthy clients — which makes a deadline a HEARTBEAT FEATURE rather than a bound, and is the prerequisite to record if one is ever wanted.** **THE RESIDUE LINE, which is the reusable part: AN OBSERVATION ABOUT HOW A LOOP ENDS CAN BE TRUE OF THE MECHANISM IT NAMES AND SILENT ABOUT THE MECHANISM THAT ACTUALLY GOVERNS IT — THE ONLY WAY TO TELL IS TO ASK WHAT BOUNDS IT RATHER THAN WHAT IT DEPENDS ON.** The instance is exactly that shape: **an observation about a context that does not cancel, against a loop bounded by a socket read.** **AND THE SHARPENING (2026-10-06; go-dev's refinement): THE RESIDUE IS NOT 'THE OBSERVATION WAS WRONG', IT IS 'THE OBSERVATION WAS ABOUT THE WRONG BOUND.'** **The loop really DOES end on a send error in a world where sends happen, so the mechanism the observation named is REAL** — **what it was silent about is the READ that governs the ending.** That is the difference between treating a **true-but-non-deciding** claim as a mistake and treating it as what it is. **AND THE BOUND-VERSUS-HEARTBEAT SENTENCE, with its number and its prerequisite together so the entry prevents a future mistake rather than describing a past one: A DEADLINE WITHOUT SOMETHING THAT PROVES LIVENESS DOES NOT BOUND A TAIL — IT TURNS A RARE ABANDONED LOOP INTO A ROUTINE FALSE DISCONNECT**, because **an idle connection is the NORMAL state for that feature.** So what is missing is **a liveness probe (ping/pong), not a shorter deadline** — and that probe is the prerequisite before today's **~2-hour** tail can be shortened by configuration at all. **AND THAT GAP IS NOW CLOSED BY MEASUREMENT RATHER THAN BY TITLE (2026-10-06; fe-dev's socket matrix, on a binary that CONTAINS the fix — which is the distinction the whole row turned on): THE BARE PRE-UPGRADE `403` IS GONE.** On the fixed build the socket **completes the handshake and closes `1008` with the SAME required-credential reason as the realtime and connector paths**, in **BOTH auth settings and BOTH token shapes — NINE CELLS, UNIFORM** — so **NO PATH IS STRICTER OR LESS LEGIBLE THAN ITS SIBLINGS**, and **go-dev's fix is confirmed on the SHAPE IT NAMED rather than inferred from its title.** **AND THE METHOD DID ITS WORK BEFORE THE PROBE: THE ANCESTRY CHECK showed the fix was NEWER than BOTH binaries the verifier had run, so THE GAP WAS STALE BY CONSTRUCTION and the probe only confirmed what the ancestry already predicted** — **the SAME RULE used TWICE IN ONE HOUR IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS** (once to stop a false gap being FILED, once to close a REAL one), which is **the strongest evidence in this record that the vintage clause CHANGES DECISIONS rather than describing them.** **AND THE STANDARD FORM TO KEEP, now the ledger's shape for socket claims: FIVE FIELDS PER OBSERVATION — PATH, PORT, SETTING, TOKEN SHAPE, and OPEN-VERSUS-CLOSED-WITH-A-REASON — because all THREE of tonight's socket disagreements would have been IMPOSSIBLE TO STATE without them.** **ONE GAP REMAINS NAMED AND UNMEASURED: WHICH DEFAULT the runtime substitutes for an empty model.** With (1) to (4) named, the recipe is followable start to finish, and the error strings above no longer appear on that path. **(5) THE LOCAL STACK CATALOG IS UNSEEDED, so every seat type fails to resolve — the largest of the omissions, and the one that stops the recipe producing a working seat even after (1) to (4) are fixed.** Measured by fe-dev on the local stack (diffing the seeded types against the catalog): all NINE embedded seat types reference six modules each — **54 references, 26 distinct modules** (`test-driven-development@1.0.0`, `queue-handoff@1.0.0`, `verification-before-completion@1.0.0`, …) — and **ALL 54 are unseeded, zero overlap**, with `resolved_seats` empty. What it LACKS is precisely the 26 CURATED skill refs, and the step that supplies them is **`4genteam team publish-skills`** (`--source-root <OpenRig checkout>`, default `$OPENRIG_SKILLS_ROOT`, reading the inventory `ai_docs/agent-system/skill-library.json`), which must run BEFORE seeding seat types; a dry run prints `plan: publish-skills 52 skill block(s) from <inventory>` with one line per skill (`cmd_publish_skills`, `agenthub_client/src/agenthub_client/team_setup.py:764-778`). AND THE ORDER IS NOW ENFORCED, not merely advised: `5803772a` makes the seeder VERIFY the module refs it writes (`seat_seeder.go`), so a seed that runs before `publish-skills` FAILS LOUDLY naming the missing ref rather than succeeding into a `404` (its own gate — a real-database check — is IN FLIGHT, not done). - [~] G7 (**superseded 2026-10-08 by group O below — O3 to O5 carry the gate, and the owner's vision of 2026-10-08 makes it part of the product rather than an option. THE HOOK POINT RECORDED BELOW NO LONGER EXISTS: `HandleTaskCompleted` and `event_handler_initializer.go` were deleted in `e1970dc5` (2026-10-08); the gate is called directly by the task completion service.** Original text:) (option, owner 2026-10-03; start after G1 to G6): Jev as a completion gate. When a task or subtask completes, Jev reads the completion summary (with the task's acceptance criteria and testing notes) and judges whether the agent worked well; if not, the seat is stopped or a higher-grade seat is asked to recheck and re-guide. Hook: `TaskEventHandlers.HandleTaskCompleted` (`task_event_handlers.go:208`, subscribed in `event_handler_initializer.go:92`); `completion_summary` is already carried (`task_application_facade.go:1301`). Jev API (docs, 2026-10-03): `POST https://api.typesafe.ai/v1/systemone`, bearer key, `state` plus typed `questions` (Noul, Choice, Score), answers with probabilities and `confidence` for Choice and Score only; limits and pricing are not documented. Design: only summaries (already in the cloud) go to TypeSafe, not transcripts; thresholds and actions live in code; accept when every criterion question is clearly yes, send back when clearly no, escalate to the `escalates_to` seat (G1 `seat_links`) when confidence is low. Rollout: (a) shadow mode, judge every completion and log, no action; (b) compare with the reviewer seat's verdicts to calibrate thresholds and set a random audit sample; (c) enforce send-back and escalation; (d) stop a seat only through F5 (`rig seat stop`). Known limit: Jev judges the claim, not the work; a convincing summary of bad work can pass unless evidence (test output, diff stat from the client) is in the state or a reviewer verifies. Check: in shadow mode on real completions, Jev flags the cases the reviewer later rejects at an agreed rate. **VERDICT 2026-10-05 reconciliation: STILL OPEN — MISSING: the whole feature, and it is an OPTION.** No `typesafe` / `systemone` / Jev code exists in `agenthub_go` (grep empty); the hook points are recorded above; nothing started, by design ("start after G1 to G6").

**O. Orchestration layer** — the owner's vision of 2026-10-08 (`ai_docs/core-architecture/agenthub-system-architecture.md`, decision `ai_docs/core-architecture/agenthub-system-architecture.md D1`). All items are NEW: nothing below exists in the tree as of `eaa41d18` (measured by the architect, each gap with its file in the architecture doc, section 7). Order: O1a → O1b → O2 → O3 → O4 → O5 → O6 → O7; O8 can start once O1a lands and carries O1c with it; go-dev2 was removed on 2026-10-08, so its items are go-dev's; O9 is owner decisions, asked now. Rules: ORM first, then SQL; `user_id` on every row; no cascading foreign keys; no compatibility path. `progress_history` is removed, not kept beside the ledger.
- [ ] O1a (go-dev; **purely additive** — no existing write path emits events yet, the status path is unchanged): **execution ledger, table and reader.** ORM `TaskEvent` and table `task_events` (`user_id`, `task_id`, nullable `subtask_id`, gapless per-task `seq`, `kind` and `actor_kind` as CHECK vocabularies per the architecture doc 4.5, `payload` JSONB, `created_at`), a repository (`Append`, `ListAfter`), a recorder service that is the one writer API and takes the caller's transaction, and `GET /api/v2/tasks/{id}/events?after_seq=` (user-isolated). May touch: new files `domain/entities/task_event.go`, `infrastructure/repositories/task_event_repository.go`, `application/services/task_event_recorder.go`, a new handler file `server/routes/task_event_routes.go` with its own narrow `TaskEventReader` interface, a new file `infrastructure/database/task_event_tables.go` that holds the hand-written `TableDef` and registers it with `func init() { Tables = append(Tables, ...) }` (precedent `seat_tables.go:362`, `email_token_repository.go:86`); append-only edits to `server/httpapp/task_routes.go` and `server/httpapp/app.go` (one field and its wiring). Ruled 2026-10-08 after go-dev found `models.go` is generated from the archived Python metadata: the Go definition is the source for this table, nothing is added to Python, nothing is regenerated, and `kind`/`actor_kind` are CHECK constraints in that `DDL`, not PostgreSQL enum types (`ck_modules_kind` is the precedent). The handler 404s through the existing `UserTaskController.GetTask` with the caller's id before reading events; `UserTaskController` itself is not widened, because its adapter wraps `TaskAPIController` and `crud_handler.go`, which O1a must not touch. Must not touch: `infrastructure/database/models.go`, `tools/gen_models/`, `agenthub_main/`, `server/routes/task_user_routes.go`, `server/httpapp/task_controller.go`, `task_repository.go`, `task_application_facade.go`, `update_task.go`, `complete_task.go`, `update_subtask.go`, `crud_handler.go`, `search_handler.go`, `entities/task.go`, `database_migrations.go`, anything naming `progress_history`, the frontend. Not to be confused with `infrastructure/event_store.go`, an in-memory Python-port store reachable only through `DomainServiceFactory`/`DIContainer`, which no non-test code calls (measured 2026-10-08) — a separate deletion candidate. Check: without PostgreSQL, `TestTaskEventTableRegisteredAfterTasks` (`task_events` is in `Tables` at an index after `tasks`) and a handler test whose fake `GetTask` returns `!Success` and gets 404 — these two RUN on this box; with PostgreSQL, failing first, `TestTaskEventAppendAssignsGaplessSeq` (two concurrent appends on one task get seq 1 and 2); negatives: a `kind='bogus'` insert is refused by the CHECK, another user's task returns 404, `after_seq=N` returns only `seq > N`. Command: `cd agenthub_go && AGENTHUB_TEST_PG_URL=<throwaway admin dsn> go test ./fastmcp/task_management/... ./fastmcp/server/httpapp/... ./fastmcp/server/routes/... -run TaskEvent -count=1 -v`; the report says the PG tests RAN, since a skip is not a pass. Progress, measured 2026-10-08 (architect): the entity and table (`1d6c09d4`, `0c8122a9`) and the read route `GET /api/v2/tasks/{id}/events` (`bc6349ac`, `httpapp/task_routes.go:107`, the one registration that took the route count from 124 to 125) are in the tree. The box stays open until the PostgreSQL tests above are reported as run, not skipped.
- [ ] O1b (go-dev; **changes the status path** — its own gate; after go-dev's read-path work lands): **status and event in one transaction.** Every status write (`update_task.go`, `complete_task.go`, the MCP `crud_handler.go`, the REST route, `update_subtask.go`) goes through the task application service, which writes the status and a `status_changed` event in one transaction. Check: failing first, a test per write path asserting `status_changed` with old and new; negative: a forced event-insert failure leaves the status unchanged; the parity query (last `status_changed.new` equals `tasks.status`) returns zero rows on a seeded database.
- [ ] O1c (go-dev and fe-dev; **changes the stored shape and the details dialog**; lands with O8, after fe-dev's dialog bugs close): **progress becomes events.** `add_progress` writes a `progress` event; `progress_history`/`progress_count` are removed from the ORM, `fastmcp/database_migrations.go` and the Go readers (19 non-test files measured 2026-10-08), and the frontend's `ProgressHistoryTimeline.tsx`, `TaskDetailsDialog.tsx`, `SubtaskDetailsDialog.tsx`, `progressHistoryUtils.ts` and types read the O8 timeline instead. Check: `grep -rn progress_history agenthub_go agenthub-frontend/src` returns nothing; `npx tsc --noEmit -p .` is 0; the O8 timeline test passes.
- [ ] O2 (go-dev; renderer and MCP route only): **seat identity on MCP calls.** Every rendered MCP block (claude-code fragment and omp `mcp` block) carries the header `X-Agenthub-Seat: <room>/<seat>`; the MCP route reads it into the event `actor` (attribution only, never authorization). Check: renderer tests pin the header for both runtimes; an MCP call with the header produces an event with `actor_kind=seat` and that key; a call without it produces `actor_kind=human` with the user id.
- [ ] O3 (go-dev; client half with the ONE-CLIENT build): **evidence from the client.** `POST /api/v2/tasks/{id}/evidence` authenticated by a machine token (T4) stores an `evidence_submitted` event; `agenthub-client evidence --task <id> --base <sha> --test "<cmd>"` runs `git diff --numstat` and the test command itself and posts shas, numstat, and per-test exit code and failed names — no file contents and no output bodies. Check: 401 without a machine token; 409 when `head_sha` equals the previous evidence's; a real run on a scratch repo posts the numstat that `git diff --numstat` prints for the same range (output attached); no token in any log.
- [ ] O4 (go-dev): **acceptance criteria, scope and resume.** `tasks`/`subtasks` gain `acceptance_criteria` (JSONB string array) and `scope` (JSONB glob array), settable by `manage_task`/`manage_subtask`. `manage_task action=resume` returns the brief in the architecture doc 4.9 (task, criteria, last verdict and reasons, last evidence, open subtasks and blockers, resolved context, last handover, derived next action) within a size budget using `contextpacks`' token estimate. Check: a test that a task with a REJECT verdict resumes with that verdict's reasons as the next action; the brief is identical for two different callers; the budget overage is reported, never silently truncated.
- [ ] O5 (go-dev): **validation gate, shadow mode.** A gate service called by the completion path: deterministic checks D1–D5 (architecture doc 4.7) in code, then Jev for each acceptance criterion; writes a `gate_verdict` event `{verdict, checks, criteria, reasons, mode}`. Shadow mode writes the verdict and changes nothing. The Jev API is re-verified against live docs before coding (the G7 record is from 2026-10-03). Jev unreachable or over the daily budget gives `uncertain`, never `accept`. Check: unit tests for each D check deciding REJECT without a Jev call; a fake Jev pins accept/reject/uncertain thresholds; on real completions over an agreed window, the verdicts are compared with the reviewer seat's and the agreement rate is recorded here.
- [ ] O6 (go-dev; after O5's agreement rate is accepted by the owner): **gate enforcement.** When enforcing, `complete` moves a task to `review`; ACCEPT moves `review → done` and appends one verified line (task, head sha, files, tests) to the branch context; REJECT moves `review → in_progress` with the reasons; UNCERTAIN writes `escalated` to the worker seat's `escalates_to` seat (G1 `seat_links`), whose verdict is recorded with `actor_kind=seat`. `done` is reachable only through a verdict. Check: tests for the three routes; a task with no `escalates_to` link escalates to a human and says so; no path reaches `done` without a `gate_verdict` event (parity query).
- [ ] O7 (go-dev, then the client): **routing and wake-on-open-work.** Assignees become seat keys (`room/seat`) and the legacy role resolution (`ResolveLegacyRole`, `normalizeAssignee`) is removed; `manage_task action=claim` sets `claimed_by`/`claimed_at` through the cloud; `GET /api/v2/openrig/machines/{machine}/work` lists open, unclaimed or rejected tasks per seat with `last_seq`; the bridge loop runs `rig send` to an idle seat (per `seat_status`) with a one-line pointer to `manage_task action=resume` and posts a `delivered` event, once per (task, last_seq). Check: in a scratch rig, a task assigned to an idle seat is delivered once, the seat claims it, a REJECT wakes it again, and a second bridge pass sends nothing. **Owner confirmation needed first:** this makes the task the cloud's work item and replaces directive (F) F3's cloud port of `queue_items` for task work.
- [ ] O8 (fe-dev or web-dev; after O1): **task timeline and KPI panel.** The task details view shows the event ledger as a timeline (phase derived from events, never stored), live through the existing task websocket. A KPI panel shows verified tasks ÷ human touches, rework rate and escape rate (architecture doc section 9), all computed by one backend query so the counts agree everywhere. Check: vitest for the timeline rendering of a TEST-fail → FIX → TEST-pass → ACCEPT sequence; the panel's numbers equal the backend query's on a seeded database.
- [ ] O9 (owner decisions, recorded here when made): (a) **privacy** — may file paths and test names go to TypeSafe, or only counts; (b) **F3** — confirm the task replaces a cloud `queue_items` port (O7); (c) **directive 3** — delete `ai_task_planning`, the five `ai_*` actions and the `ai_*` task columns (the architecture does not use a server-side model runtime). None blocks O1 to O5; (a) blocks O5's Jev call on real data, (b) blocks O7.

# Owner requests (quoted as typed, then interpreted)

Written by the session that built the Python `session_stream` feature (2026-09-30) and moved here from `MIGRATION.md`. The owner's requests are quoted as typed, then interpreted. Requests 1–4 and 6–8 are verbatim except where marked "update"; their statuses are as of 2026-09-30/10-01. Request 5 (the session-streaming reference the Go port must reproduce) stays in `MIGRATION.md`. Do not print or copy secret values from servers or `.mcp.json`.

## Request 1 — "make 4genthub work like openrig workspace and tmux send message direct to pc user"
Interpreted: agents in tmux sessions should be addressable and receive messages (agent↔agent), and the human at the PC should be notifiable.
- Status: **design only, not built.** OpenRig reference: `~/__projects__/openrig/packages/daemon/src/adapters/tmux.ts` (`sendText`, lines ~470-520) and `domain/seat-delivery-guard.ts`.
- Delivery recipe (do not use plain `send-keys` for text): write text to a temp file → `tmux load-buffer -b <unique> <file>` → `tmux paste-buffer -t <pane> -b <unique> -d -r -p` → separate `tmux send-keys -t <pane> C-m`. `-p` brackets the paste, `-r` keeps LF (default turns LF into Enter = submit), `-d` drops the buffer. Serialize sends per pane and check `tmux has-session` first.
- To build: an MCP tool `send_to_agent(name, message)`; panes named per agent (`tmux new -d -s agent-<name> 'claude'`). Optional: per-agent message queue table.
- Human notification (owner must choose): WSL→Windows toast (`powershell.exe` BurntToast or `msg.exe`), `tmux display-message`, or a dashboard push over WebSocket. **Open question to ask the owner: agent↔agent, agent→human, or both.**
- Note: tmux must run inside WSL; the backend must run in WSL or call `wsl tmux ...`.

## Request 2 — "is possible reading session direct on 4genthub.com ? how i can link session running to web"
- Status: **backend ingest done in Python; connector and dashboard not built.**
- Architecture (WSL is behind NAT, so the session side dials OUT): `tmux pane / Claude JSONL → local connector (WSL) ══outbound WSS══▶ 4genthub.com ──▶ browser`.
- Sources the connector reads: (a) Claude Code transcripts `~/.claude/projects/<project>/<session-id>.jsonl` (structured, preferred for a chat view); (b) optional raw terminal via `tmux pipe-pane -o -t <pane> 'cat >> file'` (live) or `tmux capture-pane -p -t <pane> -S -200` (snapshot), rendered with xterm.js. OpenRig reference: `packages/daemon/src/domain/transcript-capture.ts` and `transcript-redaction.ts`.
- Risks: transcripts contain secrets → **redact on the user's machine before upload**; batch `pipe-pane` output (~100 ms) and cap sizes.

## Request 3 — "4genthub can use of different user, each user can connect his own session project via 4genthub"
- Status: **tenant isolation implemented in Python; connector and UI pending.**
- Verified by SSH (`ssh 4genthub`, alias in `~/.ssh/config`): CapRover host running `4genthub-backend` (8000), `4genthub-frontend` (3800), `4genthubdb` (Postgres 14.5, database `postgresdb`, user `postgres`), `keycloak` + `keycloak-db` (Postgres 17). Backend env: `AUTH_ENABLED=true`, `AUTH_PROVIDER=keycloak`, `KEYCLOAK_URL=https://keycloak.4genthub.com`, `KEYCLOAK_REALM=mcp`, `KEYCLOAK_CLIENT_ID=mcp-api` (plus secrets — names only: `KEYCLOAK_CLIENT_SECRET`, `JWT_SECRET_KEY`, `DATABASE_PASSWORD`).
- Existing prod tables relevant here: `api_tokens` (id, user_id, name, token_hash, scopes jsonb, created_at, expires_at, last_used_at, usage_count, rate_limit, is_active, token_metadata, usage_stats), `users`, `projects.user_id`, `user_sessions` (login sessions, NOT terminal sessions; empty). Prod had no agent-session tables before this work.
- Isolation rule: PostgreSQL has no row-level security here (that was a Supabase feature), so **every query must filter on `user_id`**, centralized in one repository. The Go port must keep that property and its tests.

Added untracked `4genthub/.agents/` (symlinks to the package) and a `deepseek` entry in git-ignored `.mcp.json` and `.agents/mcp_config.json`; `sequential-thinking` and `agenthub_http` entries unchanged. ## Request 6 — remaining steps of the same feature (not started; build in Go after migration, or in Python first if the owner wants it sooner)
1. **Connector CLI** (runs in the user's WSL): discover sessions with `tmux list-sessions` and map to JSONL files; tail `~/.claude/projects/*/*.jsonl`; redact secrets locally; open `wss://<host>/ws/connector` with an API token that has scope `sessions:write` (the existing token endpoint `server/routes/token_router.py` already accepts arbitrary scopes — **and its `default=["read"]` was a Pydantic field default that did NOT cross into Go: the current server applies no scope default, so a token minted without scopes carries none and a scope-gated route refuses it with 403 (corrected 2026-10-06, context-dev's measurement; the absence is conservative, since the failure is a refusal rather than a privilege)**); reconnect with backoff; package for `pipx`/`go install`. Send the frames listed above.
2. **Dashboard page** in `agenthub-frontend` (React/Vite, port 3800): list own sessions (`/api/v2/sessions`), live view over `/ws/sessions/{id}`, optional xterm.js tab for raw terminal. Auth is Keycloak.
3. **Web → tmux input**, opt-in per session, behind a separate scope `sessions:input`. This is remote command execution on the user's machine: read-only by default, per-user tokens, each connector bound to one account, only accept commands for sessions that same connector registered, rate-limit, audit log. Use the tmux delivery recipe from Request 1.
4. **Teams/sharing** (later): `team_id` and roles (owner/viewer); start single-owner.
5. **Security follow-ups**: store only token hashes (already the case in `api_tokens`), support revocation, rate-limit per connection.
6. **Deploy notes**: the WebSocket must pass through CapRover's nginx (`Upgrade`/`Connection` headers, long `proxy_read_timeout`); confirm "Websocket Support" is enabled for `4genthub-backend`. Verify `create_all` creates the two new tables on the prod Postgres before relying on them. Do not deploy without the owner's go-ahead.

## Request 8 — "i want 4genthub have same system topology like openrig" (recorded 2026-10-01)
- **Decision update 2026-10-02 (owner, option 1): superseded.** The full OpenRig runtime in Go is replaced by "OpenRig is the client, 4genthub is the cloud data" (see "Direction" at the top). There is no `rigd`; F4 is now the client bridge and F5 optional remote commands. The decisions, target design and risks below are kept for traceability.
- Owner decisions (asked and answered): scope = **full runtime** (model + API + dashboard graph + a daemon that launches each member in tmux, bindings, `up`/`down`/`send`/`restore`); language = **Go only in `agenthub_go`, wait for the migration** (no Python version). **AND THE CONVENTION THIS RECORD IMPLIES, with tonight's LIVE instance (2026-10-06; go-dev's form): A DECISION'S QUESTION MUST CARRY EVERY CONSEQUENCE THE RULING WOULD BIND — A PERMISSIVE FALLBACK THAT TWO SURFACES BOTH DEPEND ON FOR IDENTITY IS NOT A PERMISSIVE PATH, IT IS A COUPLING**, so *may a forged token pass when auth is enabled locally* is **TWO QUESTIONS WEARING ONE**: the same code decides **that** **and** supplies **the identity both surfaces agree on when auth is OFF**. **A READER WHO RULES ON ONE AND INHERITS THE OTHER IS THE SAME FAILURE AS A CHANGE THAT MOVES A SEAM WHILE BELIEVING IT MOVED A SYMPTOM.** **THE FIX IS TO STATE BOTH CONSEQUENCES IN THE QUESTION so nobody can answer half of it** — and the instance is live rather than hypothetical: **the auth question went to the owner in the two-consequence form rather than the one-line form it was first drafted as.** **THE SAME SHAPE AS THE PAIR-INVARIANT DISCIPLINE, arriving on a DECISION instead of on a change: the pair invariant says CHECK BOTH ENDS BEFORE YOU MOVE; this says CHECK BOTH BINDINGS BEFORE YOU RULE.**
- Status: **plan only, nothing built.** Checklist group **F** above holds the work items. Full plan with the OpenRig reference analysis: `~/.claude/plans/compressed-conjuring-aurora.md` (may be overwritten by later plans; this section and group F are the durable record).
- What "OpenRig topology" means (verified in `~/__projects__/openrig`): a `rig` contains `pod`s (namespaced), pods contain `member`s/nodes (a seat = agent + runtime + cwd + model) and `edge`s. Spec format RigSpec 0.2. Only `delegates_to` (source launches before target) and `spawned_by` (parent launches before child) affect launch order (topological sort, a cycle fails); `can_observe`, `collaborates_with`, `escalates_to` are descriptive. A daemon persists rigs/pods/nodes/edges/bindings/sessions in SQLite, launches each node in a tmux pane through a runtime adapter (`claude-code`, `codex`, `terminal`), and exposes `rig up/down/ps/send/capture/whoami/restore/snapshot`.
- What 4genthub has today: agents are configuration rows (`agent_templates` 32, `user_agent_instances` 58, `agents` 3), not running processes; the data model is projects → branches → tasks → subtasks; there is no rig/pod/member/edge, no binding and no daemon.
- Target design: hub-and-spoke. The 4genthub server (multi-user, Keycloak) stores topology with `user_id` on every row. A local `rigd` on each user's machine launches seats and dials OUT to the server, so the server never reaches into a user's machine. `rigd` is the same component as the session-stream connector (Requests 2, 3, 5), so **F4 supersedes C1, D2 and D4: fold them, do not build both.**
- Risks: the TypeScript runtime is large (instantiator ~2.5k lines, restore ~1.9k, tmux ~1k), so F4/F5 are the expensive parts while F1–F3 already deliver value alone; remote launch is remote command execution (see F8); WSL2 is untested upstream (see F9).


## Request 9 — "you must see how openrig config these agent then make 4genthub adapt to openrig for make openrig is client and 4genthub is cloud data for orchestration no limit" (2026-10-02)
- Status: **F0 done and verified locally; production rollout pending** (F0a–F0d).
- How OpenRig configures an agent (verified in `~/__projects__/openrig`, `docs/as-built/architecture/agent-spec-and-startup.md`): a directory with `agent.yaml` (`name`, `version`, `defaults.runtime`, `profiles.<name>.uses`, `resources`, `startup.files` with `delivery_hint`), `guidance/*.md`, and `runtime/*.json` fragments. Startup layering is additive: agent → profile → rig culture → rig → pod → member. `agent_ref` accepts only `local:` and `path:`. A `claude_mcp_fragment` is deep-merged into `<cwd>/.mcp.json` and the fragment wins.
- Decision: the cloud renders, the client materializes (pull). See "Direction" at the top.
- Related bug found and fixed in code: `call_agent` failed for every agent on `api.4genthub.com` (Go) because nothing populated `agent_templates` (`YAMLAgentTemplateLoader.LoadAllAgents` had no callers). `agenthub -seed-agents` fixed it (removed in T6, `19373d03`, with `call_agent`); it was never run in production (F0b). **Superseded by F0b: on production the rows exist but are in the Python format.**

## Request 11 — the company-workplace model: seats, rooms, occupants, modules (2026-10-03)
Owner's words, in two messages: "each team (pod) have seat, seat is defind it never change, context, memory, tools, knowledge, skills neverchange is handle by 4genthub, each thing like a module lego can add or remove easy, we need page design/custom seat. open rig handle material and give possible to agent can communicate and working on our project, we can see is on herdr. claude, chatgpt, gemini just like a brain it easy switch by change model, we no use context local on .claude or .agents anymore. jev is eyes for see what happen for feedback to brain. deepseek-offload (or local agent or cheaper cost agent) is handle worker for do job" and "seat in office room never change but we can custom seat for make it adapt to need of companie, change how seat communicate to other or give document or give context or skill, tools, instruction how to stay on this seat, we can add seat or remove seat if need thinking like a companie workplace".
- Status: **design discussion only, nothing built, decisions pending.** No checklist items yet; add a group once the owner confirms the model.
- Interpretation: company = tenant (culture, policy); room = pod; seat = a fixed role slot with a stable address; occupant = the brain (runtime + model); job description = instructions; documents, skills, tools, memory = modules (lego) held by 4genthub; communication = edges plus rules; hiring/removing = adding/removing seats (removal is a hard delete since `945648f5`). A seat type is versioned and immutable; a company customizes it with an overlay (add, remove, override, pin a module version); 4genthub resolves the overlay into one effective seat and renders it as a flat AgentSpec.
- Verified against OpenRig (2026-10-03): OpenRig already separates seat from occupant (`rig seat launch` "a deliberate fresh occupant for exactly one existing seat", `rig seat handover`, `rig seat set-model`). Seats are added and removed on a live rig with `rig grow`, `rig expand`, `rig add`, `rig shrink`, `rig remove`. Runtimes: `claude-code`, `codex`, `agy` (Gemini/Antigravity), `pi`, `omp`, `terminal`, `stub`. Jev (TypeSafe System One) takes text only and returns Choice/Score/Noul judgments, never text (docs.typesafe.ai/concepts/system-one.md).
- Limits found (these change the design):
  1. Edges are descriptive. Only `delegates_to` and `spawned_by` affect launch order; edges do not route or restrict `rig send` (`docs/reference/edge-types.md`). Company communication rules are therefore advice (instructions plus edges in `rig whoami`) unless enforcement is built.
  2. OpenRig's startup layering is additive with no deduplication, so layers cannot remove or override. Resolve the overlay in 4genthub and emit one flat spec.
  3. Adding or removing a seat does not update edges and permissions; OpenRig's own skill warns that references go stale. 4genthub must regenerate the spec and reconcile desired state against `rig ps`.
  4. Seats are always-resident sessions in v1 (`wake_on_demand` is rejected in `agent-manifest.ts`); a large office means many live processes. `rig seat stop`/`launch` per seat is the lever.
  5. Local files are still written at launch (`CLAUDE.md` managed block, `AGENTS.md`, `.mcp.json`, user-global `~/.codex/config.toml`); they can become generated cache but cannot disappear. Module changes apply on the next launch or restore; no live-update path was found.
  6. Switching vendor means switching runtime and re-rendering per-runtime files; history and resume tokens do not transfer, cloud memory and the handover tooling do.
  7. Deepseek-offload is an MCP tool of a seat, not a seat (jobs get no MCP tools, git commits refused, file changes need review).
- Decisions (owner, 2026-10-03): (1) communication rules are **enforced**, not advisory (level L1/L2/L3 still to be confirmed, see below and G3); (2) offline mode is explicit and uses **OpenRig bundles** (G4), never a silent fallback; (3) module versions are **pinned by default**, with follow-latest as an opt-in per company; (4) Jev is an **option** defined as a completion gate (G7), started after the core works.
- Enforcement findings (2026-10-03): OpenRig has no communication enforcement and removed sender refusals on purpose ("deliver and label", `routes/require-sender-identity.ts`). The sender is a header the CLI stamps from the seat's environment (`X-OpenRig-Session`), so it is forgeable by any local process, and `rig send` can reach any seat. Three levels are possible: L1 advisory (instructions plus edges in `rig whoami`, what exists today); L2 guarded (the only communication tool a seat has is a policy-checking one, direct `rig send`/`rig queue`/`rig broadcast`/tmux denied in the seat's permissions, every message audited, messages that bypass the audit flagged); L3 hard (daemon-side ACL with authenticated identity plus OS sandboxing, which needs an OpenRig fork). L2 stops mistakes and normal agent behavior but is not a security boundary against a seat that deliberately bypasses it. Which level to build is open (see G3). Not read: `daemon/src/domain/policies/edge-artifact-required.ts` (a policy tied to edges; check whether it helps).

## Request 12 — "need make is corherence with openrig" (2026-10-03)

Seat/room/link model aligned with OpenRig's real model. Done and verified:
- Mapping: room = OpenRig pod (id = room slug), seat = member (id = seat key), seat link = pod edge. Slugs and keys use OpenRig's name rule `^[a-zA-Z0-9][a-zA-Z0-9_-]*$` (no dot, because edges qualify ends as `pod.member`); enforced in the admin API and the UI.
- Link kinds are exactly OpenRig's five: `delegates_to`, `spawned_by`, `can_observe`, `collaborates_with`, `escalates_to`. `reports_to`, `consults`, `notifies` were removed (clean break, DDL CHECK changed).
- L2 intent to kind mapping (default deny, explicit deny wins): task -> `delegates_to`; escalation and report -> `escalates_to`; question and notice -> `collaborates_with`. `spawned_by` and `can_observe` never authorize a message (`can_observe` means reading output via `rig capture`).
- `GET /api/v2/openrig/rooms/{room}/rigspec` renders the room as RigSpec 0.2 (active seats, `allow=true` links between active seats, hashes of the pinned snapshots); 404 absent room, 409 no active seats.
- Client: `openrig_seat_sync.py rig ROOM` pulls every seat at its pin and builds `<out>/<room>/rig/{rig.yaml,agents/<seat>}`; prints `rig:<path>` so the operator runs `rig up <path>`.
- Verified with the real `rig` 0.6.3 (isolated daemon): `rig spec validate` valid, `rig spec preflight` ready. Warning "permission_policy absent; launch_posture=floor" is OpenRig advice, not an error.
- Finding: `agent_ref` must be `local:agents/<seat>` (relative to the spec dir); `path:` requires an absolute path and fails validation.
- Verified on production (owner-stated 2026-10-03): the room rigspec endpoint serves the rendered spec.
- Done locally (`9115b6ab`, `1afc7555`; not on production): per-seat `permission_policy` replaces the rig-level flag. Column `seats.permission_policy`, default `standard` (never `yolo`); set with `PUT /api/v2/openrig/rooms/{room}/seats/{seat}/permission-policy`; rendered on each rigspec member; `scripts/openrig_seat_sync.py` reads it from the spec (there is no `--permission-policy` flag). `rig spec validate` and `rig spec preflight` passed for each policy (go-dev-reported; the writer did not run them).

(2) **THE DISCRIMINATOR: with the cloud UNREACHABLE, a record equal to the running hash gives `unchanged`, and REPLACING ONLY THE RECORD flips it to `changed`** — so **NOTHING BUT THE RECORD PRODUCED THE VERDICT**; (3) **a MALFORMED record reads as EMPTY** — every verdict unknown, **no traceback**; (4) **an answer carrying NO verdicts leaves the record BYTE-IDENTICAL, with its md5 recorded** — so **nothing is invented and nothing cleared**; (5) **THE CASE THAT WAS SILENT, now named: the cloud's expectation MOVED while the machine did NOT; the ANSWER named the drift, and the LOCAL verdict read `unchanged` AT THE SAME MOMENT** — which is **the division of labour the PREVIOUS version had BACKWARDS.** **The bridge suite's counts were also reproduced independently: 54 and 219.** **AND TWO INSTRUMENT NOTES, recorded so they cost nobody else the time they cost the verifier: the run NEEDS THE REAL HOME DIRECTORY because a seat shell remaps it**, and **the PRINT-ONLY mode prints WITHOUT sending, so the record is written only on a REAL send** — **a trap for anyone testing the write path by printing.**
