(ii) the connector's stale `403` test asserted the contract AS IT STOOD, so `b6d5db94` UPDATED it rather than deleting it — updating keeps the RECORD THAT THE CONTRACT MOVED, whereas deleting it would leave a reader unable to tell a deliberate change from missing coverage, which is the gap this record exists to close. Deleting a superseded test is not a clean-up; it is losing the evidence that the contract changed. **AND A CLAUSE FROM THIS EVENING'S SECOND INSTANCE OF THE SAME ACT, from the other direction (2026-10-06; web-dev's authoring inspection): A FIRST FIX THAT HIDES WHAT LOOKS LIKE AN OVERSIGHT CAN BE WRONG WHEN A TEST PINS THE OVERSIGHT AS A DECISION — WHAT LOOKS LIKE AN OVERSIGHT IN A SURFACE CAN BE A PINNED POSITION, AND THE TEST IS WHAT TELLS YOU WHICH.** The instance: web-dev hid the `mcp` kind from the publish form because it looked like an oversight, an existing case **FAILED**, and **THE CASE WAS RIGHT** — the same shape as the create-form copy claim an hour earlier, arriving from the opposite side. **AND THE VALIDATION FINDING THAT SURFACED WITH IT, FIXED IN `318c3a07` AND RECORDED BECAUSE IT IS A VALIDATION GAP RATHER THAN A UI ONE: THE ROUTE VALIDATED THE KIND BUT NOT THE BLOCK** — measured, not inferred: publishing an `mcp` module whose content is **prose** returned **200 and stored it**, so **every seat referencing it fails to resolve**, and with the earlier unresolvable-seat fix that failure now appears **one step away from its cause.** The fix **refuses the SHAPE, using the parse mirror that already existed for the `mcp` path.**

30. THE VALUE GOES IN THE SENTENCE ONLY AFTER THE COMMAND THAT PRODUCES IT HAS BEEN READ. (Beside rule 28 — the same family: a sentence composed from the output the author EXPECTED rather than the output produced.) Incident: go-dev reported a commit under a hash it had not yet read (a `74b…` placeholder written in the same breath as the commit) and corrected itself to `e51fa0b5` in the next message; measured here, `74b` resolves to NO commit at all. Its own reasoning is why this stays a rule: **A WRONG HASH IN A HANDOFF SENDS THE READER TO SOMEONE ELSE'S COMMIT, AND IT IS WORSE THAN AN OMITTED HASH — an omission is VISIBLY INCOMPLETE while a wrong hash LOOKS AUTHORITATIVE.** The PAIR is worth keeping rather than one half, because they are both worse than an omission for OPPOSITE reasons: a hash that points NOWHERE — as `74b` does, where NOTHING invites a check because a plausible short hash looks fine — and a hash that points at the WRONG COMMIT, which is at least findable by looking but sends the reader to work that is not yours. A correction also depends on the reader having stayed for the second message, which the writer cannot assume. Same shape as the note-versus-message count, the `args.path`-versus-`args.command` search (rule 28), and the misattributed changelog blob (rule 18): in each, the claim was composed from what was expected rather than from what was produced. Recovery, the same each time: READ THE OUTPUT, THEN WRITE THE CLAIM — never the reverse. **A CLAUSE ON THIS RULE, in a new medium (2026-10-06): A VALUE CAN BE SILENTLY REMOVED FROM A MESSAGE AND THE MESSAGE STILL READS AS COMPLETE.** The removal happens in the SEND path rather than in the text: in a message sent through a shell, **BACKTICKS ARE COMMAND SUBSTITUTION**, so a command or value wrapped in them is EXECUTED-and-DROPPED rather than sent — nothing written was wrong, and the loss leaves NO mark in the delivered message, which stays grammatical with a hole where the command should be. The asymmetry that makes it diagnosable: backticks in a FILE written by the edit tool are LITERAL, which is why the same command survived intact in `54736ead`'s CHANGELOG line while the message carrying it lost it. The remedy is a PRACTICE rather than a record rule: **NO BACKTICKS IN SHELL-SENT MESSAGES.** **AND IT IS RIG-WIDE RATHER THAN ONE SEAT'S SLIP (2026-10-06): THREE seats hit it in one shift** — this seat **twice**, the **reviewer once**, and **fe-dev once** (the last injecting vitest output into a sent message) — so the clause is **evidenced three times rather than once**, and **a hazard three independent seats hit in one shift is a TOOL TRAP, not a discipline failure**. The fix belongs in the tool as much as in the habit: a send path that executed-or-dropped a quoted span and reported success is the defect; "no backticks" is the workaround until it does. **FOURTH INSTANCE, AND THE FIRST ITS OWN AUTHOR CAUGHT IMMEDIATELY (2026-10-06):** this seat wrapped commands in backticks in a shell-sent message, the shell executed them, and the message arrived **WITH HOLES AND SHELL ERRORS** — the clause describing its own violation, an hour after it was written. **THE PAIR TO RECORD IS THE VIOLATION AND THE REPAIR:** a send cannot be recalled, so the only correct move is to **RESEND CLEAN** rather than explain it away — which is what happened — and **a rule that has caught its own author within the hour of being written is the strongest evidence available that it is worth keeping.** **A SECOND CLAUSE, same rule, same failure at the READ (2026-10-06): THE DEFAULT READ OF A DURABLE RECORD CAN BE A BOUNDED PREVIEW THAT READS AS COMPLETE.** `rig queue show` returns **512 characters without `--full`** and gives **NO SIGN that it is partial**, so a reader who omits the flag sees a mid-word cut and concludes the record was truncated — while the whole body is ONE FLAG AWAY (`--full` returned **1636 characters on the same row**, with all three quoted rules present by substring; measured again on a throwaway row: **610 characters in, 512 via `--json`, 610 via `--json --full`**). The test is therefore sharpened to **READ IT BACK WITH THE INSTRUMENT THAT SHOWS IT WHOLE**. The shape is worth naming because it is this rule twice in one probe: **the lead and this seat both measured a REAL difference and both attributed it to the WRITE rather than the READ, because the preview is silent about being a preview** — the count-without-its-pattern defect in a new medium, and the THIRD instrument tonight that was wrong about itself (the reviewer's census note, go-dev's line-anchored grep, this). **The write was whole; the read was bounded** — and no write-side truncation clause is recorded, because that claim was an inference from a read-side preview.

31. A TEST THAT CLICKS BEFORE AN ASYNC LIST SETTLES FAILS INTERMITTENTLY AND LOOKS LIKE A FLAKE RATHER THAN A DEFECT — AWAIT THE CONDITION, DO NOT RETRY THE RUN. (The second test-hygiene line, beside rule 29, and rule 16's family: the flake is the INSTRUMENT, not the code under test.) Incident (2026-10-06, web-dev): two list tests asserted on a seat card WITHOUT first selecting the room and so raced the asynchronous room list; the fix was to AWAIT THE BUTTON, not to retry — because a retry hides a real ordering defect behind a re-run and teaches the suite to tolerate it.

32. A DIFF THAT TOUCHES A `.env`-FAMILY FILE IS ALLOWED WHEN IT ADDS KEY NAMES, REQUIREMENTS OR COMMENTS, AND NOT WHEN IT ADDS A VALUE. (The file-handling rule, beside the pathspec/keep-out rules; the reason it exists is to keep VALUES out of the repo AND out of agent context, and a tracked SAMPLE carries no values by construction.) **THE TEST A READER APPLIES IS WHAT THE DIFF ADDS, NOT WHICH FILE IT TOUCHES** — the same scope-correction shape as rules 11/15: the boundary belongs inside the claim, and the object that matters is the VALUE, not the filename. Incident: a seat flagged its OWN edit to `.env.sample` because its guidance says never read or create `.env` files; the ruling was that the edit STANDS — `8a6976bf`'s sample diff is four COMMENT lines (`# Must be set (>=32 chars) and identical on every process and every restart.`, `# There is no default: with this unset, token minting and token validation both refuse.`, …) and adds NO value — because the guidance was about VALUES, not about the filename, and the seat was RIGHT TO ASK rather than assume.

**A conclusion from the same row, kept as a conclusion rather than a rule (2026-10-06, `8a6976bf`):** THE DIRECTION OF A FIX CAN BE DICTATED BY THE SECURITY PROPERTY RATHER THAN BY SIZE — deleting the hardcoded JWT default was both SMALLER and the ONLY safe option, because making the other component the default instead would have SPREAD A PUBLIC CONSTANT INTO AUTHENTICATION VERIFICATION EVERYWHERE. A row filed as a consistency bug closed as an authentication change, and what decided the direction was WHICH WAY THE HARDCODED VALUE POINTED.

**Two more conclusions from that same change, where they read best (2026-10-06, go-dev's mint-error row):** (1) **THE SHAPE OF A FIX THAT EXPOSES A LATENT FAILURE — THIS DEFECT EXISTS BECAUSE THE FIX WORKED.** Removing the hardcoded default stopped the mint from silently succeeding with a public constant, and what took its place is a bare `500` that does not name the variable; so the fix did not CREATE a failure, it EXPOSED ONE THAT HAD BEEN HIDDEN BEHIND A VALUE THAT SHOULD NEVER HAVE BEEN THERE — a latent silent failure whose symptom was invisible while the defective path was succeeding. The recognisable form: when a fix makes a surface LOUDER, check whether you CAUSED the noise or REVEALED it, and say which in the commit. (2) **THE DISCARD FINDING, the mechanism behind that bare sentence: THE CAUSE WAS PRODUCED, WRAPPED AND DROPPED THREE TIMES** — the controller returned `nil` instead of the error, the mount passed a `nil` error, and the router overwrote what remained with a generic sentence — so the failure existed at every step and was thrown away, and the message was the LAST-LAYER REPLACEMENT rather than the first-layer failure. Caveat go-dev drew against itself, kept because it marks the boundary: **a DEFAULT for an UNKNOWN failure** — the fallback still present at `token_router.go:189` (`pyOrStr(result.Error, "Failed to generate token")`, with its comment) — **is NOT the same as a REPLACEMENT for a KNOWN one.**

33. WHEN A MEASUREMENT AND A MECHANISM APPEAR IN ONE SENTENCE, SAY WHICH IS WHICH. (Beside rule 30's family — the placeholder hash, the stale citation, the queue-state summary — in every case **THE SENTENCE CLAIMED MORE THAN THE THING THAT PRODUCED IT**.) The incidents: the lead observed a park call REFUSED and wrote "the park verb FAILS ON AN AGENT SEAT", when the measurement was only ever **THIS CALL, WITH THESE FLAGS, REFUSED** — a FIELD-LEVEL refusal asserted as a VERB-LEVEL fact, and then BROADCAST, so nine seats worked briefly from an overstatement corrected twenty minutes later; and the reviewer's, the same shape — `git check-ignore` reported NO MATCH (a measurement, and TRUE) while the sentence wrote "a pattern that does not reach them", which is an UNREAD MECHANISM — one sentence carrying both with nothing marking the boundary. Recovery, the same two-part discipline as rule 30: **READ THE OUTPUT, and SEPARATE WHAT YOU MEASURED FROM WHAT YOU INFERRED ABOUT HOW IT WORKS.** The cost is asymmetric and belongs with the rule: an overstatement in a BROADCAST is read by every seat and corrected only for those still listening. **AND ITS SECOND INSTANCE, WHICH NAMES THE MECHANISM THAT MAKES THE BOUNDARY HARD TO SEE (2026-10-06; the reviewer's self-correction): AN UNVERIFIED MECHANISM INHERITED BY CITATION WHILE THE OTHER HALF WAS VERIFIED BY MEASUREMENT.** The case: the tool-description fix `80c3ac2b` closed **one half of its sentence BY MEASUREMENT** — a blank model on **UPDATE is replaced by the stored one, with no error**, checked in the service — and **INHERITED the other half BY CITATION**: "on CREATE it uses the runtime default", carried over from the pre-existing wording. **THEN THE SECOND HALF WAS VERIFIED AND NOTHING IN THE REPO PERFORMS IT: the RUNTIME inherits its version default on create, but THE MODEL IS DEFAULTED NOWHERE** — a tree-wide search for a model-default assignment returns nothing outside tests, and **the validator accepts a blank model.** So that clause is an **UNVERIFIED MECHANISM**, with two honest possibilities — **the runtime CLI applies its own default, OR the clause is WRONG** — and **the live check sits with fe-dev, not with this record.** **AND THE TRIGGER IS THE REUSABLE PART, in the reviewer's words: the create half was asserted FROM A COMMENT THAT SAYS THE INHERITANCE IS CREATE-ONLY — A TRUE STATEMENT ABOUT THE RUNTIME THAT SAYS NOTHING ABOUT THE MODEL — so THE COMMENT WAS RIGHT AND THE EXTENSION OF IT WAS NOT.** **That is the instance of A TRUE SENTENCE USED ONE STEP BEYOND ITS SUBJECT — the same shape as reading a heading's TEXT instead of its LEVEL.** **AND THE PRACTICE, recorded because it cost nothing: THE CORRECTION WAS SENT THREE MINUTES AFTER THE VERDICT AND BEFORE ANYONE ACTED ON IT** — which is the point of correcting in the next breath rather than after the fact. **AND THE CLOSURE, WHICH IS THE SHAPE WORTH KEEPING (2026-10-06; fe-dev MEASURED the clause rather than re-reading it): NOTHING SUBSTITUTES A MODEL DEFAULT IN THIS CODEBASE.** The three reads: **a seat created with a BLANK model returns the EMPTY STRING**; **the list read-back confirms the store HOLDS BLANK**; and **the RESOLVE RENDERS NO MODEL LINE AT ALL — not blank, NONE.** **THE RUNTIME HALF IS A NAMED GAP RATHER THAN A FACT: `omp` has NO configured default model, and whether its CLI falls back to a built-in one NEEDS A LAUNCH THAT WAS DELIBERATELY NOT RUN** — and **in this rig a launched seat's model comes from the RIG CONFIG, so that path is not exercised here at all.** **AND THE DISPOSITION IS THE USEFUL PART — AND THE SET IS BIGGER THAN FIRST RECORDED (corrected 2026-10-06): SEVEN TEXTS PLUS ONE TEST ACROSS TWO LANGUAGES CARRIED ONE CLAUSE, AND ONE MEASUREMENT DECIDED ALL OF THEM.** The sites, re-measured here: **the FRONTEND holds SIX occurrences — FIVE TEXTS PLUS THE TEST — a VALIDATOR COMMENT** (`src/lib/seatNames.ts:29`), **a PLACEHOLDER** (`SeatsPage.tsx:524`), **a USER-VISIBLE HINT on the seats page** (`SeatsPage.tsx:527`), **TWO TOPOLOGY DISPLAY FALLBACKS** (`TopologyGraph.tsx:67`, `TopologySeatsTable.tsx:51`), **and the TEST** (`src/tests/pages/SeatsPage.test.tsx:272`) — **and the GO SIDE contributes the TOOL DESCRIPTION** (`80c3ac2b`) **plus a domain comment the lead's sweep found, which is what takes the resolved set to SEVEN TEXTS + ONE TEST** (the exact list is one `grep` per language, which is how each count here was taken). **AND THE TEST IS THE STRONGEST FORM THE CLAUSE SURVIVED IN, BECAUSE A TEST READS AS EVIDENCE** — so the same sentence was assertable in a suite, shown to an operator in the UI, and printed in a table. **THE RESOLUTION IS STATED IN fe-dev's FORM, the same form the reviewer praised in the corrected description: THE MEASURED HALF PLUS THE EDGE OF WHAT IT CAN SUPPORT** — *a blank is ACCEPTED and stored blank, NOTHING HERE substitutes a default, and any substitution would have to come from the RUNTIME, whose fallback is UNMEASURED.* **And the clause was INHERITED BY CITATION in ALL FIVE PLACES, which is why THE MEASUREMENT, NOT THE WORDING, WAS THE THING TO GET.** **AND TWO FACTS FROM THE SAME VERIFICATION, ADDED TO THIS CLAUSE ENTRY (2026-10-06):** (i) **THE RENDERER PACKAGE CONTAINS NO REFERENCE TO THE MODEL IN ITS NON-TEST CODE** — `renderer.go` and `spec.go` return **zero** hits (the only occurrences are **four in `renderer_test.go`**) — **so the sentence's NEGATIVE half is CODE-BACKED rather than only measured**, and the two halves are now **backed by DIFFERENT BUT BOTH-CHECKABLE EVIDENCE: the negative by CODE, the possibility by an EXPLICIT SILENCE.** (ii) **THE SENTENCE'S SCOPE IS EXACT: it says the RESOLVED SEAT renders no model line, WHICH IS THE ARTIFACT THAT WAS CHECKED** — so **the claim is as wide as its words and no wider**; **if any surface ever needs the WIDER version, that is a NEW CLAIM needing its OWN check rather than an inherited one.** **AND A CLEAN SPLIT FROM THE SAME REVIEW, worth keeping AS A SPLIT rather than as a caution because it converts a FORBIDDEN READ into an ANSWERABLE QUESTION: THE CONFIG GIVES THE LOAD PATH, THE ENVIRONMENT GIVES THE VALUE.** The instance: **M1's premise came from a delegate command that READ a parent env file — which our standing rule forbids** — and the reviewer **accepted the miss with the right framing: RELAYED EVIDENCE IS EVIDENCE YOU ARE VOUCHING FOR.** **The rule-safe route establishes the SAME question in two halves: a config file's env DIRECTORY tells you a parent env file WOULD be loaded (a CONFIGURATION fact, readable without opening anything), while whether a variable is actually SET there is a fact about the OPERATING ENVIRONMENT and belongs to whoever owns it. **AND THE PRACTICAL FORM OF THE SAME ANSWER, CHEAPER THAN THE READ IT REPLACES (2026-10-06; the reviewer's closure): WHICH ENV FILE WINS IS VISIBLE IN THE DEV SERVER'S OWN STARTUP OUTPUT** — **its log line NAMES BOTH FILES and says which one it is USING** — so **the loaded owner can be established by reading a line the tool ALREADY PRINTS, with NO FILE OPENED and NO RULE BENT.** **AND THE DISTINCTION THAT MAKES THIS WORTH KEEPING, in fe-dev's words: the value happened to be IDENTICAL IN BOTH FILES, so READING THE CONTENTS WAS SUFFICIENT BY LUCK RATHER THAN BY METHOD — THE DIFFERENCE BETWEEN A SAFE CONCLUSION AND A SOUND ONE.** **That is the same shape as a correct finding produced by an INSTRUMENT THAT COULD NOT HAVE FOUND THE ERROR**, and it is why the rule exists rather than being a pedantic objection: **A METHOD THAT WORKS BY COINCIDENCE REPORTS NOTHING WHEN THE COINCIDENCE FAILS.** **AND THE SCOPE-CLOSURE, since it is the form the fix took: THE SENTENCE NOW NAMES THE ARTIFACT — the RENDERED SEAT, `agent.yaml` — AND CARRIES A CODE WARRANT, because the renderer package references NO MODEL AT ALL** — so **the claim is TRUE ABOUT ONE NAMED ARTIFACT rather than PROBABLY TRUE ABOUT EVERY SURFACE**, which is **the same move as scoping a count to what was measured.**** **AND THE MISS IS RECORDED ONCE FOR ALL THREE SEATS, as the reviewer asked — THIS ONE INCLUDED: THREE seats established a value by READING an env file within the hour — one through a delegate, one directly, and THIS SEAT BY A `grep` OF THE ROOT `.env` — with sound findings every time and nothing sensitive exposed; THE LINE IS RECORDED, AND THE ROUTE FOR THE NEXT SUCH CHECK IS THE SPLIT BELOW.** **AND THE CLAUSE ITSELF IS THREE-WAY, NOT TWO-WAY — CORRECTED 2026-10-06, because this clause's OWN record was wrong and the reviewer RETRACTED the stronger claim rather than defending it:** (i) **THIS CODE APPLIES NO MODEL DEFAULT** — created blank, stored blank, and the resolve renders no model line, **verified at the source: the renderer package holds ZERO model references outside tests**; (ii) **THE RUNTIME SUBSTITUTES ONE WHEN HANDED NONE — MEASURED: two one-shot runs, one with an explicit EMPTY model and one with NO flag, BOTH exit 0 and normal**; (iii) **WHICH DEFAULT IT PICKS IS ESTABLISHED BY NOBODY.** **So the sentence's clause is TRUE AND SOURCED TO THE WRONG PLACE** — which makes the frontend sites an **ATTRIBUTION fix rather than a deletion** — **and the earlier claim that a test pinned an UNESTABLISHED claim as tested behaviour WAS WRONG, retracted by its own author: THE TEST ASSERTS SOMETHING REAL.** **AND THE GENERAL FORM, WHICH SUPERSEDES THE SINGLE-INSTANCE VERSION ABOVE (2026-10-06; go-dev has now seen it TWICE and named it): A SENTENCE CAN CHANGE WHICH HALF IS MEASURED WITHOUT BEING WRONG FIRST.** **Instance one is the FILE-VERSUS-PROCESS row, which moved from "the file is in the build" to "the file is not the process"; instance two is THIS blank-model clause, which moved from "the runtime default applies" to "this repo substitutes nothing and the runtime substitutes, with WHICH default unmeasured".** **IN BOTH CASES THE REPAIR WAS THE SAME, AND NEITHER WAS A REWRITE: STATE THE MEASURED HALF FLATLY AND THE UNKNOWN AS ITS OWN CLAUSE.** **AND THE DISTINCTION THAT MAKES THAT A RULE RATHER THAN A CONFESSION, in go-dev's words: A SENTENCE THAT NEEDED THREE PASSES BECAUSE THE FACT MOVED IS NOT A SENTENCE THAT WAS WRONG ONCE** — **it is one whose EDGE WAS BEING MEASURED WHILE IT WAS BEING WRITTEN, and the named-edge form is what stops the next move from needing another pass, because A SENTENCE THAT NAMES ITS OWN EDGE ONLY NEEDS REVISING WHEN THE EDGE CLOSES.** **AND A SECOND DISTINCTION FROM THE SAME COMMIT, worth its own line: WHAT THE CLAUSE IS AND WHAT JUSTIFIES IT HAVE DIFFERENT DISTRIBUTION REQUIREMENTS** — **the clause is carried CHARACTER FOR CHARACTER across sites so a diff can compare them**, while **the warrants (a runtime probe, a renderer check, three storage reads) live in the COMMIT MESSAGE**, since they are **EVIDENCE ABOUT THE SENTENCE rather than part of it.** **AND THE RULE FOR MAINTAINING SUCH A TEXT (2026-10-06; the reviewer's ruling): WHILE ONE SENTENCE MUST SERVE SEVERAL SITES IDENTICALLY, A SITE THAT NEEDS MORE PRECISION GETS AN ADDITIONAL SITE-SPECIFIC SENTENCE BESIDE THE IDENTICAL ONE — NEVER AN EDIT TO THE SHARED TEXT.** **THE REASON IS THE FAILURE MODE RATHER THAN TIDINESS: the next person to notice a gap will WIDEN THE SHARED SENTENCE TO FIX THEIR OWN HOME, and the IDENTIFIABILITY that made the sites comparable BY DIFF is gone IN ONE COMMIT.** **The rule's own FIRST TEST is this clause: it serves SIX HOMES, the two Go sites are BYTE-IDENTICAL, and the frontend's four MUST STAY SO.** **AND THE COMPANION POINT, worth the line because it separates two things that look alike: THE SHARING IS COSMETIC FOR THE CLAIM AND REAL FOR CHECKABILITY** — **shortening the sentence left the claim EXACTLY AS WIDE AS ITS WARRANT** (the resolved seat is the renderer's own artifact, so nothing widened to "no surface shows a model"), **while what left was the reader's ability to SEE THAT SCOPE AT A GLANCE — and the warrant itself moved into the commit message, where it is FINDABLE rather than gone.** **The author asked whether the loss was material or cosmetic; the form of verdict to keep is the answer: COSMETIC FOR TRUTH AND REAL FOR CHECKABILITY.**

34. A GREEN COUNT WITH AN `Errors` LINE IS NOT GREEN — THE ERRORS LINE IS WHERE A MOUNT-TIME CHANGE HIDES — READ THE WHOLE REPORT. (The pair to rule 25, same family, opposite emphasis: rule 25 said the COUNT is the evidence when a filtered run prints a successful tail; HERE the count AND the tail AGREE and a THIRD line contradicts them — so the general form is **READ THE WHOLE REPORT — count, tail, AND the errors-and-warnings lines — because each of the three can be the only one telling the truth**.) Incident (2026-10-06, web-dev): while changing a mount path, two existing cases already set up the state the new mount branch acts on, so the mount refreshed BEFORE the case's explicit call, ATE the case's queued rejection, and let the explicit call reach an unmocked fetch — which threw a `TypeError` instead of the `Network` error the case asserts. Vitest reported the run as **1755 passed WITH AN UNHANDLED REJECTION** rather than as a failure, because the assertion escaped as an unhandled promise while the test counted PASSED; it was found only by reading the run TAIL rather than its count. **Companion line, for the shared-file hazard (rules 18/27):** the same seat handled the sweeping hazard by **SEQUENCING** rather than by careful staging — it holds one row's changelog entries OUTSIDE the tree so the other row's commit cannot take them — which is the STRONGER remedy, because it REMOVES the hazard rather than managing it. **AND A CLAUSE FROM THE MISSION'S NAMED `gofmt` FINDING, CLOSED AS A MEASURED ZERO WITH A TRAP IN IT (2026-10-06; the reviewer's reconciliation, re-measured here):** the subtask-controller finding is **NOT ABOUT FORMATTING** — scoped to the **45** tracked subtask Go files `gofmt` exits **0** with zero output, and the wider zero (**1,184** tracked `*.go`, `gofmt` clean and `go vet ./...` clean) **agrees**: **TWO INDEPENDENT MEASUREMENTS, AND THEIR AGREEMENT IS THE ARTEFACT.** **THE TRAP, which is the finding rather than the zero: THE NATURAL FORM OF THE COMMAND CANNOT FAIL CORRECTLY.** Run over the **RAW** tracked set — the pattern the tracking list returns — `gofmt` exits **123 with an EMPTY stdout and TWO stderr errors**, because **TWO of the 1,184 paths matching the glob are NOT Go source**: **`captain-definition.backend.go`** (a CapRover JSON definition: `expected 'package', found '{'`) and **`docker-system/docker/Dockerfile.backend.go`** (a Dockerfile: `illegal character U+0023 '#'`). So **on a perfectly formatted tree the command is PERMANENTLY RED for a reason unrelated to formatting**, and **A CHECK THAT GATES ON THE EXIT CODE IS BROKEN WHILE ONE THAT GATES ON THE EMPTY LIST IS CORRECT** — so whoever writes a permanent check must **FILTER THE TWO NON-GO FILES, OR GATE ON THE LIST RATHER THAN THE EXIT CODE.** **AND THE SCOPE NOTE, the same rule as the count-with-its-pattern: the UNSCALED form returns 89 or 102 depending on when it is run — ALL inside the module cache — so a number from it HAS NO VERSION**; if a figure is recorded anywhere it must be the **SCOPED** one, with the tracked list as its scope. **The reviewer's 1,184 and go-dev's 1,182 were the SAME TREE AT TWO SCOPES; the difference was the FILTER, and NEITHER WAS WRONG.** **AND THE SENTENCE THAT GENERALISES THE TRAP: THE EXIT CODE CANNOT DISTINGUISH A REAL FINDING FROM A QUESTION ASKED THE WRONG WAY, WHILE THE EMPTY LIST CAN.** **AND THE FILE NOTHING SEES, which is the finding the scope question produced (2026-10-06; the reviewer's formatting pass): `agenthub_go/fastmcp/server/__main__.go` IS TRACKED AND APPEARS IN NO BUILD LIST** — the tracker's **1,182** against the build's **1,181**, with this as the difference *(the pair is recorded AS REPORTED, and **each figure's UNIT is stated so the pair is usable rather than confusing: the reviewer's **1,181** is the build-visible SUM OF COMPILED, TEST AND EXTERNAL-TEST FILES, while this seat's **768** is the NON-TEST COMPILED SET** (`{{.GoFiles}}` summed per package, and `./...` itself skips `_`/`.`-prefixed directories) — so **BOTH NUMBERS ARE TRUE AND NEITHER IS WRONG**, the same shape as **1,184 against 1,182** an hour earlier, where the difference was a FILTER. **This is the THIRD time tonight that a disagreement between two correct numbers was a SCOPE rather than an error.**)* **AND THE SENTENCE WORTH CARRYING IN THE ENTRY ITSELF: A FIGURE I CANNOT REPRODUCE IS A FIGURE I MUST NOT PRESENT AS MINE** — recorded as **REPORTED**, with the reason, so the line separates **what this seat measured** from **what it was told**, in the same sentence, rather than leaving a reader to assume the whole line is first-hand. **`go list -f '{{.IgnoredGoFiles}}'` returns EMPTY, so it is NOT a build-constraint exclusion the tool reports**, and its only export — `func Main()`, "the programmatic entry point mirroring `python -m fastmcp.server`" — is **referenced NOWHERE** (verified here by grep); it arrived with the port commit beside its Python sibling. **THE MECHANISM IS INFERENCE AND IS MARKED AS SUCH:** the go tool ignores files whose names begin with `_` or `.` **without listing them as ignored**, which is why the exclusion is silent — recorded as the READING, since the tool itself reports nothing. **AND THE CONSEQUENCE IS WHAT TO CARRY: A SOURCE FILE THAT NOTHING COMPILES, VETS, TESTS OR FORMATS READS AS IMPLEMENTED — WHICH IS WORSE THAN ONE THAT IS OBVIOUSLY MISSING.** **THE SCOPE COMPARISON AS ITS OWN LINE, because it is the reusable part: the TRACKER-BASED list is COMPLETE and exit-correct WHEN FILTERED, while the BUILD-BASED list is CLEANER but structurally BLIND to anything the toolchain skips** — so **the two differ in exactly the place a defect hides.** The gate recommendation is therefore **the TRACKED SET MINUS THE TWO NON-GO FILES**, with the build list named as the **NARROWER ALTERNATIVE rather than as a wrong one.** **AND THE ROW CLOSES ON A MEASUREMENT RATHER THAN A VERDICT (2026-10-06; the reviewer's confirmation at the committed bytes, re-measured here):** the deletion is **ONE FILE, NINE DELETIONS, THE PATH GONE** (`bab4d4b4`, "chore(server): remove `__main__.go`, a tracked Go file nothing could see" — it arrived with `6b0bdd5f`, the port commit, beside its Python sibling); the module **builds and vets clean**; and **THE REFERENT SEARCH IS THE REAL CHECK RATHER THAN THE GREEN BUILD** — **zero imports of the server package, zero calls to the symbol, zero definitions remaining** — because **the file was never IN the build, so a green build was NECESSARY AND NOT SUFFICIENT.** **THE SUPERSESSION:** the port **lives on as the server package's own entry helper**, and **the real entry points are the two commands.** **AND THE CLOSE, stated in the same shape as the evidence, with the units side by side: BEFORE, the TRACKED Go set was 1,182 and the BUILD-VISIBLE set 1,181 — WITH THIS FILE AS THE DIFFERENCE; AFTER, BOTH ARE 1,181 AND THE DIFFERENCE IS ZERO** *(re-measured here as **1,183 raw tracked `*.go`** and **1,181 after removing the two non-Go files**, so the tracked-and-Go set and the build-visible set now agree exactly)* — **so the by-product of a scope comparison has been MEASURED AWAY RATHER THAN ARGUED AWAY, and the adopted gate form over the tracked set is clean.** **AND THE RECONCILED NUMBERS, so the entry carries the RESOLVED set rather than the confusion (2026-10-06, re-measured here):** **RAW TRACKED is 1,183 today** — it was **1,184 before the deletion**, and it is a **MOVING TARGET BY CONSTRUCTION**, because the shared tree gains Go files; **TRACKED MINUS THE TWO NON-GO NAMES is 1,181**; and **BUILD-VISIBLE is 1,181, being 768 COMPILED + 375 TEST + 38 EXTERNAL-TEST files summed over the module** (768+375+38 = 1,181). So **the two scopes AGREE EXACTLY at 1,181**, and the raw figure is a **third** that includes the two non-Go files. **THAT AGREEMENT IS WHAT CLOSED THE INVISIBLE-FILE ROW: BEFORE, the two scopes differed by EXACTLY THE FILE THAT NOTHING COMPILED.** **AND THE GATE ANSWER, which turned out to be a BOUNDARY rather than a gap (2026-10-06; go-dev's, verified here AT THE CONFIG): THE GO TREE IS EXPLICITLY EXCLUDED FROM THE CHECKS THAT EXIST** — the only pre-commit config (`agenthub_main/.pre-commit-config.yaml`) scopes its linting with `files: ^agenthub_main/` and puts **`exclude: ^agenthub_go/`** on BOTH the **trailing-whitespace** and **end-of-file** hooks, and neither CI workflow (`production-deployment.yml`, `test_coverage.yml`) contains **any Go tooling** — so **there is NO formatting or build gate for Go anywhere, BY CHOICE RATHER THAN BY OMISSION.** **NOTHING IS BEING ADDED** (the pipeline job is the owner's **declined** decision); what is recorded is **the PAIR OF CANDIDATE FORMS WITH THEIR SCOPES and the finding inside them: FORM A — TRACKED AND FILTERED — is correct in BOTH readings AND COMPLETE**, because it sees tracked files the build ignores; **FORM B — BUILD-VISIBLE — is exit-correct and extension-immune but INCOMPLETE BY CONSTRUCTION, and it would NOT have seen the file this hour removed.** **EITHER FORM MUST GATE ON THE EMPTY LIST, NOT THE EXIT CODE** — the natural command exits **123** with empty stdout on a perfectly clean tree — **and the baseline is already measured, so a first run starts from a KNOWN CLEAN TREE.**

35. A RELAXATION IS ALLOWED ONLY WHERE A CHECK SHOWS IT CANNOT MATTER — AND THE EVIDENCE MUST BE A COMMAND, NOT A JUDGEMENT. (Beside rule 15, the same family: a claim travels with the check that produced it. "Touches no code file" is checkable; "it is only docs" is not.) **THE RE-FREEZE DISCRIMINATOR, from the 2026-10-06 production freeze:** when a commit CROSSES a freeze, **MOVE THE FROZEN POINT IF THE THING THAT CROSSED IT IS VERIFIABLY INERT, AND RE-RUN IF IT IS NOT**. The case: two docs commits crossed the hold, the lead verified with `git diff --name-only` that they touch NO code file, and RE-FROZE at the new tip rather than aborting the owner's run — because aborting would spend the owner's time on a commit that cannot affect what is being pushed. The half with teeth: **if what crossed TOUCHES CODE, the safe default is a RE-RUN**, since then the thing being pushed is not the thing that was certified.

36. A NUMBER WRITTEN AS A GUESS READS AS A MEASUREMENT ONCE IT HAS A SECOND HOME. (Pairing with rule 15, which says WHAT must travel with a number — pattern, file set, unit; this says WHY a wrong one travels so far: **each copy strips the hedge and keeps the confidence**, and the second home is usually a DOCUMENT rather than a person.) Incident (2026-10-06): go-dev's mint-fix report hedged a count as "the nine sibling methods"; the lead filed a **ticket** around that hedge; go-dev carried it into a **brief**; and the count was **TEN** when finally measured — a hedge in a report became a premise in a ticket and then a premise in a brief, with nobody counting. The repair is the same both times: **COUNT IT YOURSELF BEFORE IT BECOMES SOMEONE'S PREMISE** — and go-dev's actionable half, which is the sentence to keep: **ANY NUMBER I WRITE THAT I DID NOT MEASURE GETS MARKED AS UNVERIFIED IN THE SENTENCE**, so that if it travels, it travels carrying its own uncertainty. Why the marker belongs IN THE SENTENCE rather than in a preamble is go-dev's second clause, which is better than the lead's framing: **THE SECOND HOME IS USUALLY A DOCUMENT RATHER THAN A PERSON, AND A PERSON WHO HEARS A HEDGED NUMBER CAN ASK WHILE A DOCUMENT THAT CARRIES IT CANNOT BE ASKED AT ALL** — which is exactly why a hedged NINE survived two agents and one filing and arrived as a premise. Disposition of the row itself: it is **CORRECTED rather than re-filed**, because the BODY is what the next reader trusts and a correction that lives only in a thread does not travel with it. **AND THIS RULE IS SUBORDINATE TO RULE 30, stated here because the two instruct opposite things in one situation** (found by the 2026-10-06 reconciliation): in *I need to write a count I have not measured*, 30 says the value goes in only after the command has been read while this rule says an unmeasured number gets MARKED. **36's MARKER IS THE FALLBACK FOR A READ THAT IS IMPOSSIBLE, NOT A LICENCE TO SKIP A READ THAT IS AVAILABLE; rule 30 governs whenever the command CAN be run** — **a hedge must never be cheaper than the act it replaces.** **Companion line, same row:** TWO CLAUSES THAT LOOK LIKE ONE CONDITION CAN HAVE DIFFERENT CAUSES — `err != nil` always has an ACTIONABLE cause, while `facade == nil` is a composition-root INVARIANT with none — and where a method genuinely cannot explain itself, a **COMMENT plus a TEST turns the omission into a decision**, which is the difference between a repair and a sweep. **AND A PROVENANCE NOTE FOR PACKET 3's RECORD, when it is written (2026-10-06): the release marker is `c898dd3a` — ONE LINE PLUS ITS CHANGELOG ENTRY IN THE SAME COMMIT.** **Worth stating because the PREVIOUS marker's changelog line was SWEPT INTO A SIBLING's commit, and the attribution only came to light because the AUTHOR CHECKED** — so the same-commit shape is recorded as **the marker's own property**, since a marker whose changelog rides elsewhere is exactly the shared-index hazard (rule 18) landing on the provenance line. **AND THE PACKET-3 RECORD ITSELF — FOUR COMMITS, EACH WITH THE SHAPE WORTH KEEPING (2026-10-06):** **(1) M3, `f06e42fd`, three paths — THE FIX WAS THE STATE, NOT THE WORDING:** a refusal close returns without scheduling a retry, **but the error setter left the retry flag TRUE from the preceding disconnect, so the chip promised a retry the client had decided against**; **the setter now clears it and the chip reads offline with the server's reason.** **The test drives the store's own sequence and FAILS with the state line removed**, so the finding is **reproduced as a test rather than described** — **THE DISPLAY WAS CORRECT FOR THE STATE IT WAS GIVEN.** **(2) M2, `5b9a019f`, two paths — THE CLAIM WAS WRONG AND IS CORRECTED IN PLACE:** the config sets its env directory to the PARENT, so **the REPO ROOT env file is the one read and this directory holds only a sample**, **measured through the resolver itself (it returned the parent directory and a concrete value)**; the old sentences are corrected **with a pointer**, and **the author's standard is recorded: A READER MUST NOT BE ABLE TO FIND THE WRONG CLAIM AND BELIEVE IT — a correction that leaves the old sentence standing elsewhere has not corrected anything.** **(3) M1's ANSWER — NOT A REVERT, AND BOTH THE SYMPTOM AND THE FIX WERE OBSERVED IN LOCAL DEV:** the app's own log shows **the variable UNSET, the DEFAULT being its own origin, and the final URL equal to that default**, so **the socket went through the dev server's proxy** — **both readings were true at once, which is why the disagreement existed at all.** ****AND THE FINDING IS NOW RESOLVED, WITH THE MECHANISM ESTABLISHED — and THE SOURCE IS A FILE RATHER THAN A MESSAGE: `.openrig/agenthub-seats/4genthub-min/VITE-ENV-INVESTIGATION.md` CARRIES THE TWO INJECTED ENVIRONMENT OBJECTS VERBATIM** (the harness server's object holding **only the two process-environment variables**, and the repo config's object holding **the full parent environment with `VITE_WS_URL: "ws://localhost:8000"` present as a literal**) — **the value of that artifact being THE STRINGS THEMSELVES rather than a description of them.** **THE PAIR OF CONTRADICTORY OBSERVATIONS RESOLVED AS TWO DIFFERENT DEV SERVERS — the client's unset reading and the config's resolved value DESCRIBE TWO SERVERS RATHER THAN ONE BEHAVIOUR — and THE MECHANISM IS THE CONFIG'S ENV LOADING: the injected environment REFLECTS THE CONFIG'S ENV LOADING AND ITS ENV DIRECTORY** (with no `envDir`, Vite uses its default, the `agenthub-frontend` directory holding only `.env.sample`, so ONLY process-environment variables are injected; with `envDir: '..'`, the parent `.env` contents are injected too). **THE VALUE THEREFORE NEVER ARRIVED rather than arriving and being discarded, and THE CLIENT'S READ AND ITS FALLBACK WERE BOTH CORRECT** — so **THE QUESTION MOVED FROM THE CLIENT TO THE CONFIG.** **AND TWO OF THE THREE CANDIDATES ARE REFUTED RATHER THAN MERELY UNCHOSEN — BUT THE FIRST REASON IS CORRECTED HERE (2026-10-06) BECAUSE IT WAS TAKEN FROM THE ARTEFACT'S OWN PROSE INSTEAD OF BEING MEASURED: the RUNTIME WINDOW PATH (`window._env_`) is NOT ABSENT IN DEVELOPMENT — THE RUNTIME ENVIRONMENT OBJECT IS PRESENT AND SIMPLY CARRIES NO WEBSOCKET KEY — so it falls through harmlessly and CANNOT produce this; and the COMPUTED-KEY READ is NOT a defect — in the BUILT BUNDLE the env object is emitted WITH ITS KEYS (including `import.meta.env.VITE_WS_URL`, one occurrence of the ws URL), so a dynamic read resolves against a populated object in the build as well as in dev. THE CONCLUSION STOOD AND THE REASON DID NOT: the reviewer withdrew that part of its certification, which is the same failure as the truncated line one layer in — a sentence standing in for a measurement.** **AND ONE RESIDUAL STAYS A RESIDUAL: WHICH CONFIG THE ORIGINAL OBSERVING RUN USED IS NOT PROVABLE FROM HERE** — both measurements are consistent with **a dev server whose env directory was not the parent**, but **the text must not assert the harness that produced the symptom.** **AND THE REUSABLE PART IS THE DIAGNOSTIC, QUOTED VERBATIM: fetch the transformed config module from the running dev server (`/src/config/environment.ts`) and read its FIRST LINE — the injected object is literally there and A MISSING KEY IS VISIBLE WITHOUT INFERENCE, so any future instance is settled in ONE COMMAND rather than a night.** **AND THREE BOUNDARIES, each a way this paragraph could become wrong again: (1) it does NOT generalise to "variables do not reach the client", which is FALSE because ON THE REPO'S CONFIG THEY DO — the mechanism is THE CONFIG'S ENV LOADING AND ITS ENV DIRECTORY; (2) it does NOT imply the client DISCARDED a value, since the key was NEVER IN THE OBJECT IT READ; and (3) it does NOT NAME the config the original observing run used, because that is not provable from here.**** **(4) THE CLAUSE SET, `0cfee720`, seven paths:** the shared sentence landed **CHARACTER FOR CHARACTER against the two Go sites, taken BYTE-EXACT so the check is a DIFF**; **the two topology labels kept `runtime default`, as ruled**; and **a test was RENAMED to what it asserts — an empty model is SENT AS AN EMPTY STRING — with its assertions UNTOUCHED.** **THE RENAME IS THE RIGHT KIND OF TEST EDIT: THE NAME CLAIMED A SUBSTITUTION THE ASSERTIONS NEVER PINNED.** **AND THE NUMBERS: the full frontend suite is 102 files and 1761 passing with ZERO errors, the type check at zero and the build ok, at BOTH the M3 commit and the clause commit.**

37. A CORRECTING NOTE ANNOUNCES ITSELF IN ITS FIRST WORDS. (Beside rule 36 — its practical half; the same principle as naming the pattern beside a count: **THE CORRECTION HAS TO TRAVEL WITH THE CLAIM IT CORRECTS**.) Mechanism, measured (go-dev): a **queue row BODY is IMMUTABLE and its NOTES are APPEND-ONLY**, so a row cannot carry a corrected statement of its own premise — it carries the ORIGINAL first and the correction after, and a reader who stops at the body reads the WRONG number. The convention: open the correcting note with a marker that NAMES ITSELF (go-dev used `PREMISE CORRECTION`), so the stale statement and its correction cannot be read apart. And the reason it matters beyond the queue: **a correction that lives in a THREAD does not travel — the ROW and the FILE are the places it has to land.** **A VARIANT ON THIS ENTRY, attached rather than numbered (a variant attaches to its PARENT, because a numbered list is what readers cite and its length is a cost everyone pays):** **TWO INSTRUCTIONS INSIDE A MINUTE CARRYING OPPOSITE DISPOSITIONS ARE THE CROSSED-MESSAGE HAZARD IN INSTRUCTION FORM, AND THE RECOVERY IS FOR THE RECEIVER TO STATE THE NET STATE AND NAME WHICH INSTRUCTION WON FOR WHICH OBJECT** — rather than asking the sender to re-derive it from the thread, which is the same reason the receiver is the one who can see both.

38. A DURABLE ARTIFACT THAT CANNOT BE EDITED IN PLACE MAKES ITS FIRST VERSION AUTHORITATIVE, WHETHER OR NOT IT WAS RIGHT — AND EVERY MECHANISM THAT APPENDS AROUND IT IS A WORKAROUND FOR THAT. (Beside rules 36/37, and the same family as the counting and provenance rules one layer out: **WHAT A READER MEETS FIRST IS WHAT THEY TRUST.**) go-dev extended it past the queue verb, and the extension is what makes it a RULE rather than a platform note: **IT IS AS TRUE OF A COMMIT MESSAGE AS OF A QUEUE BODY** — neither can be corrected in place once someone has read them. And the consequence it draws is why tonight's habit has been **CORRECT IN THE NEXT BREATH rather than a rewrite**: even where an artefact COULD be rewritten (a shared file, a hash in a message), **REWRITING COSTS MORE THAN THE CORRECTION IS WORTH** — the next breath is cheap, the rewrite is expensive in other people's work. **A CLAUSE ON THIS RULE — content-hash identity must be FROZEN BY CONVENTION (2026-10-06):** AN ARTEFACT WHOSE IDENTITY IS ITS CONTENT HASH MUST BE FROZEN BY CONVENTION, BECAUSE NOTHING ELSE FREEZES IT: an artefact WITH a commit cannot drift from its identity, because the identity IS the commit; an artefact WITHOUT one drifts the moment anyone types. **Two forms of the failure, both invisible for the same reason** — nothing in the file says which hash it corresponds to: **hashing BEFORE an edit you then make**, and **editing AFTER you have hashed**. The instance: `473144ca` was a REAL measurement taken after two edits and before a third, cited to the reviewer once that third edit had landed, so the artefact sat READING AS GATED while its content had already moved past the hash it carried. **The instrument that would have caught it is one already in use — rule 37's read-back, extended from STATE to CONTENT:** the state was read back and was right while the hash was cited and never re-derived. **The ordering fix: take the hash as the LAST act, not the first act after deciding to gate.** **And a refinement that makes staleness VISIBLE: A HASH CARRIED IN A MESSAGE SHOULD NAME THE MOMENT IT WAS TAKEN** — cite it with the mtime or the read's timestamp, so a reader can compare it against the file's own mtime and see whether the artefact has moved; the same move as naming the interval in rule 40. **AND ITS COMPANION, for an artefact with NO commit (2026-10-06, the reviewer's closing form): IDENTITY IS THE CONTENT AND HISTORY IS THE MTIME.** A reader must check the **HASH** to know **WHAT** they are gating and the **MTIME** to know **WHETHER IT MOVED** — **a match on one alone is HALF A CHECK**. Instance: the closing revision was approved at mtime `05:59:18`, then a REVERTED edit restored the SAME content hash at mtime `06:00:28`, so that approve was **VALID BY CONTENT and LUCKY BY TIMESTAMP** — both statements true, and not the same statement. Same shape as the count and its pattern, the value and its moment, the number and the tree state. **AND THE INVERSE OF THE FREEZE RULE, with tonight's instance (2026-10-06): REVERTING TO AN APPROVED REVISION IS NOT AN AMENDMENT.** A revert that restores the SAME BYTES makes the artefact's identity equal to the content that was approved, so **a gate over that content SURVIVES a revert** — while an edit that ADDS or REMOVES content cannot. The mechanism is that **a gate names BYTES rather than a filename**, which is why two readers, one writer and one crossed instruction could disagree about WHICH revision was current and still lose nothing. **AUDIT COROLLARY: COMPARE CONTENT, NOT MTIME** — an auditor checking mtimes alone reports movement where identity is unchanged, which is the mirror of a stale hash looking like a moved file. Instance: the closing revision was reverted into place at mtime `06:00:28`, so the hash matched the approval while the timestamp did not; the approve was **valid by content**, and the timestamp difference is only a fact about when the bytes landed. **AND THE CONSEQUENCE THAT MAKES BOTH FIELDS NECESSARY RATHER THAN EITHER** — the reason the clause is not merely hygiene: **a content match proves the artefact NOW is the approved one, and says NOTHING about the revision that existed BETWEEN the read and the revert**, so **anything that read the file in that window read UNGATED BYTES**. For this file nothing consumed it; **for a file that a LOOP EXECUTES, that interval is the whole risk** — which is why a gate records the hash AND the mtime, and a reader checks both.

39. ANY CONDITION THAT LICENSES INACTION MUST NAME ITS OWN FALSIFIER — AND A VERDICT IS A PERMISSION TO ACT. (The GENERAL statement, of which the freeze is one instance; cross-referencing the counting and provenance rules 15 and 36, because all are the same family: **A STATE ASSERTED ONCE AND TRUSTED THEREAFTER**.) An APPROVAL is precisely a licence for someone else to STOP CHECKING — which is why the not-verified list every verdict carried tonight is NOT ceremony: it STATES WHAT WOULD HAVE TO BECOME TRUE, or what nobody has looked at, for the approval to be wrong. Two of tonight's verdicts are that distinction OPERATING rather than a form of words: `ff5f10d6` was approved ON READING and only became approved-on-the-live-pair when the two header pairs arrived, and the seeder row was held open for the ARTEFACT half after the BEHAVIOUR half was independently confirmed — in both cases the verdict NAMED ITS OWN BOUNDARY and could be revisited without anyone remembering why it was provisional. **COROLLARY (the reviewer's words): THE DEFAULT SHOULD BE THE NARROWER CLAIM UNTIL THE EVIDENCE WIDENS IT**, because AN APPROVAL THAT OVERSTATES IS INDISTINGUISHABLE TO ITS READER FROM ONE THAT DOES NOT — the same defect as a count without its pattern and a measurement without its mechanism, and the reason WHAT I DID NOT RUN is worth stating even when the answer is obvious. **THE INSTANCE from the freeze side:** a commit freeze was set saying the push was expected within the hour, and FOUR HOURS later nothing had RE-MEASURED whether it happened — production still `0.0.18`, `origin/main` still `89feea4a` — while the hold kept TWENTY-FOUR modified paths NON-DURABLE, which is the exact hazard the hold was not supposed to create; so a freeze must be lifted by an OBSERVABLE fact ("hold until `origin/main` moves"), never by a duration, or nobody notices when it never becomes true. A HOLD, a GATE, a PARKED ROW and a BLOCKED QUEUE are all PERMISSIONS NOT TO ACT — **and a permission without a check is how tonight produced FOUR INHERITED STATES**: the 23 TypeScript errors, the `gofmt` finding, the retired agent library, and the freeze itself. **A CLAUSE ON THIS RULE, credited to the reviewer (2026-10-06): A SAFETY STEP WHOSE VALIDITY DEPENDS ON STATE THE CHANGE ITSELF CREATES MUST CARRY THAT BOUNDARY.** The test is two questions: **what has to remain true for this to still work, and does the change I am about to ship break it.** Instance: the production rollback that restores the five-value `ck_modules_kind` constraint — it is a true revert only until the first row carrying `kind='mcp'` exists, and the SAME packet ships the frontend palette that creates such a row, so the rollback EXPIRES the moment the change succeeds (it then fails its validating ADD, safely but uselessly). A rollback is a PERMISSION TO UNDO, and this one has an expiry the change itself sets.

40. RE-RUN THE GATES WHEN THE INTERVAL BETWEEN VERIFICATION AND COMMIT WAS LONG ENOUGH FOR THE TREE TO MOVE — NAME THE INTERVAL. (The third of one lesson beside 39: a HOLD, a VERDICT and a RE-VERIFICATION are all claims about what someone else may STOP DOING.) **THE THING THAT DECAYS IS NOT THE CHANGE BUT THE EVIDENCE ABOUT IT:** the diff is the same bytes, but the tree it sits in is SHARED, and a sibling commit or a hook window can change what a build or a test actually exercised. So a commit made in the SAME MOVEMENT as its verification needs no re-run, and one that WAITED needs its gates renewed — the same shape as the pathspec rule (18), scoping a claim to what was measured INCLUDING WHEN. Case and cost accounting (2026-10-06): two rows sat four hours in a shared tree, and at commit time go-dev re-ran `gofmt`, the build and the four affected packages on the CURRENT tree — **NO LOSS, ONE EXTRA VERIFICATION**; the four hours cost ONE RE-GATE PER COMMIT, which is cheap, while committing into a frozen window would have cost the owner an ABORTED PUSH RUN. **ONE GATE RUN AGAINST ONE ABORTED DEPLOY is not a close comparison** — that is the honest way to price a hold. **A CLAUSE ON THIS RULE — the same rule applied to the EVIDENCE rather than to the change (2026-10-06; credited to fe-dev, confirmed by the reviewer): A TEST RESULT IS EVIDENCE ABOUT A TREE STATE, NOT ABOUT A COMMIT.** The instance is unusually clean because the difference IS the proof: the reviewer ran `52ff19e9`'s test on a **dirty tree** and got **16 with 1 failed**, while a **clean worktree at the same commit** gives **15 passed and no error** — so the count difference establishes that the in-flight edit had ADDED the case, and a reader given only the number could not tell the two states apart. fe-dev's own sample spans tree states because other seats committed mid-loop (**1755 to 1758**), and it **killed the remaining runs once the tree was moving**, on the ground that **a count from a moving tree measures nothing**. Same instrument question as rules 15/34: a number without the state it was taken in is not a fact about the commit. **THE FAMILY'S INDEX (2026-10-06 — go-dev's, in his words, and it is the HEADER the three clauses below belong under rather than a fourth clause beside them, because it is what makes them memorable as ONE family rather than three rules): THE THREE POSITIONS BETWEEN A CLAIM AND ITS READER ARE** — (i) **WRONG TIME**: the claim was **TRUE WHEN TAKEN** and reads as **CURRENT** *(the present-tense clause below is this case)*; (ii) **WRONG SUBJECT**: the claim was about the file or object **ADJACENT** to the one meant — **the repo-root path, the one-ended sample** *(the attachment clause below is this case)*; (iii) **WRONG DIMENSION**: the claim described a measurement's **FLAT VALUE without the pattern or boundary that makes it interpretable** — **the count without its rule, the number without its instant** *(the blind-instrument parent on rule 16 is this case)*. **AND EACH HAS THE SAME REPAIR SHAPE: SAY WHEN IT WAS MEASURED, WHAT IT WAS MEASURED ON, AND HOW IT WAS MEASURED** — one sentence in a message, and **the difference between A FACT AND A PLAUSIBLE SENTENCE**, which is **the whole night's finding in a line: every defect we chased was a sentence that was PLAUSIBLE AND UNMEASURED in exactly one of those three directions.** **A CLAUSE ON THIS RULE, with a packet document as its instance (2026-10-06): A RECORD MAY DESCRIBE THE PAST; IT MUST NOT DESCRIBE A PRESENT THAT HAS PASSED.** The frozen `DEPLOY-READY.md` carried packet-1 paragraphs whose PRESENT-TENSE sentences claimed that nothing had committed, that the tip was still `1b4ce29b`, that the push never ran, that production was `0.0.18` and that `origin/main` was `89feea4a` — **every one true at its date and false by the time the same document's new tip line asked the loop to act**, so the file asserted **TWO INCOMPATIBLE STATES ABOUT WHETHER A PACKET EXISTED**, and the stale sentence was about the very thing the tip asks the loop to push. **The fix is the DDL precedent: KEEP THE HISTORY, REMOVE THE PRESENT-TENSE CLAIM** — retire the old paragraphs under their own historical heading and mark the supersession in place, so a reader learns the transition (frozen → recalled → pushed → DDL run) without being told a present that has passed. This is rule 40's own decay, applied to a document's tense rather than to a test count. **THE MECHANISM (credited to the lead, 2026-10-06): A RECORD IS WRITTEN ONCE AND READ AT MANY MOMENTS**, so every sentence in it that describes the PRESENT carries an **EXPIRY ITS AUTHOR CANNOT SEE** — the author's present is the write, the reader's present is whenever they arrive. **THE RULE: A RECORD WITH A MUTABLE CLAIM SHOULD HAVE EXACTLY ONE PRESENT-TENSE SLOT, AND EVERYTHING ELSE DATED.** `DEPLOY-READY.md` already had the shape — the TIP LINE is the designated present and the rule at its head says so — and the failure was present-tense sentences surviving **OUTSIDE the slot**, in a region a reader takes as terms. **So it RUNS AS A CHECK rather than a judgement: grep the file for state verbs and infinitives about the current moment, and for each hit ask whether it is INSIDE the designated slot or BEHIND A DATE** — anything current outside the slot is a claim that will rot, and its rot is invisible to whoever wrote it. **THE THREE INSTANCES ARE ONE DEFECT WITH DIFFERENT SUBJECTS:** the authorisation section described a present authority that had been refused; the preview-versus-stored read described a present state that disagreed with what was stored; and the packet's present tense described a packet that had moved past it. **IN EACH CASE THE SENTENCE WAS TRUE WHEN WRITTEN — which is why every author was right and every record was wrong.** Run on the frozen file, the check returned **FOUR CANDIDATES AND NO SURVIVOR** once the three were fixed (the file's own rule at its head, an attributed measurement, a conditional about the mechanism, and the supersession marker itself) — **so the count really was three, and the search that would have found a fourth found none.** **THE ANALOGY ACROSS ARTEFACT TYPES, which is why this generalises: for a FILE, CONTENT IS IDENTITY AND MTIME IS HISTORY; for a DOCUMENT, THE DESIGNATED SLOT IS IDENTITY AND EVERYTHING DATED IS HISTORY** — the same shape in a different artefact: **the mutable thing gets ONE NAMED PLACE and everything else is evidence about a moment.** **A SECOND CLAUSE ON THIS RULE — the same family with a DIFFERENT failure, and the first finding of the night about ATTACHMENT rather than TRUTH (found 2026-10-06 by the reviewer AGAINST ITS OWN APPROVAL): A CLAIM'S SUBJECT IS INHERITED FROM ITS POSITION, NOT FROM ITS WORDS.** The sentence at the centre of it — 'the coverage sweep returned ZERO UNCERTIFIED on the tip it read (`2cafa873`, 51 commits above `origin/main`)' — **IS TRUE, every number in it was measured, and it is MIS-ATTACHED because its subject comes from a heading that released it: READING THE SENTENCE CAN ONLY CONFIRM ITS TRUTH, AND ONLY ITS PATH TELLS YOU WHAT IT IS ABOUT.** So the question is not 'is this sentence true' but **'WHAT IS THIS SECTION'S SUBJECT, AND DOES EVERY NUMBER UNDER IT BELONG TO THAT SUBJECT'**, asked by a single pass over the WHOLE FILE — every heading listed with the packet identifier appearing in the lines beneath it, **so anything unlabelled or mislabelled FALLS OUT OF THE LIST rather than out of somebody's memory** (this one was found by accident while chasing a different finding). The instance: `DEPLOY-READY.md`'s packet-1 label was written as an **H3 nested under the tip's H2**, so it scoped only its own subsection — the next H2 closed both and left everything below TOP-LEVEL and unlabelled, including a sweep paragraph carrying **packet 1's figures** (`2cafa873`, 51 commits above `origin/main`, 24 code commits certified) under a tip line naming **this** packet as `32ce0294` with six, and a SECOND `DEPLOY STEPS` block where one of the two is packet 1's and neither says which. **WHY THE PRESENT-TENSE CLAUSE ABOVE DID NOT CATCH IT: that clause's check searches SENTENCE CONTENT for state verbs, and this defect lives in the HIERARCHY ABOVE THE SENTENCES — markdown scope is set by HEADING LEVEL, so a label at one level cannot scope content at a shallower one. The instrument was a content search; the thing it could not see was structure** — the same shape as a case-sensitive grep run to deny a case-insensitive claim. **THE CHECK IS ONE QUESTION PER HEADING: WHICH PACKET'S NUMBERS APPEAR UNDER IT.** Both the reviewer and this seat read the label and accepted that it scoped what it claimed; neither compared the level against where the content actually sits — **which is why a rule written specifically to catch stale claims did not see this one, and why the failure is invisible to an author who knows what they meant.** **THE FIX IS DEFERRED BY DECISION RATHER THAN OMITTED:** the tip line is live and is what the loop acts on, nothing below the H2 boundary affects the push, and a write while the loop may be reading the file is a cost we refused once tonight on a nit — so it is the **FIRST ITEM OF THE NEXT REVISION** of that file, together with the deferred wording nit (`still serving` becomes `was serving at the reviewer's read`), and the test stated above is what runs on it. **AN INSTANCE ON THIS CLAUSE, AND THE WRONG-OBJECT AXIS WITH ITS SHARP TEST (2026-10-06; go-dev's generalisation from tonight's auth contradiction, in his words): A CONFIGURATION FILE IS A CLAIM ABOUT A PROCESS, NOT THE PROCESS.** It says what someone **INTENDED** the process to be started with; **the only authority on what it WAS started with is the process itself** — `/proc/<pid>/environ`, a marker it reports, or its own logs. **That is why the contradiction survived CAREFUL READERS ON BOTH SIDES: both claims were read correctly, and only one was about a RUNNING THING.** **Same family as the vintage clause, DIFFERENT AXIS** — there the claim was **OLD**, here the claim was about the **WRONG OBJECT** — and **the repair has the same shape: name what you measured, and say whether it was a FILE or a RUNNING PROCESS.** **AND THE GENERALISATION IS WORTH A LINE BECAUSE IT PREDICTS THE NEXT INSTANCE: WHENEVER A CLAIM AND AN ARTIFACT DISAGREE AND NEITHER READER MADE A MISTAKE, THE FIRST QUESTION IS WHETHER THEY ARE ABOUT THE SAME KIND OF THING** — **a file versus a process, a copy versus the original, a preview versus the stored record, a plan versus the build.** The attachment clause is about a claim in the wrong **PLACE**; this is a claim about the wrong **KIND** — **the same failure seen from the sentence and from the object.** **AND A THIRD INSTANCE ON THIS CLAUSE, AND IT IS THE FAMILY'S FIRST MEDIUM WHERE THE READER IS NOT A PERSON (2026-10-06; go-dev's tool-description fix `80c3ac2b`, one line):** the `set_occupant` tool's own parameter description read **"Model id; empty uses the runtime default"** — **the CREATE semantics, which is FALSE on an UPDATE, where the stored model is KEPT** — so **an agent that read the description and sent an empty string to RESET a model would have found the seat unchanged and NO ERROR.** The fix **states both operations where the sentence lives**: empty means the runtime default **on CREATE**, is **IGNORED on UPDATE with the stored model kept**, and **CANNOT be used to reset a model** — **no behaviour change and NO TEST, for the reason the copy commit gave: a test asserting the WORDING passes whether or not the sentence is TRUE.** **THE GENERAL FORM, in go-dev's words: A TOOL DESCRIPTION IS PROMPT-FACING TEXT AN AUTONOMOUS CALLER ACTS ON** — **a human reads a wrong hint and ADAPTS; an agent reads a wrong contract and BELIEVES IT** — so **the same defect that is a minor inaccuracy in a UI becomes A SILENT NO-OP IN AN AGENT'S HANDS.** **AND THE REVIEW QUESTION IT GIVES FOR EVERY TOOL SCHEMA: not 'is this description accurate' but 'WHAT WOULD AN AGENT DO IF IT ACTED ON THIS SENTENCE, AND IS THAT THE BEHAVIOUR'.** go-dev's closing line is the precise one and is kept verbatim: **THE FAILURE WAS NOT THAT THE SENTENCE WAS VAGUE — IT WAS THAT THE SENTENCE WAS PRECISE AND WRONG ABOUT WHICH OPERATION IT DESCRIBED.** **AND THE SHARPENING THAT CHANGES THE REPAIR RATHER THAN DECORATING IT (2026-10-06; go-dev's): IN EVERY OTHER MEDIUM OF THIS FAMILY THE FIX IS TO MAKE THE RECORD MORE ACCURATE FOR A HUMAN — a person who is misinformed notices the friction and adapts — BUT HERE THE READER EXECUTES WHAT IT READS, SO ACCURACY IS NOT SUFFICIENT: THE DESCRIPTION MUST ALSO STATE WHAT A CORRECT ACTION WOULD BE.** That is **why the useful sentence in the new text is the NEGATIVE one — "an empty value CANNOT be used to reset a model" — rather than the descriptive pair.** **go-dev's distinction: A HUMAN NEEDS TO KNOW WHAT THE PARAMETER MEANS; AN AGENT NEEDS TO KNOW WHAT WILL HAPPEN IF IT USES IT.** **AND THE TEST THAT FOLLOWS, cheap enough to apply by READING: FOR EVERY PARAMETER, WRITE DOWN THE ACTION AN AGENT WOULD TAKE FROM THE DESCRIPTION AND CHECK IT AGAINST THE SERVICE** — and **where the two disagree, THE DESCRIPTION IS THE DEFECT EVEN WHEN THE CODE IS RIGHT, because the caller has only the description.** **The same asymmetry as the reason text that never reached the chip, one layer further: THERE the client had a reason it could not show; HERE the caller has a contract that is not true.** **AND THE DISAGREEMENT METHOD, which is this family's COMMON EXPLANATION rather than a new rule (2026-10-06; go-dev's): A DISAGREEMENT BETWEEN TWO CORRECT CLAIMS IS ALMOST NEVER ABOUT WHO READ IT WRONG — IT IS ABOUT WHICH OBJECT EACH WAS READING**, and the repair is **to make the OBJECT part of the sentence rather than to argue about the conclusion.** The instance: **two seats measured OPPOSITE things on the same process** — one watched the **handshake complete**, one watched the **outcome close** — and a **three-field probe settled it by naming the path, the port, and whether the connection was open AFTER HOLDING**, so **the fields that make two runs comparable are precisely the ones that name the object.** **THE CONNECTION, stated once because it turns four instances into one idea: the vintage clause, the file-versus-process instance, the preview-versus-stored read, and the socket disagreement are ALL THE SAME FAILURE — THE READERS AGREED ABOUT THE WORDS AND DISAGREED ABOUT THE OBJECT — and every repair applied tonight ADDS THE OBJECT TO THE CLAIM rather than improving the argument.** **A THIRD CLAUSE ON THIS RULE, and it is the FAMILY'S ROOT (2026-10-06; go-dev's general form, in his words because they are better than any paraphrase): A CLAIM CARRIES A VINTAGE, AND A CLAIM THAT DOES NOT STATE ITS VINTAGE WILL BE READ AS CURRENT.** It is the **same defect as a count without its pattern and a hash without its mtime, ONE STEP EARLIER IN THE CHAIN** — before a number is wrong or a sentence is mis-attached, the reader has no way to know **WHEN** the claim was taken. **THE FOUR INSTANCES ARE ALL FROM ONE DAY, which is the evidence that this is a LIVE FAILURE MODE rather than a tidy principle — and not one of them was found by looking for it:** (i) the design row that assumed a fix had not landed; (ii) the push line asserted from memory; (iii) the citable count inherited rather than measured; (iv) an auth observation older than the commits that changed its own mechanism. **IN EVERY CASE THE CONTENT WAS TRUE WHEN TAKEN, and the reader had no way to tell it was old except by re-measuring.** **THE CHEAP REPAIR IS THE SAME EVERYWHERE AND IS THE WHOLE CLAUSE: DATE THE CLAIM, OR SAY WHAT TREE YOU MEASURED** — so a reader can tell current from recalled without running anything. That is what already works in this rig: **a hash with its mtime, a count with its tree, a measurement with its moment, a claim with its vintage.** **AND THE THREE CLAUSES ON THIS RULE ARE ONE FAMILY AT THREE POSITIONS IN THE CHAIN FROM SENTENCE TO READER:** a claim can carry the wrong **TIME** (this clause — no vintage), be attached to the wrong **SUBJECT** (the attachment clause — its path, not its words), or be read through the wrong **DIMENSION** (the blind-check parent on rule 16 — the instrument that cannot see it). **AND ITS CONSTRUCTIVE HALF, stated as a PROPERTY rather than a trick (2026-10-06; go-dev's): ANY BUILD-IDENTIFYING VALUE THAT TRAVELS WITH THE ARTIFACT DATES THAT ARTIFACT.** A deploy marker is an **IDENTITY checkable at BOTH ENDS of a push** — the pusher reads it before, the operator reads it after — and a **THIRD** reader can ask *which bytes is this process* without rebuilding anything. **That is why it answers a vintage question at all: it was DESIGNED to be an identity a human can see, and PROVENANCE IS THAT SAME QUESTION ASKED LATER.** So where this clause says a claim without a vintage will be read as current, this names the **cheapest structural fix: have the artifact carry its own build identity, so the vintage travels WITH the thing rather than in a sentence beside it.** The concrete instance is live rather than hypothetical: `curl` on the running process returns `healthVersion`, and that constant maps through the commit history to the commit that bumped it — **which is how an auth observation's vintage is being settled right now by a one-command read rather than a rebuild.** **AND THE CLAUSE'S FIRST SAVE, recorded because the instance is the RULE'S OWN AUTHOR (2026-10-06, fe-dev):** about to report that the two halves of a bridge commit **disagreed** — the client parsing **per-seat verdicts** from an answer that carried none — fe-dev **asked the ANCESTRY question FIRST and found that its own BINARY PREDATED THE SERVER HALF that adds the verdicts field.** So **the gap was the INSTRUMENT, not the product**, and a rule written hours earlier **prevented a false finding** — the strongest available evidence that **a vintage question is cheaper than a wrong report.** **AND ITS NEWEST MEDIUM IS ARITHMETIC RATHER THAN PROSE (2026-10-06; go-dev's count reconciliation): AN ARITHMETIC RESULT CARRIES THE TREE IT WAS COMPUTED ON AND EXPIRES WHEN THE TREE CHANGES.** The instance: go-dev quoted **"1,182 minus the two non-Go names"** in the gate row **AFTER** the file deletion, so the figure had been computed against **a tree that no longer existed** — **in a row whose entire subject is scope and counts.** The repair is this family's usual one — **SAY WHICH TREE, OR RECOMPUTE** — and the honest statement is that **a number computed before a deletion and quoted after it is a claim about a tree that is GONE.** **AND THE STRONGEST EVIDENCE THAT THIS CLAUSE CHANGES DECISIONS RATHER THAN DESCRIBING THEM (2026-10-06): THE SAME RULE WAS USED TWICE IN ONE HOUR IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS** — once by fe-dev to **STOP A FALSE GAP BEING FILED** (its binary PREDATED the server half that added the field) and once to **CLOSE A REAL ONE** (the fix was NEWER than BOTH binaries the verifier had run, so **the gap was STALE BY CONSTRUCTION** and the probe only confirmed what the ancestry already predicted). **A rule that both PREVENTS a report and CONFIRMS a closure in the same hour is not describing a good practice — it is OPERATING ON DECISIONS.**

41. A DDL STEP CARRIES ITS OWN READ-YOUR-DATA LINE. (The owner's STANDING REQUIREMENT, recorded 2026-10-06.) Beside the statement, in the same place a reader meets it: **what it touches**, **whether it is idempotent or must run exactly once**, **what a re-run would do**, **the rollback WITH its expiry boundary** (that boundary being **rule 39's principle APPLIED** — what has to remain true for the revert to still work, and whether the change itself breaks it, which is why this checklist CITES 39 rather than restating it), and **THE OBSERVED DISTRIBUTION** — so the FACT TRAVELS WITH THE DDL and nobody derives it at execution time. **This rule exists because it was VIOLATED once, not because it was proposed:** the version the owner read at 05:50 did NOT carry these, which is why the freeze was recalled at 05:51 and the block rewritten with its safety facts, its time-bounded rollback and its read-only validation step. **The instance that closes it:** the owner then read the distribution themselves — **52 `instruction`, 9 `document`, 1 `skill`, 1 `tool`, 63 rows, every one inside the new set** — agreeing EXACTLY with two earlier probes by a seat; the DDL ran, and the constraint reads back including `mcp`.

42. A TEST OF A DESTRUCTIVE PATH ASSERTS THE DIFF, NOT THE TARGET — AND A GATED TEST DRIVES THE SHIPPED COMPOSITION. (2026-10-06, from the DELETE-path test, promoted here because these are CONVENTIONS THE NEXT AUTHOR SHOULD COPY WITHOUT BEING TOLD.) **HALF ONE: WHEN A PATH REMOVES ROWS, ASSERT WHAT THE WHOLE OPERATION CHANGED, NOT WHAT IT WAS AIMED AT.** The strong form is not "the deleted table's count went down" but **"THE ENTIRE CHANGE SET EQUALS EXACTLY THIS STRING"**: **one helper counts EVERY TABLE IN A LIST and NAMES EVERY TABLE WHOSE COUNT MOVED, and the assertion compares THAT WHOLE DIFF against ONE LITERAL.** **THE CONSEQUENCE IS THE POINT: AN OVER-BROAD DELETE FAILS AS LOUDLY AS A MISSING ONE, at the cost of ONE HELPER.** **HALF TWO: A GATED TEST THAT CONSTRUCTS ITS OWN WIRING PROVES THE WIRING IT CONSTRUCTED, NOT THE ONE THAT SHIPS** — so it drives **THE SHIPPED COMPOSITION** (the file's own words: it mirrors the route's wiring "rather than a second convention"), **which is what stops a test from passing while the route fails.** **AND TWO CONVENTIONS BESIDE THEM, worth copying unchanged: THE SKIP FORM MATCHES ITS NEIGHBOUR — it NAMES THE VARIABLE and reaches NO FALLBACK — and THE LAYER DIVISION IS DECLARED IN A COMMENT, so the file says WHICH LAYER asserts the ROW COUNTS and WHICH asserts the HTTP STATUS, and the next author does not duplicate the mount tests.**

43. A DELETION TAKES THE FALSE CLAIM, NOT THE COMMENT — AND A CONTRACT-BOUND DELEGATE'S EMPTY RESULT IS AMBIGUOUS. (2026-10-06, both from the identity pass; the second sits beside the silent-delegate rule.) **HALF ONE: A DELETION TAKES THE FALSE CLAIM, NOT THE COMMENT.** The shape, recorded because it is now the **THIRD time tonight that an all-or-nothing edit would have destroyed something true: a false sentence sat beside a TRUE CAVEAT explaining why a method's signature differs from its facade's, and the deletion took the CLAUSE rather than the LINE.** The same shape appeared in the seven-line pass (a false first sentence beside a true second one) and in the cleanup's remainder — **so the rule is stated plainly: DELETE THE FALSE HALF AND LEAVE ANY TRUE CAVEAT STANDING; IF A LINE MIXES THEM, THE EDIT IS A CLAUSE.** **AND THE THIRD CONSTRAINT, SO THE PASS'S EDITING RULE IS A TRIO RATHER THAN A PAIR (2026-10-06; from the port-prose pass's last commit): A TRUE NOTE THAT CAN BE READ AS A LARGER ABSENCE GETS THE ONE CLAUSE THAT NAMES WHAT EXISTS.** **IT IS NEITHER A STATUS CLAIM — which the pass forbade — NOR A DELETED CAVEAT — which it also forbade — BUT THE THIRD OPTION, and its instance is A REGISTRATION LINE THAT IS TRUE ABOUT THE FUNCTION WHILE THE TOOL ITSELF IS DISPATCHED, so a reader could conclude a capability is ABSENT WHEN IT IS LIVE, and the clause NAMES THE DISPATCH SITE INSTEAD.** **THE THREE TOGETHER ARE THE PASS'S EDITING RULE: NO FALSE HALF LEFT, NO TRUE CAVEAT DESTROYED, AND NO TRUE NOTE LEFT MISREADABLE.** **HALF TWO, NEW AND BESIDE THE SILENT-DELEGATE RULE: A SCOUT WAS GIVEN A STRICT OUTPUT CONTRACT IT COULD NOT SATISFY — it LOOPED ON A SCHEMA MISMATCH and returned NOTHING FOR FOURTEEN LINES.** **THE POINT IS NOT THAT IT FAILED BUT THAT ITS FAILURE WAS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM A SEARCH THAT FOUND NOTHING: AN EMPTY RESULT FROM A CONTRACT-BOUND DELEGATE CAN MEAN THE WORLD IS EMPTY OR THAT THE INSTRUMENT NEVER RAN.** **And the prescription the seat applied, recorded as the recovery: REPORT THE BATCH AS FAILED AND UNMEASURED RATHER THAN CLASSIFYING IT; REDO IT WITH A CONTRACT THAT CAN BE MET; and LEAVE PARTIALLY MEASURED LINES UNCLASSIFIED RATHER THAN ASSUMING THEM.** **The seat that hit it NAMED IT RATHER THAN COVERING IT, which is why this is a rule and not a rumour.** **AND THE BOUNDARY EPISODE, worth its clause for how the work is organised: three lines measured false were HELD because they sat OUTSIDE THE GROUP THE LEAD HAD DECLARED OUT OF SCOPE, and were edited only when the word came — A SEAT THAT EDITS OUTSIDE ITS SCOPE BECAUSE THE EVIDENCE LOOKS GOOD IS THE SEAT WHOSE DIFFS NOBODY CAN ATTRIBUTE.**

44. A CONTRACT THAT ASKS FOR A VERDICT WHERE THE ANSWER NEEDS A QUESTION PRODUCES SILENCE. (2026-10-06; the THIRD delegation failure diagnosed tonight, and the same failure as rule 43's second half with a different cause.) **THE EVIDENCE IS CLEAN AND IT IS THE SAME FOURTEEN LINES TWICE: under a STRICT OUTPUT SCHEMA the scout returned NOTHING FOR FOURTEEN LINES, and under PLAIN PROSE WITH PER-GROUP DECISIVE QUESTIONS it returned FOURTEEN CLASSIFIED ANSWERS AND ZERO "COULD NOT TELL".** **THREE THINGS DIFFERED AND ONLY ONE IS THE FIX: (a) the SCHEMA was REMOVED; (b) an EXPLICIT "COULD NOT TELL" OPTION WAS OFFERED AND DESCRIBED AS A VALID ANSWER; and (c) THE QUESTIONS BECAME PER-GROUP AND DECISIVE — is there a file of that name in the same package, is that hook EVER assigned, is this "the module has no file" or "the construction is impossible".** **THE THIRD IS THE FIX; THE OTHER TWO ARE THE SHAPE THAT LETS IT WORK.** **AND THE CORROBORATION THAT MAKES IT A DIAGNOSIS RATHER THAN A STORY: THE "COULD NOT TELL" OPTION WAS NEVER TAKEN — so THE QUESTION WAS ANSWERABLE ALL ALONG and THE FIRST CONTRACT WAS THE ONLY OBSTACLE.** **THE GENERAL FORM, beside the silent-delegate rule: AN EMPTY RESULT FROM A CONTRACT-BOUND DELEGATE CAN MEAN THE WORLD IS EMPTY, THE INSTRUMENT NEVER RAN, OR THE QUESTION WAS UNASKABLE.**

45. A ROW BLOCKED ON THE ROUTER IS NOT THE SAME AS A ROW BLOCKED ON THE ANSWER. (2026-10-06, from a watchdog wake that resurfaced five parked rows at once.) **THE DIFFERENCE IS VISIBLE ONLY IN THE BLOCKER FIELD: every one of the five was blocked ON THE LEAD and NONE of it was actually the lead's work — they were REQUESTS, DEFECTS AND OBSERVATIONS that needed ROUTING, and THE CORRECT END STATE IS A ROW WHOSE BLOCKER NAMES WHAT IT TRULY AWAITS.** **After routing, all five read as THREE AUTHORITY GATES (an owner decision) and TWO EXTERNAL GATES (a fix or a call belonging to another system) — and a ROUTER-HELD ROW IS INVISIBLE in a sweep that only asks how long a row has been held.** **The practical form: WHEN A ROW'S BLOCKER IS "WAITING ON ME" AND THE WORK IS NOT MINE, THE ACTION IS A ROUTING ACT, NOT A DECISION — and a queue whose blockers do not distinguish the two will keep reporting THE ROUTER as the bottleneck.** **AND ITS COMPANION FROM THE SAME WAKE, because it is the same field seen from the other side: A DIRECTIVE ROW UNCLAIMED FOR SIXTY-ONE MINUTES WITH ITS SEAT IDLE AT THE PROMPT IS A CLAIM FAILURE RATHER THAN A BLOCK** — the owner was woken with **the go-ahead, the first deliverable, the two serialized lines that come through another seat, and the acceptance shape**, and **THE SWEEP'S FINDING CLOSES ITSELF ONCE THE ROW IS CLAIMED.** **Record the ASSIGNMENT AND ITS STATE, not the row's creation, since the row was already on the board and only its claim was missing.** **AND ITS SECOND INSTANCE, WHICH NAMES THE DURATION THAT IS NOT THE RELEVANT UNIT (2026-10-06): A WATCH PARKED ON A RECURRENCE IS REALLY PARKED ON WHATEVER WOULD MAKE THE RECURRENCE MATTER.** The state after **SEVENTEEN FULL-SUITE RUNS WITH THE FAILING STRING APPEARING ZERO TIMES** and **the first fully green run in hours**: the row **NOW EITHER RETIRES WITH THE PIPELINE FINDING OR REVIVES WITH THE PIPELINE WIRING, AND NEITHER DEPENDS ON TIME PASSING.** **So WHEN THE RELEVANCE IS A DECISION, THE ROW SHOULD SAY SO RATHER THAN COUNTING HOURS** — the second time tonight a parked thing turned out to depend on a decision rather than on a duration.

46. AFTER A PARK OR A REPAIR, RE-READ ALL THREE FACTS — WHAT IT WAITS FOR, THAT IT IS PARKED, AND THAT A WAKE IS ARMED. (2026-10-06; found by reading five rows back after a re-park, and then sharpened by a row that read correct in two fields while its third was blank. The title was "re-read the blocker field" until its second half showed one field is not enough, and then "re-read both fields" until its third half showed the check is not a count of non-empty fields.) **A PARK CAN ARM THE WAKE AND CLEAR THE GATE, SO A ROW THAT LOOKS PARKED MAY HAVE LOST THE ONLY FIELD THAT SAYS WHAT IT IS WAITING FOR.** The instance: five rows were re-parked through the **BLOCK** verb with a typed gate and a two-hour timer, and **THE READ-BACK SHOWED THE TIMER ARMED AND THE BLOCKER FIELD EMPTY ON ALL FIVE — THE PARK ACCEPTED THE GATE ARGUMENT AND DID NOT PERSIST IT** — so the rows read as blocked **with a wake and no stated reason**, which is exactly the state rule 45 says must be visible in that field. **Re-applying the gate through the UPDATE verb RESTORED ALL FIVE, and both facts are now present together, VERIFIED BY READING EACH ROW BACK.** **AND THE GENERAL SHAPE, beside the night's other instrument findings: this is NOT an instrument answering the wrong question but A WRITE THAT SUCCEEDED PARTIALLY WHILE REPORTING SUCCESS — the same family as A COMMIT THAT CAPTURES MORE THAN IT WAS ASKED FOR, ONE LAYER DOWN.** **AND THE GENERAL FORM FOR ANYONE USING THE QUEUE: A PARK IS TWO FACTS — WHAT IT WAITS FOR AND WHEN IT WILL WALK BACK — and a command that sets one of them can quietly drop the other, so THE ONLY SAFE BELIEF IS A READ-BACK OF BOTH.** **AND ITS SECOND HALF, WORSE THAN THE FIRST, WHICH TURNS THE RULE FROM ONE FIELD INTO TWO (2026-10-06): THE TWO VERBS CLEAR EACH OTHER'S FIELD.** **MEASURED: BLOCK ARMS THE WAKE AND CLEARS THE BLOCKER FIELD; UPDATE RESTORES THE BLOCKER FIELD AND, ON ITS OWN, DROPS THE TIMED BACKSTOP** — so **repairing the gate with one command REMOVED THE WAKE THE OTHER HAD JUST ARMED**, which is why the rows **KEPT FIRING WHILE READING AS PARKED**, **and why A READ-BACK THAT CHECKS ONLY ONE FIELD KEEPS PASSING.** **THE CONFIGURATION THAT HOLDS BOTH IS A SINGLE UPDATE CALL WITH BOTH ARGUMENTS — THE STATE,
