53. A PACKAGE THAT DOES NOT COMPILE IS NOT A PRIVATE WORK-IN-PROGRESS — IT IS A SHARED RESOURCE HELD BROKEN. (2026-10-06; measured TWICE in one shift, in both directions, and it is **rule 27's SIBLING rather than its clause**: same family — the shared tree — and a different instrument, **THE COMPILER rather than the stash**.) **THE TWO INSTANCES: go-dev2's mid-edit `seat_admin_mount.go` stopped go-dev compiling, and then go-dev's UNTRACKED `submit_feedback_wiring.go` stopped go-dev2 — the second being a SINGLE UNUSED IMPORT (`seatservices` imported, zero references) while the author's own edits type-checked, so the only proof available was a PACKAGE-LEVEL build (`go build ./...` reported nothing but that line).** **AND THE ASYMMETRY THAT MAKES IT A RULE RATHER THAN A NAG: THE AUTHOR'S OWN FILE CAN BE PERFECTLY VALID WHILE THE PACKAGE IS BROKEN, so the failure lands on whoever builds NEXT rather than on whoever caused it** — the same displacement rule 27 describes, one layer out, and the reason a sibling's red build reads as ITS OWN mistake until someone says otherwise. **THE PRACTICE: keep each package compiling at every step while you work in it; and when you must leave one broken — an untracked file mid-write, a rename half-applied — SAY SO IN ONE LINE to the seat that depends on it.** **THE RECOVERY is rule 27's (c) again: when you find a sibling's package broken, NAME IT rather than working around it quietly.** **PLACEMENT NOTE: this rule postdates the rules-reconciliation block above, which is a dated snapshot of the rules then existing rather than a live index.** **ITS COMPLEMENT, measured the same night by go-dev (2026-10-06) — WHAT TO DO WHEN SOMEONE ELSE HOLDS A PACKAGE BROKEN: A PRISTINE-COPY RUN GIVES BOTH PROVENANCE AND IMMUNITY.** **When go-dev2's mid-edit D5 work left the `httpapp` TEST binary uncompilable, go-dev ran its own tests FIRST at HEAD in an export (`git archive HEAD` plus its own files, under `/tmp`) and then re-checked the binary in the LIVE tree once the tree settled — which is how its commit landed WITH EVIDENCE while the package tests were unbuildable in place.** **AND THE DISCIPLINE THAT MAKES THAT HONEST RATHER THAN CONVENIENT: NAME WHICH RUNS WERE IN THE EXPORT AND WHICH WERE LIVE** — an export run is evidence about the commit's own bytes against HEAD, a live run is evidence about the tree, and a report that does not say which was which claims more than it measured. **AND THE PRECONDITION, which is the half that makes it non-trivial: THE EXPORT MUST INCLUDE THE SEAT'S OWN UNCOMMITTED FILES, or it proves nothing about them** — a bare `git archive HEAD` tests somebody else's code, not yours. **AND ITS WHOLE-TREE INSTANCE, measured (2026-10-06): AN UNTRACKED FILE IS INVISIBLE TO EVERY SEAT AND TO EVERY EXPORT, SO A TREE CAN BE GREEN FOR TEN SEATS AND BROKEN FOR THE REPOSITORY.** **The window, with its times: for the minutes before `947bb81b` committed `column_ensurers.go` at 18:53:54, that file existed ONLY AS AN UNTRACKED NEW FILE in the shared worktree — so the live tree compiled for every seat while THE COMMITTED HEAD DID NOT, and a pristine-export build of HEAD failed with `undefined: RunColumnEnsurers` (the writer's own check, ~18:52).** **AND THE GENERAL SHAPE, which is why it is stated rather than left as the incident: A LIVE BUILD IS A STATEMENT ABOUT ONE WORKTREE AND AN EXPORT IS A STATEMENT ABOUT THE REPOSITORY, and the two differ EXACTLY by the untracked files — invisible to the repository, to every other seat, and to every export.** **Consequence for any "is it certifiable" claim: THE CLAIM MUST CARRY THE MOMENT IT WAS TAKEN, because a claim taken inside such a window was taken against a tree that does not exist in the repository** — which is why the packet-4 docket states the moment of any certifiability verdict it carries.

54. A TYPE-GATE PARK HAS NO RESOLVER VERB — AN ANSWERED GATE IS INVISIBLE UNTIL SOMEBODY RECONCILES IT BY HAND. (2026-10-06; **the SECOND stale-against-the-fact state of the day, and both were found by a seat CHECKING rather than assuming** — the first was the TypeScript cleanup entry, corrected hours earlier.) **THE MECHANISM, as measured: `rig queue resolve` refuses any park whose `blockedOn` is not a HUMAN-SEAT session — the refusal names the row `qitem_not_leg1_parked` and the verb is documented as resolving "a leg-1 parked qitem (state=blocked on a human seat)".** **The exact refusal string was not found in the installed bundle (version 0.6.3) by this seat; the PREDICATE it turns on was: `isHumanSeatSessionRef` — a human seat matching `human…@(kernel|host)`, or the A2 virtual-domain leg — in the daemon's queue routes (`@openrig/cli/daemon/dist/routes/queue.js:642-651`).** **SO A ROW PARKED ON A TYPED GATE — `auth:owner <question>`, `fold:<what>`, `external:<what>` — STAYS BLOCKED AFTER THE ANSWER ARRIVES**, because the verb that unparks a leg-1 park will not accept it, **and NOTHING SURFACES IT: the stuck-sweep watches for UNCLAIMED and OVERDUE obligations, not for ANSWERED GATES.** **THE INSTANCE: `qitem-20261005185557` sat `blocked` on an owner answer the owner had given HOURS earlier (OWNER-DECISIONS.md item 5 — one team id, viewer read-only), while the seat it gated had been building the wiring for twenty minutes.** **The row is the ledger, and the ledger was wrong about the world in the direction that makes started work look unstarted.** **THE PRACTICE IS A MOVEMENT RATHER THAN A HABIT: WHEN AN OWNER ANSWER LANDS, THE SEATS HOLDING TYPED-GATE PARKS ON IT ARE RECONCILED IN THE SAME MOVEMENT — the row moved out of `blocked` and the decision text recorded in its transition log — OR THE ANSWER IS INVISIBLE IN THE ONE PLACE THAT IS SUPPOSED TO BE THE LEDGER.** **AND THE TELL FOR A READER: a `blocked` row whose `blockedOn` is a TYPED GATE is a claim about the PAST, not about the present — check whether the gate's condition has since been answered before treating the row as live work.**

55. A GREEN LOCAL GATE SET IS SILENT ABOUT WHETHER THE PROCESS STARTS. (2026-10-06; go-dev earned it the hard way, and it completes a family of three: **53** says do not hold a package broken, **53's complement** says run in an export when someone else has, and **THIS** says that even the WHOLE local gate set passing proves nothing about booting.) **THE INSTANCE, measured: `go build` green, `go vet` clean, the package tests green — AND THE SERVER COULD NOT START: `app: unknown table "seat_feedback"`, exit 1.** **THE MECHANISM, and the tree carries its own explanation because the seat that hit it wrote one into the code (`fastmcp/server/httpapp/app.go:60-64`): the controller CONSTRUCTS its repository at boot, `NewORMRepository` resolves the table BY NAME from the shared registry (the error is `unknown table %q`, `fastmcp/task_management/infrastructure/repositories/base_orm_repository.go:60`), and that table's `TableDef` sat in a DELIBERATELY HELD commit — so the code compiled against a NAME while the running process could not resolve it, "on every database, AUTO_MIGRATE true or false".** **AND WHY IT FOOLED EVERYTHING: THE GATES THIS PROJECT RUNS — gofmt, vet, build, test — ALL STOP SHORT OF BOOTING THE PROCESS, so a change that compiles and passes while killing startup REACHES THE TREE LOOKING GREEN.** **AND THE SHAPE OF THE MISTAKE TO CATCH NEXT TIME: the change was dismissed as "only a mount line", while the killer was the BOOT-TIME CONSTRUCTION of a controller inside `NewApp`, not the mount.** **SO THE QUESTION IS NOT "DOES THIS COMPILE" BUT "WHAT DOES STARTING THE PROCESS CONSTRUCT" — and the check that answers it is a boot, which no gate in this repository performs today (a separate row is expected for a boot check).** **The tree also models the honest interim: the wiring deliberately does NOT compose that controller until the table lands, and says so where the next reader meets it (`app.go:60-64`, and `:132` at the mount) — a held dependency named rather than a break discovered at boot.** **AND TWO COMPANIONS FROM THE REVIEWER'S PAIR SUPPLEMENT ON DIRECTIVE H (2026-10-06), each measured rather than supposed.** **(i) "SAFE IN TWO STEPS" AND "NO BROKEN COMMIT IN HISTORY" ARE DIFFERENT CLAIMS, AND ONLY THE FIRST IS TRUE — WITH THE BROKEN COMMIT EXPLAINED IN PLACE RATHER THAN REWRITTEN AWAY.** **`1b183904` composed the feedback controller and mounted its routes (`app.go:60`, `:69`, `:132`) while the `seat_feedback` TableDef sat in the held D5 slice, so it does not boot; `d41fba79` unmounted both; and the ruling is that `1b183904` STAYS IN HISTORY.** **THE ARGUMENT, recorded because the alternative looks tidier: a bisect that lands on it finds the break AND the explanation — its own message says the table is held, `app.go` carries the reason, and the repair is two minutes later — while rewriting history would rewrite the SHARED tree's HEAD with six seats working in it, a far worse hazard than an explained, measured, repaired commit that nobody checks out and that is not pushed.** **AND THE SENTENCE A READER NEEDS, VERIFIED BY EXECUTION RATHER THAN ASSERTED: HEAD BOOTS, AND A CHECKOUT OF THAT ONE COMMIT WILL NOT.** **Measured this hour from pristine exports (rule 53's complement): a build of `1b183904`, run with `AUTO_MIGRATE=false` against the scratch database, EXITS 1 with `app: unknown table "seat_feedback"`; a build of HEAD starts, listens, and answers `GET /health` with 200 and `version 0.0.22`.** **(ii) A HELD SPLIT HAS AN INTERMEDIATE STATE, AND IT SHOULD BE MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSUMED BENIGN.** **Between the unmount and the table landing, the ROUTE is not mounted (`mountSeatFeedbackRoutes` stands as a comment at `app.go:132`, while the function itself is defined at `seat_feedback_mount.go:72`), so that path answers 404 — BUT THE MCP TOOL IS STILL ADVERTISED, and every call answers HTTP 200 with `isError` true and `"SubmitFeedbackController not initialized"`: the schema is appended UNCONDITIONALLY (`mcp_routes.go:271-279`) while the dispatch refuses a nil controller (`mcp_routes.go:438-445`).** **Honest, discoverable, writes nothing, and unreachable once both lines land with the table — but it is the concrete reason the re-enable must restore BOTH lines IN THE SAME COMMIT AS THE `TableDef`, which is now a condition written onto the Directive H row.**

56. MEASURE THE TREE, NEVER A LIST — AND THE TWO REASONS THIS HAD TO BE LEARNED. (2026-10-06; go-dev's rule, after **TWO false gate sentences landed in ONE commit** (`1b183904`). It sits with rule 53's family: 53 is the compiler as an instrument, this is the FILE SET as one.) **THE TWO SENTENCES: "everything here compiles and is tested" was TRUE OF COMPILATION and FALSE OF THE ARTIFACT; "gofmt empty" was TRUE OF A HAND-WRITTEN FILE LIST and FALSE OF THE TREE.** **AND THE MEASURED FORM IS `git ls-files`-BASED — the file set comes from the REPOSITORY, not from the author's memory of what they touched.** **THE TWO INSTRUMENT FACTS THAT MAKE A LIST LOOK SAFE WHEN IT IS NOT:** **(a) A BARE `gofmt -l .` INSIDE `agenthub_go` REPORTS FILES THAT ARE NOT OURS.** **Measured here (2026-10-06): `.gomodcache/` lives INSIDE the module directory and holds 1,142 `.go` files, so the bare form returned 128 files — every one of them vendored — while `git ls-files '*.go' | xargs gofmt -l` returned 0.** **And the same command means two different things in two trees: in a FRESH EXPORT there is no cache, so the bare form reports only our own files (the lead measured 1 there against roughly a dozen in the shared tree), which is why "the same check" can hand two seats different answers.** **(b) THE PRE-COMMIT HOOKS ARE BLIND TO GO FORMATTING.** **The only config in the tree runs ruff, trailing-whitespace, end-of-file and YAML checks (`agenthub_main/.pre-commit-config.yaml`), so NOTHING catches a Go style miss at commit time — THE GATE IS THE SEAT THAT COMMITS, which is exactly why a list-shaped claim is the natural mistake here and why the repository-shaped measurement is the fix.**

57. A STATE CLAIM NAMES ITS INSTANCE BY ITS **DATA DIRECTORY**, AND A STOP IS VERIFIED BY THE **LISTENER'S ABSENCE** RATHER THAN BY THE COMMAND'S EXIT. (2026-10-06; **two seats, two CORRECT reports, one colliding sentence** — rule 11's family, "the boundary belongs inside the claim", applied to a PROCESS claim rather than a match claim.) **THE STORY, because it is the reusable part: go-dev2 reported "POSTGRES STOPPED, probe artefacts removed, no stray processes" as part of a clean, and go-dev measured, IN THE SAME MINUTE, that A postgres was up answering on `127.0.0.1:54331` and created and dropped a database on it. BOTH WERE RIGHT** — go-dev2 had stopped **its own** throwaway (the `d5pg` instance) and 54331 is **the OF4 verification stack's** instance, which it had never touched. **THEY COLLIDED BECAUSE BOTH ARE STARTED FROM THE SAME SHARED BINARY: `~/.cache/agenthub-testpg/bin/postgres` — re-read by this seat for this entry, `/proc/<pid>/exe` for the instance on 54331 resolves to exactly that path — so the two look alike in a process list**, and a sentence naming **neither a data directory nor a port** was readable as being about either.
**THE FIELD, AND WHY NOT THE OTHER TWO: THE DISCRIMINATOR IS THE DATA DIRECTORY.** **The BINARY is shared** (one binary served both instances), and **a PORT CAN BE REUSED by the next instance**, so a port is evidence about **a moment** rather than about **an instance**. Only the data directory is unique per instance. **The instance on 54331, read live: `postgres -D /tmp/of4pg/data -p 54331 -k /tmp/of4pg/sock`.**
**THE INSTRUMENT, which is where the claim becomes checkable: a stop verified by the command's EXIT CODE is a claim about THE COMMAND; a stop verified by `ss -lntp | grep <port>` RETURNING NOTHING is a claim about THE WORLD** — and only the second can be true while a database is answering. **Measured while writing this entry (2026-10-06, this seat): `ss -lntp` listed `127.0.0.1:54331` (postgres) and NOTHING on 54339** — the instance nobody touched is up, the one that was stopped is absent, and the two reports are reconciled by the instrument rather than by either author giving way.
**THE ONE-LINE FORM, from the rule's author** (go-dev2 adopted the rule and re-verified retroactively on its own, which is what makes it a rule rather than a correction): **`stopped: <binary> -D <datadir> -p <port>; verified afterwards because ss -ltnp shows no listener on that port AND no process carries that data directory.`**
**AND THE AUTHOR'S CLOSING SENTENCE IS THE GENERALISABLE HALF: "my phrasing error cost one round and my quoting error cost one more, both in the same class — THE SENTENCE NOT CARRYING THE FIELD IT NEEDS."** That is the same defect as the rule above and as rule 11: **the SENTENCE fails, not the fact.**
**THE QUOTING HALF, GENERALISED — AND THE HAZARD IS NOT "BACKTICKS IN DOUBLE QUOTES": IT IS THE QUOTING STYLE MEETING A CHARACTER THAT STYLE DOES NOT PROTECT.** **The mirror instance is what turns one seat's bug into a rule. TWO REAL INSTANCES IN ONE SHIFT: go-dev2 lost the MIDDLE of a message to BACKTICKS INSIDE DOUBLE QUOTES (command-substituted locally, EMPTY output, the transport innocent — nothing wrong with `rig send`, the loss happens before the call), and go-dev lost an ENTIRE SEND to AN APOSTROPHE INSIDE SINGLE QUOTES (unterminated quote, nothing sent at all).**
**THE TABLE: SINGLE quotes are safe against backticks and UNSAFE against APOSTROPHES; DOUBLE quotes are safe against apostrophes and UNSAFE against BACKTICKS, DOLLAR SIGNS AND COMMAND SUBSTITUTION; A QUOTED HEREDOC and the queue's `--body-file` form are safe against BOTH — and those two are what this rule recommends** (`rig queue create --body-file`'s own help says it exists to kill this class).
**AND A THIRD SAFE FORM, from fe-dev, WHICH GENERALISES THIS SEAT'S OWN HEREDOC HABIT: PASSING A FILE'S CONTENTS — `"$(cat <file>)"` — IS SAFE AGAINST BACKTICKS, APOSTROPHES AND DOLLARS ALIKE, because the expanded text IS NEVER RE-SCANNED: the substitution runs once, deliberately, and its result is DATA rather than shell input.**
**AND THE DETECTOR, WITHOUT WHICH A SILENT HOLE HAS NONE: VERIFY DELIVERY BY RECEIPT, NOT BY ECHO.** **fe-dev's two self-checks both looked at WHAT IT SENT and both came back weak for different reasons; the check that worked looked at WHAT ARRIVED.** **So for any message whose exactness matters, the verification is a read of the RECEIVED text rather than a re-read of the sent one.**
**AND THE DISTINCTION THAT MATTERS MORE THAN THE TABLE, in go-dev's words: THE TWO FAILURES HAVE OPPOSITE SHAPES — ONE IS LOUD (nothing sent; the author sees an error) and ONE IS SILENT (the middle is gone and the message ARRIVES LOOKING COMPLETE) — AND ONLY THE SILENT ONE REACHES THE RECIPIENT LOOKING WHOLE.** **That asymmetry is why this is worth a rule rather than a note: a LOUD failure costs a RETRY; a SILENT one costs a WRONG RECONSTRUCTION NOBODY CAN SEE.** **And the failure is CORRELATED WITH CONTENT — a message loses its middle exactly when it carries a command, a path or an example, which is when it carries the part someone acts on.**
**SO: IF YOU SEE A HOLE IN SOMETHING SENT TO YOU, SAY SO RATHER THAN RECONSTRUCTING IT SILENTLY — "SAYING SO IS CHEAPER THAN BEING RIGHT" (go-dev), who reached that by noticing its own reconstruction of a mangled handoff was correct and STILL made the record depend on its inference.** *(This seat's messages use the quoted-heredoc form — `cat > /tmp/m <<'MSG' … MSG` then `rig send <seat> "$(cat /tmp/m)"` — so a hole in one of mine is a real hole rather than this failure.)* **The OpenRig-side home is finding 3 of `ai_docs/reports-status/openrig-side-defects-2026-10-06.md`, which now carries a SIXTH instance and a CAUSE where it previously carried only a shape.**

58. A CORRECTION IS A CLAIM LIKE ANY OTHER, AND IT IS THE CLAIM LEAST LIKELY TO BE RE-EXAMINED — because a correction paragraph READS AS THE REMEDY rather than as the error. (2026-10-06; **the instance took THREE revisions of ONE paragraph, across two seats, in about ten minutes, and the MIDDLE revision was this file's own author's.**) **THE SEQUENCE, because each step is the point: (1) the first wording said the seat-MCP mount arrives ASYNCHRONOUSLY and needs no relaunch — WRONG IN ITS CAUSE, since the root cause is a 250 ms startup timeout and a relaunch is part of the fix; (2) the lead's correction then over-corrected the other way ("the mount does NOT land asynchronously", "what does not survive is that it requires a restart") — and THE WRITER SEAT RE-READ THE ROOT CAUSE AND WROTE THAT REVERSAL INTO THE DEPLOY RECORD, so the SECOND error belonged to the correction rather than to the claim it replaced; (3) the reviewer's gate measured the case that settles it — THE DEVICE APPEARED BETWEEN TWO ATTEMPTS WITH NO RELAUNCH — so the device DOES arrive late without one.** **THE ACCURATE FORM IS TWO HALVES ABOUT DIFFERENT THINGS: the 250 ms default DELAYS the mount, and the SETTING is what puts the device there from the first turn, with the relaunch needed for the SETTING.** **AND THE LESSON IS NOT "do not correct" — every revision moved toward the measurement — it is that A PARAGRAPH THAT CORRECTS SOMETHING ELSE IS WHERE BOTH READER AND AUTHOR STOP LOOKING, SO THE CORRECTION GETS THE SCRUTINY AND THE CLAIM KEEPS IT.** **Corollary for how they are written: say what the correction is CORRECTING, so a later reader can see which way it moved and re-measure it rather than inherit it.** **AND IT HAD ONE MORE SURFACE, FOUND BY A LATER SWEEP RATHER THAN WHILE THE RULE WAS BEING WRITTEN — AND IT IS THE SHARPEST OF THE FOUR, BECAUSE THERE IS NO GATE OVER A COMMENT: the fix's OWN SOURCE COMMENT carried the same over-correction** (`agenthub_go/fastmcp/seat_management/domain/seatrenderer/renderer.go:488-489` — *"not an asynchronous mount — 'asynchronous' described the symptom"*), **and the reviewer's gate QUOTES THAT COMMENT while offering the refinement, so THE SOURCE AND THE GATE CONTRADICTED EACH OTHER and a reader following the gate to the comment found them disagreeing.** **Reported by the docs seat rather than edited by it — a doc diff inside the renderer would blur that file's attribution for its reviewer — and assigned to the file's owner to fix.** **AND IT LANDED: `46e45205` ("the mechanism the omp MCP fix ships with now matches the refined measurement"), whose comment now reads *"THE DEFAULT DELAYS THE MOUNT RATHER THAN PREVENTING IT"* in place of *"not an asynchronous mount — 'asynchronous' described the symptom."* So the surface that HAS NO GATE was closed by the file's owner, which is the only route it could have taken.** **SO RULE 58 IS DEMONSTRATED ACROSS FOUR SURFACES IN ONE EVENING — a lead's sentence, a writer's correction of it, a reviewer's refinement, and a shipped comment — and EACH ONE READ AS THE REMEDY, WHICH IS WHY EACH WAS LOOKED AT EXACTLY ONCE.**

59. A SECTION HONEST SENTENCE BY SENTENCE CAN STILL READ AS A VERDICT — SO THE TEST IS NOT "IS EACH SENTENCE TRUE" BUT **"WHAT WOULD A READER CONCLUDE ABOUT WHO VERIFIED THIS"**. (2026-10-06; **rule 58's harder cousin: 58 is a claim that reads as THE REMEDY, this is an AGGREGATE that reads as FIRST-HAND when it is second-hand — and it is harder to catch because EVERY INDIVIDUAL SENTENCE PASSES.**) **THE INSTANCE, caught by the seat writing it: a section of `NEXT-DEPLOY.md` summarising a commit was correct sentence by sentence and still read as a gate — the literal, the alias and the protocol lines were MEASURED BY THE WRITER, while "all six surfaces now read one value" and the falsification text were THE AUTHOR'S CHANGELOG PLUS ITS TESTS, which the writer had read but not run.** **THE FIX IS ONE PARAGRAPH THAT SEPARATES WHAT WAS MEASURED HERE FROM WHAT IS SOMEONE ELSE'S CLAIM PLUS THEIR TESTS** — and it is now written whenever a section summarises another seat's work. **THE SAME DISTINCTION DECIDED THREE OTHER SENTENCES TONIGHT: "the delivery rests on the tests" became "the delivery is gated end to end" only when a GATE existed; "the call proves the setting and the dispatch, NOT the delivery"; and "the reviewer reproduced none of the four links" travelled in the same breath as the approval that said so.** **THE PRACTICE: A SUMMARY OF SOMEONE ELSE'S WORK STATES ITS PROVENANCE PER CLAIM GROUP — measured here, or gated, or the author's plus their tests — because a reader who cannot tell who verified what will assume the strongest reading available.**
**AND THE HALF THAT MAKES IT USABLE RATHER THAN MERELY TRUE — BECAUSE THE FAILURE IS INVISIBLE TO THE AUTHOR, IT CANNOT BE CAUGHT BY RE-READING: IT CAN ONLY BE CAUGHT BY ASKING THE PROVENANCE QUESTION OF A SECTION IN WHICH SOMEONE ELSE'S WORK IS SUMMARISED.** A rule that says *"do not let an aggregate read as first-hand"* tells you what to **avoid**; this tells you **where to look**, which is the only form that survives a busy day.
**AND IT FIRED WITHIN THE HOUR OF BEING WRITTEN, ON THIS SEAT'S OWN FILE, IN THE SECTION THAT EXISTS TO ANSWER THE QUESTION:** the packet-5 docket's **PROVENANCE TABLE** was a version behind the prose above it — the rows for the newest claims were missing while the sentences citing them were already written. **THAT IS WHAT MAKES IT INVISIBLE: THE ANSWER HAD ALREADY BEEN WRITTEN, IN THE WRONG SECTION.**
**AND THE SECOND HALF OF THE FIX MATTERS AS MUCH AS THE FIRST: A PROVENANCE TABLE THAT CARRIES LIMITS IS ONE A READER CAN ACT ON, WHILE ONE THAT CARRIES ONLY ATTRIBUTIONS TELLS THEM WHO TO TRUST WITHOUT TELLING THEM HOW FAR.** So a row naming a gate travels with **that gate's own limits** — not a runner-launched rig seat, the single stub it used, and the four links it did not reproduce.
**AND THE CLAUSE THAT STOPS A READER CONCLUDING THEY WILL CATCH IT BY TRYING HARDER — THE FAILURE FAVOURS THE DILIGENT: THE PERSON MOST LIKELY TO HAVE THE ANSWER IS THE ONE MOST LIKELY TO HAVE PUT IT SOMEWHERE ELSE.** *(Which is where the diligence goes: not into re-reading your own section more carefully, but into asking the provenance question of the sections where you summarised somebody else.)*

60. THE TEST OF A RULE IS NOT THAT IT WAS WRITTEN DOWN BUT THAT SOMEBODY WHO WAS NOT IN THE CONVERSATION APPLIED IT WITHOUT BEING TOLD. (2026-10-06; **stated by the lead as the test for tonight's rules, and offered back with its own evidence DOWNGRADED, which is the case that makes it credible.**) **THE EVIDENCE AT ITS TRUE STRENGTH, which is less than it first looked: rule 59 was applied on the day it was written by TWO OF ITS OWN AUTHORS** — this seat in its sections, and the lead in a habit line stated independently — **and that is adoption BY THE PEOPLE WHO WERE IN THE CONVERSATION.** **The reviewer's gates already carry their coverage limits, but they PREDATE the rule, so they are evidence that the PRACTICE is right rather than that the RULE travels.** **SO THIS RULE'S OWN TEST IS NOT YET MET, and it says so in its own text rather than waiting to be graded: WHAT WOULD MEET IT IS A SEAT THAT NEVER READ IT APPLYING IT, OR A LATER PASS FINDING THE PROVENANCE QUESTION ALREADY ASKED IN A FILE NONE OF US OPENED TONIGHT.** **A RULE THAT STATES ITS OWN FALSIFICATION CONDITION IS WORTH TEN THAT ONLY STATE A PRESCRIPTION.**
**AND THE COROLLARY, which gives it a consequence rather than a preference: A RULE THAT CANNOT TRAVEL IS A NOTE** — a rule that has not travelled yet is not yet a rule, and saying so is what keeps a list of rules from filling with things we merely intended.
**AND THE FIRST THING THIS RULE WAS APPLIED TO WAS THE EVIDENCE OFFERED FOR RULES 59 AND 60 THEMSELVES:** *"three authors, one rule, the strongest evidence a rule can have"* was **an aggregate of adoption reading as proof that the rule travels**, which is precisely the overclaim rule 59 exists to catch — and the seat it was addressed to **downgraded it rather than accepting it.** **A RULE THAT CAN CONVICT ITS OWN AUTHOR IS THE ONLY KIND WORTH HAVING.**

61. A ROUTE OR TOOL THAT EXISTS IN CODE WITH NO ENTRY FAILS, AND AN ENTRY WITH NO ROUTE OR TOOL FAILS — AND IT IS ONLY A CHECK ONCE EACH DIRECTION HAS BEEN SEEN FAILING. (2026-10-06; **the docs page's step 1, and directive B — "never document what you have not seen in code" — turned into a machine.** Stated here as well as enforced there, because a rule that exists only inside a test is a rule nobody can read.) **WHY THE CLASS EXISTS AND NOT JUST THE INSTANCE: a reference maintained BY HAND against one codebase drifts in ONE DIRECTION — by OMISSION — and the drift is invisible, because A MISSING ROW LOOKS EXACTLY LIKE A ROUTE THAT DOES NOT EXIST.** **THE FIRST INSTANCE, MEASURED BEFORE ANY OF THE PACKET WAS BUILT: the code mounts 144 distinct `(method, path)` registrations while `agenthub-frontend/src/docs/api-reference.en.md` carried 127 rows — SEVENTEEN ROUTES WITH NO ENTRY (twelve per cent: the friction channel's two, eight Supabase auth routes, four token routes, and the D5 room-sharing route) AND ZERO ENTRIES WITH NO ROUTE.** **Direction two being zero is the control, and it is what makes the whole gap OMISSION — which is what a generator fixes and a drift rule then keeps fixed.** **AND THE DOCUMENT'S OWN SENTENCE STATED `141 / 121`, A STALE PAIR THE SURFACE INVENTORY HAD CARRIED INDEPENDENTLY AND WRONGLY THE SAME EVENING: two hand-maintained documents drifting in the same place against one codebase is the class rather than a coincidence.** **THE UNIT IS PART OF THE RULE RATHER THAN A STYLE CHOICE: Go's mux treats `GET /api/v2/tokens` and `GET /api/v2/tokens/` as DIFFERENT patterns (exact versus subtree) and `{$}` as exact-only, so the unit is THE REGISTRATION — 144 rather than 127 — because COLLAPSING THEM IS HOW A SECOND, QUIETER SOURCE OF DRIFT ENTERS. Grouping them for a reader is a rendering decision, not the unit of the rule.** **AND THE ARCHITECTURAL CLAUSE, CORRECTED THE SAME EVENING BECAUSE ITS FIRST FORM CONFLICTED WITH ITSELF: the rule is NOT "one extractor in the codebase" but ONE PRODUCER AND ONE WITNESS — the generator and the page share ONE PRODUCER, and THE CHECK IS THE INDEPENDENT WITNESS, so the two are DIFFERENT ROLES AND MUST BE DIFFERENT INSTRUMENTS. A consumer-side test would need a second producer, which is still forbidden; a WITNESS is not a producer.** **THE TEST THEREFORE READS THE CODE ITSELF RATHER THAN CALLING THE GENERATOR'S PARSER: routes by its OWN parse of the `*_mount.go` source text, and the tool surface from the code that is NOT the runtime call (the `case …ToolName:` dispatch entries plus the appended `"name":` lines) — because SOURCE-TEXT VERSUS CALLED-CODE IS A GENUINELY DIFFERENT INSTRUMENT, and it is exactly the cross-check that caught this file's author's own 9-versus-10 miscount the same evening.** **A test that calls the generator's parser compares the generator TO ITSELF AND CAN NEVER FAIL — the own-shadow failure in a third form, and the reason THE GENERATOR MUST NOT EXPORT ITS PARSER FOR THE TEST TO REUSE.** **Its acceptance is this file's self-test discipline applied to a document: both directions implemented, and BOTH SEEN FAILING — a document check that has only ever passed is a claim about a document, not a check on one.**

62. A REPRODUCE LINE MUST CITE A COMMAND THAT WAS RUN IN THIS ENVIRONMENT. (2026-10-08; found by the writer, recorded by the architect. Beside rule 36: a number with a second home reads as measured; this is the same failure moved into the instrument.) A command that would work in a better environment reads as evidence and cannot fire. A reader who sees "reproduce with: ..." gets no signal that the tool is missing. Incident: `ai_docs/api-integration/surface-inventory.md` cited `rg -n` to reproduce its route count. There is no `rg` binary on PATH, and its scope included `*_test.go` (127 lines, not the figure's 125). The line now cites the `grep` that produced the number (`561a5b1c`). "This environment" is per seat, not per machine: measured by the architect (claude-code), `type -P rg` finds nothing, but `rg` in that seat's shell is a function that runs the harness's embedded copy, and it answers. In a plain login shell on the same box (`env -i HOME="$HOME" PATH="$PATH" bash -lc 'command -v rg'`), it is not found, which matches what the writer's omp seat saw. So one seat can run a command that another cannot, on the same box and the same tree. Practice: run the command you cite, at the root you name, and cite it with its result. Where the shell's tool is not the plain binary (this harness's `grep` is a function that can fail silently and print nothing), cite the form you ran, such as `command grep`.

63. A LINE REFERENCE CAN RESOLVE PERFECTLY AND STILL SUPPORT ONLY HALF ITS SENTENCE. SAY WHICH PART A CITATION SUPPORTS. (2026-10-08; found by the writer, recorded by the architect. Beside rule 37: a correction travels with its claim, and a citation has to as well.) Checking that a citation resolves (the file is tracked and the line is within its length) is not checking that it supports the sentence. A sweep that tests only resolution can call a report clean while a cited line supports half of what it is cited for. Incident: `agenthub_go/PROD_READINESS_REPORT.md:179` cites `MIGRATION.md:951` for two parity facts. Line 951 carries the stats-returns-500 half, and no line in MIGRATION.md carries the `PUT {id}` requires-`task_id` half. The writer's sweep found all six citations resolving and still declined to call it a clean bill. Applied: `ai_docs/core-architecture/agenthub-system-architecture.md D3` (`782302f1`) scopes both parity citations to the stats route, and says that no parity record mentions `GET /api/tasks/{task_id}`.

64. READ THE PATH BEFORE YOU COMMIT IT AND READ YOUR COMMIT AFTER — THE DIFF IS THE ONLY INSTRUMENT THAT SEES EITHER FAILURE. (2026-10-09; stated by the lead as a rule for the whole fleet after two instances in one night, and it is the same class one layer apart: a commit whose message said it only added a section landed 9 insertions / 7 deletions, and a pathspec commit took the working-tree copy of a shared file and carried a policy decision with it.) **BEFORE committing a path another seat could have touched - CHANGELOG.md above all, but any shared file - run `git diff HEAD -- <path>` and confirm the change is YOURS AND ONLY YOURS. AFTER committing, read your own commit against its parent: `git show --numstat <hash>`. A commit whose numstat does not match what you intended is a revert you did not notice you were making.** **WHY NOTHING ELSE CATCHES IT, measured: the hook ladder passed, the pathspec was obeyed, the trailer was right — `git commit -- <paths>` takes the WORKING TREE copy of the named paths, so a temporary index or a stale worktree silently changes what lands.** **THE RULE APPLIES TO ITS OWN AUTHOR: the first application was the note that wrote it.** **AND A MECHANICAL GUARD OVER THIS RULE MUST STATE WHAT IT GUARANTEES BEFORE WHAT IT CATCHES, BECAUSE A SMOKE ALARM THAT READS AS A GUARANTEE IS THE DEFECT IT EXISTS TO CATCH (2026-10-09; added by the writer after running one on `CHANGELOG.md`, the hottest path in the tree, and after the lead ruled that the limit belongs in the text rather than in the author's head). THE GUARD AS RUN: read ONE file's `git diff HEAD -- <path>`, take the ADDED lines beginning with `## `, count them after excluding the author's own heading, and run the commit only when that count is zero — check and commit in one shell invocation. WHAT IT GUARANTEES: that at that instant no other seat had an uncommitted entry in THAT file whose first line begins with `## `. WHAT IT DOES NOT CATCH, and every item here is a case the mechanism cannot see: (i) a FOREIGN EDIT inside an existing entry — no new heading appears, so the count stays zero; (ii) a foreign entry at another heading level, or with no heading at all; (iii) foreign added lines that are not headings; (iv) a foreign hunk landing BETWEEN the check and the commit, because the window is one command rather than zero; (v) ANY OTHER PATH — the same commit checked its second file by a different and weaker count (a count of its own added rule line), and that weaker check is not the strong one wearing fewer words. THE STRONG FORM, for anyone who generalizes it: ADDED-LINE SET EQUALITY for the file — set equality against your own entry's lines rather than a heading count — or re-compute the diff immediately before the commit and require it to equal the one taken immediately after your edit.**

65. A RELOCATION'S CHECKLIST IS THREE CHECKS, AND EACH ONE EARNED ITSELF IN A SINGLE DAY. (2026-10-09; found by feedback-dev while landing the client relocation, adopted by the lead from the sweep that came with it. Beside rule 64: that rule protects a COMMIT from the shared tree; this one protects a MOVE from the callers the move never sees.) **(i) BEFORE STAGING A DELETION, GREP THE OLD PATH AS A STRING ACROSS EVERY LANGUAGE — `git grep -n '<old path>'` — BECAUSE TESTS, FIXTURES, REFUSAL MESSAGES AND DOCS HOLD PATHS NO IMPORT GRAPH SEES.** Three callers of this relocation were found by a person seeing a red test, never by a check: the Go guard executing `scripts/seat_feedback.sh` (`ff6fed10`), the Python roster test loading `scripts/openrig_seat_policy.py` (`ec220212`), and prose in a directory of data, `scripts/team/4genthub-min/NOTES.md:3` (`b10ed919`). **(ii) EVERY MOVED ENTRY POINT NEEDS A VERB OR A DOOR AT ITS NEW HOME, OR THE OBVIOUS WORD FALLS THROUGH TO SOMETHING WORSE.** With no `feedback` verb, `4genteam feedback ...` reached the last line of `main()`, `return lifecycle("up", args)`, so a seat on a runtime with no MCP asking for the friction channel would have STARTED a daemon, a compaction supervisor, herdr and a UI instead of reporting anything (`ff6fed10`). **(iii) A FILE THAT SHIPS INSIDE A PACKAGE MUST BE NAMED IN PACKAGE-DATA, OR IT EXISTS ONLY IN AN EDITABLE CHECKOUT.** `uv build` produced the twelve modules and no `seat_feedback.sh`, so the door existed on one machine and nowhere else; the fix was verified by installing the built wheel into a throwaway venv and driving it against a stub of the route (`ff6fed10`). **(i) AND (iii) BOTH BIT THIS TREE, BOTH SILENTLY, AND BOTH WERE FOUND BY A PERSON RATHER THAN BY A CHECK.**
**AND THE LIMIT OF THE INSTRUMENT, WHICH IS WHY A SWEEP IS A CANDIDATE LIST AND NOT A WORK LIST: `git grep` CANNOT TELL A STRUCK CITATION FROM A LIVE INSTRUCTION** — both are the same string — **so it reports WHERE a deleted path appears, not WHETHER a reader would be misled. THE COUNT FOUND FIFTY FILES; READING THE LINES FOUND TWO.** What a sweep correctly leaves alone is not always an oversight: `README.md:141-143,147`, `ai_docs/operations/openrig-seat-limits.md:26-28` and `ai_docs/operations/seat-approval-and-the-startup-call.md:151` are the line-B paths, held with the line decision, and the writer's `WRITER-BLOCKED-DOC-DEFECTS-2026-10-09.md` carries their patches.

66. STATE WHICH TREE YOU MEASURED — WORKTREE, INDEX OR HEAD — AND WHEN THE INSTRUMENT IS A STRING MATCH RATHER THAN A READING, SAY THAT TOO. (2026-10-09; three instances in one day, each found by the seat that had made the measurement, adopted by the lead after the third, and the text cleared by the architect. Beside rule 65: that rule says grep before you delete; this one says name what you grep.) **THE THREE TREES DISAGREE BY CONSTRUCTION IN A REPOSITORY MID-MOVE, and a claim that does not name its tree reads as a fact about all three.** **INSTANCE (iii), the one that named the rule:** `ls: cannot access 'scripts/openrig_seat_sync.py'` was TRUE OF THE WORKTREE, where `git rm` had already removed the file, and FALSE of every committed tree — HEAD (`586a4105` then; still true at `cbc5d08e`) and line B `827b8ecb` both hold it — while the sweep that found the docs ran `git grep --cached`, the INDEX, and NEITHER WAS HEAD; the severity was written as "names a deleted path" when the measurement said "names a path STAGED for deletion", and that difference is the whole of whether anything is broken. **INSTANCE (i):** the reviewer's wheel arm reused the author's GITIGNORED `agenthub_client/build/` directory, so it measured the WORKTREE while its claim was about the COMMIT — setuptools reused the stale output, BOTH ARMS AGREED, and the verdict superseded itself inside its own file (`GATE-ff6fed10-feedbackdoor-2026-10-09.md`, section (3)). **AND THE SAME PAIR AS A NUMBER, WHICH IS WHY IT IS A RULE AND NOT A NAG: 306 passed was reported; the worktree measured 302 passed, 9 failed out of 311 collected, the 9 caused by another seat's uncommitted edit (`GATE-ff6fed10-feedbackdoor-2026-10-09.md:59`), and restoring that file gives 309 passed, 2 failed. EVEN THE COLLECTED COUNTS DIFFER — 306 against 311 — SO THE TWO NUMBERS DO NOT DESCRIBE ONE SUITE, and the number that gets quoted is whichever was read last.** **AND THE POINT OF INSTANCE (ii) IS THE LIMIT RATHER THAN A FOURTH CASE: NAMING THE TREE DOES NOT MAKE A STRING MATCH A READING: see rule 65's limit paragraph.**

67. HASH PRESENCE IN A TRANSCRIPT IS NOT AN ATTRIBUTION METHOD — AND NEITHER IS HASH PRESENCE IN THE OUTPUT OF YOUR OWN COMMAND. (2026-10-09; found by the writer while re-deriving a trial arm's commit list, adopted by the lead. Beside rule 64: that rule says read the path before and after you commit it; this one says NAME THE INSTRUMENT THAT TELLS YOU A COMMIT IS YOURS, because the obvious one does not.) **THE FAILING TEST, IN THE TWO FORMS IT WAS TRIED IN THIS SESSION.** The first: "does my session store contain this commit hash" — run against the day's 74 commits it marked **ALL 74 AS THE WRITER'S**, including fe-dev's, go-dev's and the architect's, **because every other seat's hash passes through a transcript every time anyone reads `git log` or a handoff file.** The second, tightened to "does the hash appear in the output of my own `git commit` call", **fails identically and the instance is recorded: `105dadba` (`fix(team_setup)`, another seat's commit) was confirmed as the writer's that way, because the same shell call that commits also prints a `git log` tail and a neighbouring commit's hash is in that tail.** The mechanism is one sentence: **a hash in an output tells you the commit EXISTS NEAR YOUR WORK, never that you made it.**
**THE THREE INSTRUMENTS THAT DO WORK, EACH WITH ITS BLIND SPOT STATED. (i) THE SEAT TRAILER** (`Seat: <rig>-<seat>@<rig>`, added by `prepare-commit-msg`) — decisive where it exists, **BUT ITS POPULATION IS PARTIAL AND IT HAS NO CLEAN ONSET, WHICH A CENSUS SETTLED AGAINST THIS RULE'S OWN FIRST DRAFT — AND ITS "EARLIEST" IS WINDOW-DEPENDENT, WHICH IS THE PART THIS SENTENCE FIRST GOT WRONG: MEASURED OVER THE 164 COMMITS SINCE 2026-10-08 (that census's own count, and a count moves, so THE WINDOW IS THE DATE AND NOT THE NUMBER), READING BOTH `%(trailers:key=Seat)` AND THE RAW BODY, the earliest commit carrying a `Seat:` line is `44c81bcb`, 2026-10-08 18:33:49Z — A DAY EARLIER than the 19:00:03Z the draft claimed — BUT OVER THE WHOLE HISTORY THE EARLIEST IS `ca6574e8`, 2026-10-06T18:35:32+02:00, TWO DAYS BEFORE IT, whose trailer reads `4genthub-min.web-dev` (`3e4b2605`, `c81eff94` and `7ba67f9e` follow it the same evening), SO AN UNWINDOWED "EARLIEST" IS FALSE AND THE WINDOW MUST TRAVEL WITH THE CLAIM. WITHIN THAT CENSUS WINDOW `cd472751` IS NOT THE FIRST (its own DIRECT PARENT `5a2fc602`, and `4814b711` a minute before it, both already carry one), and within that same hour some commits carry the line while others carry none (`12006c50`, `9ba5a358`). SO ABSENCE OF A TRAILER IS NOT EVIDENCE ABOUT WHO AUTHORED A COMMIT: read it as "no trailer", NEVER as "not this seat", and any attribution that treats the trailer as switched on at a time will silently undercount — which is what the writer's own arm did to seven of its commits.** **AND THE INSTRUMENT HAS A FALSE-NEGATIVE MODE, WHICH IS A LIMIT OF IT RATHER THAN A FOOTNOTE: FIVE COMMITS IN THAT WINDOW CARRY A `Seat:` LINE THAT `%(trailers:key=Seat)` DOES NOT PARSE (`ff6fed10`, `12bc9bce`, `ec220212`, `b10ed919`, `ba19e624` — the parsed trailer comes back EMPTY while the body shows the seat), so THE PARSED TRAILER AND THE BODY LINE MUST BE READ TOGETHER. A PARSER THAT ANSWERS "NO TRAILER" FOR A COMMIT THAT VISIBLY CARRIES ONE IS THE SAME DISEASE AS RULE 69'S STORE PATH: AN EMPTY ANSWER AND A NEGATIVE ANSWER LOOK IDENTICAL.** **WHAT THIS CORRECTION DOES AND DOES NOT DO: IT NARROWS ONE INSTRUMENT AND DOES NOT RETREAT FROM THE RULE — the test that failed is the HASH test (74 of 74 commits that day, including other seats'), that finding stands, and instruments (ii) and (iii) are untouched.** **(ii) THE PER-COMMIT GATE FILE** in the seat workspace, which names the author in its prose (`GATE-a2ff4332-row8-2026-10-09.md` reads "matches the architect's report") or in its filename. **(iii) THE COMMIT CONFIRMATION IN THE AUTHORING SEAT'S OWN STORE**, read as the git commit line for that call and never as any hash appearing anywhere in its output — and it misses every commit whose output was truncated; in the arm's window it returned 19 of the 20, the false one being `105dadba`.**
**AND THE TIE-BREAK THAT KEEPS THE LIST HONEST WHEN THE INSTRUMENTS DISAGREE: A LIST BUILT BY AGREEMENT BETWEEN TWO COUNTS IS NOT DERIVED — agreement proves only that the counts SHARE A BLIND SPOT.** The writer's own "23 at reading 16" was withdrawn for this reason: it had added back five commits that POSTDATE the reading it was added to, so the number was double-counted rather than measured. **State the instrument behind every attribution, and the window it covers.**

68. A TEST COUNT IS SHARED-TREE STATE, NOT A PROPERTY OF A COMMIT — EVERY COUNT CARRIES THE TRACKED / UNTRACKED / STAGED SPLIT BESIDE IT. (2026-10-09; found by the reviewer, adopted by the lead in the same pass as rule 67, and it is the fourth instrument failure of one night: rule 66 said name your tree, this one says the tree CHANGES UNDER A COUNT THE READER TAKES FOR A FACT ABOUT CODE.) **THE INSTANCE, AND ALL THREE READINGS ARE CORRECT: the SAME command — `python3 -m pytest --noconftest -p no:cacheprovider scripts/tests -q` from the repository root — read 14 passed at `69f7f3a4` (the writer's own measurement, carried in that commit's changelog), 19 in the reviewer's frame, and 20 in the writer's next pass. NOTHING WAS WRONG WITH ANY OF THE THREE: the count is what pytest COLLECTS FROM THE WORKTREE, and in this repository the worktree, the index and HEAD disagree BY CONSTRUCTION while a relocation is landing.**
**THE SPLIT, MEASURED RATHER THAN QUOTED, in the pass that landed this rule: `git ls-tree -r --name-only HEAD -- scripts/tests` gives `pytest.ini` plus THIRTEEN test files; `ls scripts/tests` shows `pytest.ini` plus FOUR; and `git status --porcelain -- scripts/tests` carries NINE `D` lines — the `test_openrig_*.py` files staged for deletion. So "20 passed" and "13 test files" are both true and describe DIFFERENT TREES, and only the split says which.** **THE PRACTICE: write a count WITH the split — "20 passed in the worktree; of the directory's test files, 13 are tracked at HEAD and 9 are staged for deletion" — and when the count is meant to be about a COMMIT, run it against that commit in an export rather than in the shared tree, because a live count measures the tree every seat is editing (rule 53's complement, one layer down: the same instrument, pointed at a different object).** **AND THE ANTI-PATTERN THIS NAMES: a number that MOVED between two readings of ONE commit reads as a contradiction, so a reader either disbelieves a correct number or believes a number about the wrong tree — and both failures are silent, because every one of the three counts was green.**

69. A STALE INSTRUMENT IS NOT AN ABSENT FACT — SAY WHEN THE INSTRUMENT WAS LAST READ, BECAUSE A CACHED VALUE LOOKS EXACTLY LIKE A CURRENT ONE. (2026-10-09; found by the writer while measuring a trial arm's window, adopted by the lead on a test that is itself worth stating: **TWO FINDINGS THAT ROUTE TO DIFFERENT ACTIONS STAY TWO RULES.** It is the FOURTH instrument question, beside rule 66 — WHICH TREE — and rule 67 — WHICH ATTRIBUTION METHOD: **this one names WHEN IT WAS LAST READ**, the easiest of the four to skip.) **THE INSTANCE, WITH ITS NUMBERS: measuring the window for `a9041461`, the first extraction returned NO records after 20:07Z and the conclusion drawn was that the session store LAGS THE TURN — a conclusion that had been CORRECT TWICE EARLIER IN THE SAME SESSION (a report that had in fact been delivered, and a store that had genuinely not flushed). IT WAS WRONG HERE: the store had grown 10228 -> 10397 records while the measurement held a CACHED PARSE of the file in a kernel variable, so the instrument had stopped being the file. Re-reading the source gave the reading — 110.3 s, 10 requests, input 12883, cacheRead 2105216, output 24386, reasoning 16093, cost $0.022880 — which is the arm's reading 24.**
**WHY IT IS NOT RULE 68, AND THE DISCRIMINATOR IS THE ACTION: 68 is the WORLD moving under a count — the number changed because the tree changed, and the recovery is to publish the TRACKED / UNTRACKED / STAGED split; THIS is the INSTRUMENT ceasing to track the world while NOTHING ABOUT ITS MEANING CHANGED — no tree moved, the copy simply stopped being the source — and the recovery is to RE-READ THE SOURCE, not to qualify the number. Collapsed into one rule they would teach the right lesson and cause the WRONG ACT: qualifying a number that was never about the wrong tree, and WAITING FOR A STORE THAT HAD ALREADY ANSWERED.**
**THE HONEST FORM OF THE FIRST READING WAS "MY PARSE IS EIGHT MINUTES OLD", NOT "THE STORE HAS NOT CAUGHT UP" — the two statements route to different actions (re-read, versus wait), and the second would have written a FALSE UNAVAILABLE into a row whose acceptance FORBIDS ESTIMATING, where a false UNAVAILABLE is a DEFECT rather than a gap.** **AND THE SAME FAILURE'S SECOND FORM, measured in this store: usage lives at `record['message']['usage']`, while a parse that reads the record's `data` returns NOTHING for every assistant record and the TOP LEVEL returns zeros — so A WRONG PATH AND AN EMPTY FILE LOOK IDENTICAL.**

## Environment facts useful to the next session
- **A DURABLE ARTEFACT THAT CANNOT BE EDITED IN PLACE MAKES ITS FIRST VERSION AUTHORITATIVE, WHETHER OR NOT IT WAS RIGHT (platform fact, 2026-10-05; kept as the file COPY — the platform items' durable home is the lead's messages to the owner with their evidence).** A queue row's BODY is IMMUTABLE and its NOTES are APPEND-ONLY, so a row cannot carry a corrected statement of its own premise: it carries the original first and the correction after, and a reader who stops at the body reads the wrong number. Appending around it — with a self-announcing marker such as `PREMISE CORRECTION` (rule 37) — is a WORKAROUND rather than a fix. The general rule, which go-dev's extension carries past the queue verb (it is as true of a commit message as of a queue body), is rule 38.
### All ten seats run the same runtime — one fact, two symptoms (owner-requested prominence, 2026-10-06)

**ALL TEN SEATS IN THIS RIG RUN THE SAME RUNTIME** (measured: `rig whoami --json` gives **ten of ten `"runtime":"omp"`**, and the topology holds **no claude-backed seat**). **AND THAT ONE FACT EXPLAINS TWO FINDINGS THAT LOOKED UNRELATED:** **(a) A CLAUDE-SPECIFIC ENFORCEMENT CHECK HAS NOWHERE TO LAND HERE** — the typed-deny check exercises the CLAUDE runtime's permission rules, so verifying it needs a claude-backed seat, which this rig cannot provide without spending the owner's account (`OWNER-DECISIONS.md` item 20: answered, deferred to after the restart, and blocked on a repair to the Claude CLI on this machine); **and (b) THE DEMAND THAT EVERY SEAT BE ABLE TO DELEGATE NEEDED A CONFIGURATION ANSWER RATHER THAN A CODE ONE** — offload to the DeepSeek harness is a property of RENDERED SEAT CONFIG, so it is satisfied by what each seat is rendered with rather than by anything the server implements. **SAME FACT, TWO SYMPTOMS — one structural property read as two unrelated problems, where the honest answer to both is what the topology is rather than what the code does.** **AND THE COROLLARY FOR READING THIS RIG'S EVIDENCE: ANY CLAIM ABOUT RUNTIME-SPECIFIC BEHAVIOUR HERE IS A CLAIM ABOUT `omp`, so a platform finding that names another harness is UNMEASURED IN THIS RIG rather than refuted by it.**

- **AND THE PARITY CLAIM IS CLOSED RATHER THAN LEFT OPEN: THE THREE UNMOUNTED WEBSOCKET ENDPOINTS ARE DECLARED OUT OF SCOPE (owner decision, 2026-10-06).** **The old-protocol surface served by `fastmcp/websocket/server.go` is NOT mounted on the live handler** — it registers `/ws/{user_id}` (`server.go:92`), `/ws/health` and `/ws/stats` (`server.go:93-94`) on **its own app**, and **nothing outside that package constructs it** (`NewWebSocketServer` has **no non-test caller**) — **so this declaration closes the parity item this file's backlog has been carrying as unfinished.** **THE COMPANION SENTENCE THAT KEEPS THE CLAIM HONEST: THE PORT IS COMPLETE FOR THE SURFACE IN USE, with `/ws/{user_id}`, `/ws/health` and `/ws/stats` DELIBERATELY EXCLUDED** — so nothing anywhere reads as though the old protocol is fully served. **AND IT IS A DECISION RATHER THAN AN OMISSION:** mounting them would have added endpoints **nothing consumes**, and **the owner chose the honest label over the tidier-looking port.** (Named and dated in `ai_docs/api-integration/surface-inventory.md`, §1.7.)
- **NO HUMAN SEAT IN THIS RIG — the exact, TESTED route for a decision that needs one (2026-10-05; corrected twice, now proved by construction).** Every peer is runtime `omp` and there is NO human seat. The verb in this build is **`block`** (which parks a row as HELD) — there is **NO `park` subcommand** (verified from the CLI: `block` is listed as "Park a qitem as HELD with a continuation and wake", and `rig queue park --help` returns the generic usage). **THE CREATE CARRIES THE HUMAN-NESS AND THE BLOCK CARRIES THE WAIT:** (1) **CREATE with the gate flag** — `rig queue create … --gate human` — because the `gate:human` TAG IS WRITTEN AT CREATE TIME ("translated to a `gate:<role>` tag") and a row created without it cannot be tagged later with the verbs available here; (2) **BLOCK it on the lead WITHOUT the two refused fields** — `rig queue block <qitem> --on 4genthub-min-lead@4genthub-min --continuation <what resumes> --note <prose> --wake-after <dur>` — because `--summary` and `--evidence-ref` are the ONLY things that fail: both are documented as required "for human-seat parks", and there is no human seat here, while the BLOCK VERB ITSELF works on an agent seat. Proved by construction (go-dev2's throwaway row, read back): pickup state **parked**, item state **blocked**, `blockedOn` the lead, tag `gate:human` present. **STILL OPEN — and it is the reason to ALSO say it in a message:** whether a `gate:human` row actually SURFACES to the owner where they read is an interface question with the owner, so state the human decision in your MESSAGE to the lead as well; the row alone may not surface it. And **READ THE STATE BACK** after any state-changing call, because a refused change can leave the row looking as it was — a false wait (rule 13's family).
- **OPENRIG-SIDE DEFECT — A REFUSED PARK LEAVES THE ROW `in-progress`, SO A SEAT CAN BELIEVE IT IS WAITING WHEN NOTHING WAS RECORDED (found and tested 2026-10-06 by go-dev2; the durable home is the lead's report to the owner, kept here as the copy).** The refusal is COHERENT and is not a broken field shape: `rig queue block <qitem> --on <agent seat> --summary … --evidence-ref …` answers `{"error": "summary_evidence_not_persistable", "message": "summary + evidenceRef persist only on a human-seat park (state=blocked on a human seat); the 'blocked' transition cannot store them.", "invalidFields": ["summary", "evidenceRef"]}` — those two fields persist only on a human-seat park, and an agent seat takes the plain `blocked` path. Two consequences are the defect. (1) **THE ROW IS LEFT `in-progress`**, exactly as it was, so **a refusal and a park that quietly did nothing are indistinguishable in the moment, and READING THE STATE BACK IS THE ONLY WAY TO TELL THEM APART** — a real park answers `pickup.state: "parked"`, a refused one leaves `"working"`. (2) **THE PRESCRIBED ROUTE DOES NOT EXIST IN THIS RIG:** the message tells the caller to park on a human seat, every peer here is runtime `omp` (see the route fact above), so a seat that needs a human decision follows the instruction into a refused call and is likely to carry on as if it had parked. **SUGGESTED FIX, for the owner:** persist summary and evidence_ref when the blocker is a typed human gate or an agent seat acting for a human, name a route that exists in the topology rather than one that does not, and never leave the row `in-progress` on a refused park — that is where the intent dies. **AND THE HYPOTHESIS IS DISPROVEN BY EXPERIMENT, which is why this is a report rather than a guess:** the first reading was that the evidence ref's SHAPE triggered it (path-like accepted, free prose rejected — suggested by `daemon/dist/routes/attention.js:51` testing `isAbsolute` and a scheme regex); a throwaway row parked with an ABSOLUTE PATH evidence ref failed IDENTICALLY with the row left `in-progress`, so the shape is NOT the trigger.
- **THE THREE OPENRIG-SIDE FINDINGS — ONE REPORT, THREE REPRODUCTIONS (2026-10-06).** (1) the **park defect above**; (2) the **silent preview read** — a durable record's default read is a BOUNDED PREVIEW with no marker saying so, so a reader who omits the full flag sees a mid-word cut and concludes the record was truncated (which the lead did, to the owner, before the writer disproved it); (3) the **send-path asymmetry** (NEW, go-dev) — `rig queue create` ships `--body-file`, whose own help says it kills the backtick-shell-corruption class for multiline bodies, while the SEND path has no equivalent, so the hazard is mechanically solved on one path and left open on the other (five instances across four seats, two self-caught). **THE ASK: give `rig send` the same shape as `rig queue create` — a body-file or a no-expansion mode —** because a message that must carry a path or a command is precisely the case with no body-file equivalent today, and a rule that catches its own authors within the hour is evidence the rule cannot be the control. **All three were found by RUNNING the real thing rather than by reading documentation**, which is why they are worth sending. Full report, framed per finding (what happened / exact reproduction / consequence / what a fix would look like): `ai_docs/reports-status/openrig-side-defects-2026-10-06.md`.
- **SHARED WORKING TREE — the pre-commit hook stashes unstaged files and restores them, so an uncommitted edit is NOT durable (2026-10-06, measured by web-dev).** Every seat shares one working tree, and pre-commit's `Stashing unstaged files to …` / `Restored changes from …` pair (visible in every commit of this session) means a file left uncommitted can be REVERTED TO HEAD silently when another seat commits — no error to either side, and the only symptom is an anchor that no longer matches. web-dev lost in-flight `AuthContext.tsx` edits that way. Commit as soon as a change is verified with an exact pathspec, re-read the anchor before editing (assume the hazard, do not blindly re-apply), and report a reverted file rather than silently re-doing it. **THE OTHER DIRECTION, recorded because it is the SAME MECHANISM RUNNING BENIGNLY (2026-10-06, this seat): the stash/restore pair fired on a two-file documentation commit and NOTHING was lost** — the unstaged files were stashed while the gates ran and restored after, and **the restoration was CHECKED rather than assumed**: `git status --short` on both paths came back empty and the files were still present, which is the only reason anyone knows it was clean. So the hook is not itself a defect — it is a mechanism whose **DANGEROUS direction is a concurrent commit and whose BENIGN direction is a solo one**, and the rule is the same in both: **verify the tree after the hook has run**, because a silent revert and a clean restore are indistinguishable from the committer's side. **This is the hazard's FOURTH instance tonight and the first benign one** — the swept deletion earlier was the same mechanism in the other direction. **AND IT FIRED AGAIN ON THE VERY COMMIT THAT WROTE THIS PARAGRAPH** — benignly, with the same check run rather than assumed, which is the best evidence in this entry: **the hazard demonstrated itself while being documented.** A rule whose instance arrives uninvited is one the work is producing rather than one the record is merely accumulating. Full rule: rule 27.
- **OFFLINE BUNDLES — WHAT A BUNDLE CARRIES, WHAT IT REFUSES, AND WHAT "CLOSED" WOULD STILL COST (G4's two boxes; verified 2026-10-06 by **go-dev2**, whose every claim this seat re-read at the cited lines; recorded in the environment-facts section because this is where an operator looks for what a bundle does and does not carry).** **(i) THE ENFORCEMENT POINT READS THE PINNED POLICY FROM INSIDE A BUNDLE — HOLDS for a pulled, bundled seat.** The policy is written at pull (`scripts/openrig_seat_sync.py:320`), copied into the rig root (`:539`, docstring `:531-533`), and installed to `<home>/.openrig/agenthub-seats/<rig>/<member>/policy.json` (`:667`, `:670`); **THE BUNDLE BINDS IT BY CONTENT** — `bundle.yaml:25` declares `agents/developer/policy.json = e60392774f4fb4b711599395ce283ed8cc8cc2aa987c0a9ac7f310a9bb1cf8a1` and the file's own sha256 matches (the reviewer seat's `9b822abe…` likewise) — and `seatcheck` reads exactly that path (`agenthub_go/cmd/seatcheck/main.go`: `defaultPinsDir` `.openrig/agenthub-seats`, `ReadFile(seatDir/policy.json)`, `commpolicy.ParsePolicy`, the policy-Seat-IS-the-caller check, `commpolicy.Decide`, denial **exit 3**, and `audit.jsonl` beside it carrying **`PolicyHash`** at `:150`). **EXERCISED OFFLINE** (`unshare -rn`, with a failing `curl` control): an alpha→beta escalation DENIED exit 3 and audited, an unknown recipient denied, and **A PERMITTED INTENT ALLOWED (audit `Allowed:true`, `Reason:"allowed"`)**. **IDENTITY CAVEAT, verified in both directions:** identity comes from `rig whoami`, which needs the **LOCAL daemon on loopback** — with the real rig and no network it **exits 2 and FAILS CLOSED**, sending nothing. **THE ONE REMAINING INCH, OWNER-GATED:** a seat **BUNDLED AND RUNNING** (`rig up <bundle>`), so identity answers natively while its outward path is cut. **(ii) SEATS CANNOT REACH `agenthub_http` WHILE OFFLINE — HOLDS, and the failure is LOUD.** A stored seat fragment declares `agenthub_http` as `{type http, url https://api.4genthub.com/mcp, Authorization Bearer ${AGENTHUB_TOKEN}}`; after `SIGKILL`ing a mock server, **calls #2 and #3 FAILED with ECONNREFUSED, `listTools` FAILED, there is no cached tool list and no successful retry**, and a closed port fails at the **first** call (44 ms). **NO `mcp__` GRANT exists in any rendered settings fragment** — it allows only `Bash(seatcheck send:*)` and `Bash(rig whoami:*)`. **LIMITS:** the `claude-code` MCP client itself could not be driven (its native binary is missing), so the demonstration used the official `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` client — same protocol family, **not the same implementation** — and any proxy in front of `api.4genthub.com` is **untested**. **AND THE REUSABLE LINE (go-dev2's): THREE DENIALS CANNOT DISTINGUISH A POLICY THAT IS READ FROM ONE THAT DENIES EVERYTHING** — the audited `Allowed:true` for a permitted intent is **the only case the wrong mechanism cannot produce** — the same shape as the flake and zero-count rules: **a green a different mechanism could have generated, closed by adding the case only the right mechanism passes.** Scratch for a later re-run: `/tmp/g4chk`, `/tmp/g4verify`. **AND THE TWO INSTRUMENTS THAT FAILED WHILE RECORDING THIS, kept IN the file because they are the SAME FAILURE this section describes, committed by the seat recording it:** (1) a grep for a policy hash **inside `scripts/openrig_seat_sync.py`** found nothing, because **the binding lives in the GENERATED `bundle.yaml` manifest at `:25`, not in the script** — the claim was true and the instrument searched the wrong artefact; (2) a grep for `PolicyHash` **under `agenthub_go/seatcheck/`** found nothing, because **the source is `agenthub_go/cmd/seatcheck/main.go:150`** — the directory is named for the TOOL, the field is named in the COMMAND's own source. **In both cases THE CLAIM WAS TRUE AND THE INSTRUMENT COULD NOT SEE IT — the blind-dimension case with a grep as the instrument** — so the failed lookups stay beside the citations, and a reader now knows **where the binding and the field ACTUALLY live** rather than only where they do not.

Its sibling `claude-4genthub` does the same for `${AGENTHUB_TOKEN}`. Name these files, never their values. The `deepseek` MCP server's bridge (`.mcp.json`) is separate and gives delegated jobs no MCP tools (Request 4).
- **HOST-LEVEL: AN UNCAPPED `vitest` RUN IN A SWARM WORKTREE OOM-CRASHED THE WHOLE WSL BOX TWICE (found and fixed 2026-10-06, from the owner's host-level Claude Code session — not a rig seat, recorded here because it is exactly what a seat running tests next needs to know).** This machine's WSL VM is capped at 16GB (`~/.wslconfig`, `memory=16GB`, host has 32GB) and, at idle with all ten seats merely running, was already sitting at ~8GB used plus ~6/8GB swap — the ten `omp` seat processes plus one `tsserver` (configured with `--max-old-space-size=8192`, i.e. an 8GB ceiling for one LSP) are the baseline load. `agenthub-frontend/vite.config.ts` was capped (`maxWorkers: 2`, `poolOptions.forks.execArgv: ['--max-old-space-size=2048']`) after an earlier incident on 2026-10-05, **but the mirror copy at `.swarm/agenthub-frontend/vite.config.ts` — the worktree a seat runs tests from — had NO cap**, so Vitest there defaulted to ~one jsdom worker per CPU (12 configured for the VM). Kernel log (`dmesg`) shows the OOM killer firing repeatedly overnight into 2026-10-06 06:56, killing `node (vitest 5/6/9/11)` workers at 1.5–5GB RSS each; the parent process then aborted (SIGABRT) and WSL wrote a 2.6–4.7GB crash dump per abort to the **Windows host's** `%TEMP%\wsl-crashes` (ten dumps, ~32GB, in under 24h) — that filled the Windows C: drive to ~3GB free, which is what surfaced this (twice: 2026-09-27 and again 2026-10-06). **Fix applied:** `.swarm/agenthub-frontend/vite.config.ts` now carries the same `maxWorkers: 2` / `minWorkers: 1` / `poolOptions.forks.execArgv: ['--max-old-space-size=2048']` cap as the main checkout. **Still open, not fixed here:** (1) check any *other* worktree before running its tests — a new `.swarm/<x>` or similar copy of a vite/vitest config starts uncapped by default and this will recur; (2) the `tsserver` 8GB heap and the ten always-on seat baseline were not touched, so the safety margin on this box is thin even with the cap; (3) separately, `~/.openrig/state/omp/<seat>/*` (per-session agent state, no retention policy in openrig) grew 13GB in 3 days and is a second, unrelated mechanism that fills the same disk — a host-level cleanup script (`~/bin/disk-cleanup.sh` + Windows Scheduled Task `WSL Disk Cleanup`, every 6h) now prunes it, but openrig itself still has no built-in pruning.

(`CLAUDE.md` is no longer a keep-out: it was renamed to the tracked `AGENTS.md` — see the owner-directives note above.)
